## II A Morning’s Sport / 二 晨猎

===Original===
“Moving some five or six miles round the base of the great peak of which I have spoken, we came the same day to one of the fairest bits of African country that I have seen outside of Kukuanaland. At this spot the mountain spur that runs out at right angles to the great range, which stretches its cloud-clad length north and south as far as the eye can reach, sweeps inwards with a vast and splendid curve. This curve measures some five-and-thirty miles from point to point, and across its moon-like segment the river flashed, a silver line of light. On the further side of the river is a measureless sea of swelling ground, a natural park covered with great patches of bush—some of them being many square miles in extent. These are separated one from another by glades of grass land, broken here and there with clumps of timber trees; and in some instances by curious isolated koppies, and even by single crags of granite that start up into the air as though they were monuments carved by man, and not tombstones set by nature over the grave of ages gone. On the west this beautiful plain is bordered by the lonely mountain, from the edge of which it rolls down toward the fever coast; but how far it runs to the north I cannot say—eight days’ journey, according to the natives, when it is lost in an untravelled morass.

“On the hither side of the river the scenery is different. Along the edge of its banks, where the land is flat, are green patches of swamp. Then comes a wide belt of beautiful grass land covered thickly with game, and sloping up very gently to the borders of the forest, which, beginning at about a thousand feet above the level of the plain, clothes the mountainside almost to its crest. In this forest grow great trees, most of them of the yellowwood species. Some of these trees are so lofty, that a bird in their top branches would be out of range of an ordinary shot gun. Another peculiar thing about them is, that they are for the most part covered with a dense growth of the Orchilla moss; and from this moss the natives manufacture a most excellent deep purple dye, with which they stain tanned hides and also cloth, when they happen to get any of the latter. I do not think that I ever saw anything more remarkable than the appearance of one of these mighty trees festooned from top to bottom with trailing wreaths of this sad-hued moss, in which the wind whispers gently as it stirs them. At a distance it looks like the gray locks of a Titan crowned with bright green leaves, and here and there starred with the rich bloom of orchids.

“The night of that day on which I had my little difference of opinion with Gobo, we camped by the edge of this great forest, and on the following morning at daylight I started out shooting. As we were short of meat I determined to kill a buffalo, of which there were plenty about, before looking for traces of elephants. Not more than half a mile from camp we came across a trail broad as a cart-road, evidently made by a great herd of buffaloes which had passed up at dawn from their feeding ground in the marshes, to spend the day in the cool air of the uplands. This trail I followed boldly; for such wind as there was blew straight down the mountainside, that is, from the direction in which the buffaloes had gone, to me. About a mile further on the forest began to be dense, and the nature of the trail showed me that I must be close to my game. Another two hundred yards and the bush was so thick that, had it not been for the trail, we could scarcely have passed through it. As it was, Gobo, who carried my eight-bore rifle (for I had the .570-express in my hand), and the other two men whom I had taken with me, showed the very strongest dislike to going any further, pointing out that there was ‘no room to run away.’ I told them that they need not come unless they liked, but that I was certainly going on; and then, growing ashamed, they came.

===Chinese===
“绕过我前面说过的那座大山峰的山脚走了五六英里，当天我们就来到一片非洲乡野——在我看过的风光里，除库库阿纳兰（Kukuanaland）之外，数这一处最秀美。在这里，与大山脉成直角斜伸出的一条山嘴，朝那条云遮雾绕、南北绵延直到天边的大山脉，以一道壮丽非凡的巨大弧线向内弯拢。这道弧线两端相距约三十五英里，一条河横过它那新月般的地段，闪着一道银线似的亮光。河的对岸是一片无边无际、起伏如浪的高地，一座天然园林，覆着一团团大丛的灌木——有的绵延好几平方英里。灌木丛与灌木丛之间隔着一块块草坡，草坡上间或点缀着一簇簇成材的大树；有的地方隔着的则是一座座奇特的、孤零零的石丘（koppie），甚至是一方方拔地而起的独块花岗岩巨崖，仿佛人工雕凿的纪念碑，而非造化竖在逝去岁月坟头上的墓碑。这片美丽的平原西边以那座孤山为界，从山边一路向瘴疠横行的海岸滚落下去；至于它往北延伸多远，我说不上来——照当地人的说法是八天路程，尽头没入一片人迹不至的沼泽。

“河这一边的景色又自不同。沿岸地势平坦处，是一块块绿色的沼泽。再往里，是一条宽阔美丽的草地带，猎物密密匝匝地栖满其上，地势缓缓趋起，直连到森林边上。森林从高出平原约一千英尺的地方开始，把山坡几乎一直盖到山脊。林中长着大树，多半是黄木（yellowwood）一类。有的树高到这般地步：鸟落在顶枝上，普通猎枪都够不着。这些树还有一桩特别之处：它们大多披满厚厚一层染衣苔（Orchilla moss）；土人用这种苔造出极好的深紫色染料，拿来染熟皮子，偶尔弄到布匹时也染布。我想我从未见过比这更奇的景象：这样一株巨树，从顶到脚挂满一垂垂暗色苔藓织成的花彩，风一动，便在苔间低语。远远望去，恰似一位巨人的灰白长发，冠着鲜绿的树叶，星星点点还缀着兰花的浓艳花朵。

“我与戈博（Gobo）小小争执了一场的那个晚上，我们就在这片大森林边上扎营；第二天一早天一亮，我便出门打猎去了。因为肉食短缺，我决定先打一头水牛——这一带水牛多的是——然后再去找大象的踪迹。离营地不过半英里，我们就撞上一条车道般宽的小径，显然是一大群水牛拂晓时分从沼泽里的觅食地经过留下的，它们要上高地凉快的空气里消磨白昼。我大着胆子顺着小径走；因为仅有的一点风径直顺山坡吹下来，也就是说，从水牛去的方向朝我吹来。往前约一英里，林子渐渐密了，小径的样子告诉我，猎物必在近处。再走两百码，灌木密得要不是有这条小径，我们几乎钻不过去。即便如此，替我背八号口径步枪的戈博（我手里提着那支 .570 特快步枪），连同我带在身边的另外两人，都表现出极强烈的不愿再往前走的意思，指出此地‘没有逃跑的余地’。我告诉他们，不愿来尽可以不来，我可是一定要往前走的；于是他们臊了起来，跟上了。

===Original===
“Another fifty yards, and the trail opened into a little glade. I knelt down and peeped and peered, but no buffalo could I see. Evidently the herd had broken up here—I knew that from the spoor—and penetrated the opposite bush in little troops. I crossed the glade, and choosing one line of spoor, followed it for some sixty yards, when it became clear to me that I was surrounded by buffaloes; and yet so dense was the cover that I could not see any. A few yards to my left I could hear one rubbing its horns against a tree, while from my right came an occasional low and throaty grunt which told me that I was uncomfortably near an old bull. I crept on towards him with my heart in my mouth, as gently as though I were walking upon eggs for a bet, lifting every little bit of wood in my path, and placing it behind me lest it should crack and warn the game. After me in single file came my three retainers, and I don’t know which of them looked the most frightened. Presently Gobo touched my leg; I glanced round, and saw him pointing slantwise towards the left. I lifted my head a little and peeped over a mass of creepers; beyond the creepers was a dense bush of sharp-pointed aloes, of that kind of which the leaves project laterally, and on the other side of the aloes, not fifteen paces from us, I made out the horns, neck, and the ridge of the back of a tremendous old bull. I took my eight-bore, and getting on to my knee prepared to shoot him through the neck, taking my chance of cutting his spine. I had already covered him as well as the aloe leaves would allow, when he gave a kind of sigh and lay down.

“I looked round in dismay. What was to be done now? I could not see to shoot him lying down, even if my bullet would have pierced the intervening aloes—which was doubtful—and if I stood up he would either run away or charge me. I reflected, and came to the conclusion that the only thing to do was to lie down also; for I did not fancy wandering after other buffaloes in that dense bush. If a buffalo lies down, it is clear that he must get up again some time, so it was only a case of patience—‘fighting the fight of sit down,’ as the Zulus say.

===Chinese===
“再走五十码，小径豁然通进一小片林间草地。我跪下来望了又望、瞧了又瞧，却一头水牛也看不见。牛群显然在这里散了队——这是我从蹄印上看出来的——一小股一小股钻进了对面的灌木。我穿过草地，拣了一条蹄印跟了约莫六十码，便明白自己已被水牛团团围住；可遮蔽物密得连一头也看不见。左边几码外，我听得见一头水牛在树上磨角；右边则不时传来一声低沉的、从喉咙里滚出来的哼声，告诉我一头老公牛近在咫尺，近得让人浑身不自在。我朝它匍匐过去，心都提到了嗓子眼，轻得好比同人打赌踩着鸡蛋走路，把路上每一根小树枝都拈起来放到身后，生怕它一脆响惊了猎物。我的三名随从鱼贯跟在后面，我说不清哪一个的脸上最惊惶。忽然戈博碰了碰我的腿；我回头一瞥，见他斜斜指向左方。我把头略略抬高些，从一蓬蔓草上望过去；蔓草外是一丛密密的尖叶芦荟，就是叶子横着四面伸出的那一种，芦荟那边，离我们不足十五步，我认出了一头硕大无朋的老公牛的角、脖颈和脊背。我接过八号枪，单膝跪定，准备打它的脖子，碰碰运气看能否切断它的脊髓。趁芦荟叶还容得我瞄准、我刚刚套住它的当口，它却像叹了口气似的，卧了下去。

“我沮丧地四下张望。这可怎么办？它躺着，我看不见也打不着，即便我的子弹能穿透隔着的那丛芦荟——这还很悬；我若站起来，它不是逃走便是冲过来。我思量一番，得出结论：唯一的办法是也躺下；我可不想在那片密灌木里再去找别的水牛。水牛既卧下了，明摆着总有一天要站起来，所以这不过是比耐心——祖鲁人所谓‘坐地之战’。

===Original===
“Accordingly I sat down and lighted a pipe, thinking that the smell of it might reach the buffalo and make him get up. But the wind was the wrong way, and it did not; so when it was done I lit another. Afterwards I had cause to regret that pipe.

“Well, we squatted like this for between half and three quarters of an hour, till at length I began to grow heartily sick of the performance. It was about as dull a business as the last hour of a comic opera. I could hear buffaloes snorting and moving all round, and see the red-beaked tic birds flying up off their backs, making a kind of *hiss* as they did so, something like that of the English missel-thrush, but I could not see a single buffalo. As for my old bull, I think he must have slept the sleep of the just, for he never even stirred.

“Just as I was making up my mind that something must be done to save the situation, my attention was attracted by a curious grinding noise. At first I thought that it must be a buffalo chewing the cud, but was obliged to abandon the idea because the noise was too loud. I shifted myself round and stared through the cracks in the bush, in the direction whence the sound seemed to come, and once I thought that I saw something gray moving about fifty yards off, but could not make certain. Although the grinding noise still continued I could see nothing more, so I gave up thinking about it, and once again turned my attention to the buffalo. Presently, however, something happened. Suddenly from about forty yards away there came a tremendous snorting sound, more like that made by an engine getting a heavy train underway than anything else in the world.

===Chinese===
“于是我便坐下点了斗烟，心想烟味也许会飘到水牛那儿，叫它站起来。可是风向不对，没起作用；一斗抽完，我又点了一斗。后来我有的是理由懊悔这斗烟。

“就这样，我们蹲了半个钟头到三刻钟，末了我开始由衷地腻味起这场把戏来。这份乏味，抵得上喜歌剧的最后一个钟头。我听得见四周水牛又是喷鼻又是走动，看得见红嘴的啄蜱鸟从牛背上扑棱棱飞起，翅膀带出一种*嘶嘶*声，有点像英国槲鸫的动静，可我连一头水牛也瞧不见。至于我那头老公牛，我想它必是睡了个问心无愧的觉，因为它动也没动一下。

“正当我打定主意，非得想个法子扭转局面的时候，一阵古怪的、磨牙似的声响引起了我的注意。起初我以为准是哪头水牛在倒嚼，可那声音太响，只好放弃这个想法。我挪转身子，顺着声音来处，从灌木的缝隙里张望，有一回仿佛看见一团灰色的东西在五十码外动，却看不真切。磨牙声虽然还在响，我再看不到别的，也就不再想它，重又把心思放回水牛身上。然而不多时，出事了。忽然，约莫四十码外传来一声惊天动地的喷鼻，世上再没有什么声音比它更像一列火车头拖起沉重的列车了。

===Original===
“‘By Jove,’ I thought, turning round in the direction from which the grinding sound had come, ‘that must be a rhinoceros, and he has got our wind.’ For, as you fellows know, there is no mistaking the sound made by a rhinoceros when he gets wind of you.

“Another second, and I heard a most tremendous crashing noise. Before I could think what to do, before I could even get up, the bush behind me seemed to burst asunder, and there appeared not eight yards from us, the great horn and wicked twinkling eye of a charging rhinoceros. He had winded us or my pipe, I do not know which, and, after the fashion of these brutes, had charged up the scent. I could not rise, I could not even get the gun up, I had no time. All that I was able to do was to roll over as far out of the monster’s path as the bush would allow. Another second and he was over me, his great bulk towering above me like a mountain, and, upon my word, I could not get his smell out of my nostrils for a week. Circumstances impressed it on my memory, at least I suppose so. His hot breath blew upon my face, one of his front feet just missed my head, and his hind one actually trod upon the loose part of my trousers and pinched a little bit of my skin. I saw him pass over me lying as I was upon my back, and next second I saw something else. My men were a little behind me, and therefore straight in the path of the rhinoceros. One of them flung himself backwards into the bush, and thus avoided him. The second with a wild yell sprung to his feet, and bounded like an india-rubber ball right into the aloe bush, landing well among the spikes. But the third, it was my friend Gobo, could not by any means get away. He managed to gain his feet, and that was all. The rhinoceros was charging with his head low; his horn passed between Gobo’s legs, and feeling something on his nose, he jerked it up. Away went Gobo, high into the air. He turned a complete somersault at the apex of the curve, and as he did so, I caught sight of his face. It was gray with terror, and his mouth was wide open. Down he came, right on to the great brute’s back, and that broke his fall. Luckily for him the rhinoceros never turned, but crashed straight through the aloe bush, only missing the man who had jumped into it by about a yard.

===Chinese===
“‘天哪，’我一边想，一边朝磨牙声来处转过身去，‘那准是头犀牛，它闻到我们的气味了。’因为，你们这班家伙是知道的，犀牛闻到你气味时的那动静，是错不了的。

“紧接着又是一瞬，我听见一声天崩地裂的巨响。不等我想出该怎么办，不等我甚至站起来，身后的灌木仿佛轰然裂开，离我们不足八码处，现出一头冲将过来的犀牛的大角和那只凶光闪烁的眼睛。它闻见的是我们，还是我的烟斗，我说不好；总之，它照这类畜生的做派，顺着气味一路冲了上来。我起不了身，连枪都举不起来，来不及了。我唯一来得及做的，就是就地打滚，滚到灌木容许我滚到的、离那庞然大物冲路最远的地方。再一瞬，它已经从我身上越过，庞大的躯体像一座山似的在我头顶高耸。说句实话，它的气味在我鼻子里一个礼拜都散不掉——是当时的场面把这气味印进了我记忆，至少我这么猜想。它滚热的呼吸喷在我脸上，一只前脚险险擦过我的脑袋，一只后脚则当真踩着我裤子的松处，夹到了我一点皮肉。我仰面躺着，眼看它从我上方过去；下一瞬，我看见的是另一幕。我的人在我身后一点，不偏不倚正处在犀牛的冲路上。一个向后猛地扑进灌木，躲过了它。第二个狂喊一声蹦起来，像皮球一样径直弹进芦荟丛，稳稳落在尖刺中间。可第三个——正是我的朋友戈博——怎么也脱不了身。他好歹站了起来，也仅止于站了起来。犀牛是低着头冲的；那角从戈博两腿之间穿过，鼻子上觉出有个东西，猛往上一挑。戈博就飞了出去，高高地上了天。他在抛物线的顶点结结实实翻了个筋斗，就在那时，我瞥见了他的脸：吓得灰白，嘴张得老大。他落下来，正落在大畜生的背上，那一下卸了他的力。总算他运气好，犀牛并不回头，径直撞穿芦荟丛而去，离跳进丛中的那人不过一码之差。

===Original===
“Then followed a complication. The sleeping buffalo on the further side of the bush, hearing the noise, sprang to his feet, and for a second, not knowing what to do, stood still. At that instant the huge rhinoceros blundered right on to him, and getting his horn beneath his stomach gave him such a fearful dig that the buffalo was turned over on to his back, while his assailant went a most amazing cropper over his carcase. In another moment, however, the rhinoceros was up, and wheeling round to the left, crashed through the bush downhill and towards the open country.

“Instantly the whole place became alive with alarming sounds. In every direction troops of snorting buffaloes charged through the forest, wild with fright, while the injured bull on the further side of the bush began to bellow like a mad thing. I lay quite still for a moment, devoutly praying that none of the flying buffaloes would come my way. Then when the danger lessened I got on to my feet, shook myself, and looked round. One of my boys, he who had thrown himself backward into the bush, was already halfway up a tree—if heaven had been at the top of it he could not have climbed quicker. Gobo was lying close to me, groaning vigorously, but, as I suspected, quite unhurt; while from the aloe bush into which No. 3 had bounded like a tennis ball, issued a succession of the most piercing yells.

“I looked, and saw that this unfortunate fellow was in a very tight place. A great spike of aloe had run through the back of his skin waist-belt, though without piercing his flesh, in such a fashion that it was impossible for him to move, while within six feet of him the injured buffalo bull, thinking, no doubt, that he was the aggressor, bellowed and ramped to get at him, tearing the thick aloes with his great horns. That no time was to be lost, if I wished to save the man’s life, was very clear. So seizing my eight-bore, which was fortunately uninjured, I took a pace to the left, for the rhinoceros had enlarged the hole in the bush, and aimed at the point of the buffalo’s shoulder, since on account of my position I could not get a fair side shot for the heart. As I did so I saw that the rhinoceros had given the bull a tremendous wound in the stomach, and that the shock of the encounter had put his left hind-leg out of joint at the hip. I fired, and the bullet striking the shoulder broke it, and knocked the buffalo down. I knew that he could not get up any more, because he was now injured fore and aft, so notwithstanding his terrific bellows I scrambled round to where he was. There he lay glaring furiously and tearing up the soil with his horns. Stepping up to within two yards of him I aimed at the vertebra of his neck and fired. The bullet struck true, and with a *thud* he dropped his head upon the ground, groaned, and died.

===Chinese===
“接着又生出一重乱子。灌木那头睡着的水牛听见响动，一跃而起，有一秒钟不知如何是好，呆立不动。就在这一瞬，那庞然的犀牛一头撞在它身上，角插进它腹下，狠狠一挑，水牛被掀了个肚皮朝天，而那位袭击者则从它的躯体上结结实实栽了个大跟头，栽得无比精彩。但转眼间犀牛已爬起来，向左一转，撞穿灌木，顺坡直奔旷野去了。

“霎时间，整个地方被种种骇人的声响闹腾活了。四面八方都是成群的水牛喷着鼻子在林子里狂奔，吓疯了；灌木那头受了伤的公牛则开始像疯了一样吼叫。我一动不动地躺了片刻，虔诚祷告逃窜的水牛别朝我这边来。等险情稍减，我站起身，抖了抖，四下张望。我的一个小伙子——就是向后扑进灌木的那位——已经爬到半树高了；哪怕树顶上是天堂，他也不能爬得更快。戈博躺在我近旁，起劲地哼哼着，但正如我疑心的那样，毫发无损；而三号伙计像网球似的弹进去的那丛芦荟里，则接连发出一声声最凄厉的嚎叫。

“我一看，只见这位倒霉的家伙处境窘迫已极。一根粗大的芦荟尖刺穿透了他皮腰带的背面——幸而没有刺着肉——把他钉得动弹不得；而离他不足六英尺，那头受伤的公牛——无疑认定了他就是行凶之人——一边吼一边人立起来要够他，大角把厚厚的芦荟扯得稀烂。要救这人的命，一刻也耽搁不得，这是明摆着的。于是我抓起万幸无损的八号枪，向左跨了一步——犀牛已把灌木上的窟窿撞大了——瞄准公牛的肩点，因为以我的位置，打不着正侧的心口。就在这时我看见，犀牛在公牛肚子上开了一道可怕的大口子，相撞的冲力又使它左后腿的髋关节脱了臼。我开枪，子弹打中肩胛，把它击碎，公牛应声栽倒。我知道它再也起不来了，因为它如今前伤后伤俱全，于是不顾它吼声骇人，我手脚并用地绕到它跟前。它躺在那儿，怒目圆睁，用角把泥土刨得翻起。我走到离它两码处，瞄准颈骨开了一枪。子弹正着，*噗*的一声，它的头垂到地上，哼了一声，死了。

===Original===
“This little matter having been attended to with the assistance of Gobo, who had now found his feet, I went on to extricate our unfortunate companion from the aloe bush. This we found a thorny task, but at last he was dragged forth uninjured, though in a very pious and prayerful frame of mind. His ‘spirit had certainly looked that way,’ he said, or he would now have been dead. As I never like to interfere with true piety, I did not venture to suggest that his spirit had deigned to make use of my eight-bore in his interest.

“Having despatched this boy back to the camp to tell the bearers to come and cut the buffalo up, I bethought me that I owed that rhinoceros a grudge which I should love to repay. So without saying a word of what was in my mind to Gobo, who was now more than ever convinced that Fate walked about loose in Wambe’s country, I just followed on the brute’s spoor. He had crashed through the bush till he reached the little glade. Then moderating his pace somewhat, he had followed the glade down its entire length, and once more turned to the right through the forest, shaping his course for the open land that lies between the edge of the bush and the river. Having followed him for a mile or so further, I found myself quite on the open. I took out my glasses and searched the plain. About a mile ahead was something brown—as I thought, the rhinoceros. I advanced another quarter of a mile, and looked once more—it was not the rhinoceros, but a big ant-heap. This was puzzling, but I did not like to give it up, because I knew from his spoor that he must be somewhere ahead. But as the wind was blowing straight from me towards the line that he had followed, and as a rhinoceros can smell you for about a mile, it would not, I felt, be safe to follow his trail any further; so I made a detour of a mile and more, till I was nearly opposite the ant-heap, and then once more searched the plain. It was no good, I could see nothing of him, and was about to give it up and start after some oryx I saw on the skyline, when suddenly at a distance of about three hundred yards from the ant-heap, and on its further side, I saw my rhino stand up in a patch of grass.

===Chinese===
“这桩小事在戈博——他如今也找回了自己的脚——的协助下料理完毕，我便去把那位倒霉的同伴从芦荟丛里解救出来。这是件多刺的差事，但他终于被拖了出来，毫发无伤，只是满心虔诚、一嘴祷告。他说，他的‘守护灵方才定然朝这边看了一眼’，否则他此刻已经没命了。我一向不愿干涉真正的虔诚，也就没敢多嘴说：他的守护灵竟肯屈尊借用我的八号枪来保佑他。

“打发这小伙子回营，叫挑夫们来宰牛之后，我想到自己欠那头犀牛一笔账，很乐意去还。于是我一句心里话也没对戈博讲——他如今比先前任何时候都更坚信，命运女神正在万贝的国度里四处游荡——只顺着那畜生的蹄印追了下去。它一路撞穿灌木，直到那片小草地。随后略略放缓脚步，顺着草地走到底，又向右转穿过森林，直奔灌木边缘与河流之间的开阔地。再跟了一英里上下，我发现自己已完全身处开阔地。我取出望远镜搜索平原。前方约一英里处有个棕色的东西——我以为是那头犀牛。又推进四分之一英里再看——不是犀牛，是个大蚁冢。这就叫人犯疑了，可我不肯罢手，因为从蹄印我知道它必在前方某处。但风正从我这边朝它走的路线吹去，而犀牛隔着一英里就能闻见人，再往前跟，我想是危险的；于是我兜了个一英里多的圈子，绕到几乎与蚁冢正对的地方，再搜索平原。没用，什么也看不见；我正要放弃，改去追天际线上望见的几只大羚羊，忽然，在离蚁冢约三百码、它的那一头，我看见我的犀牛在一丛草里站了起来。

===Original===
“‘Heavens!’ I thought to myself, ‘he’s off again;’ but no, after standing staring for a minute or two he once more lay down.

“Now I found myself in a quandary. As you know, a rhinoceros is a very shortsighted brute, indeed his sight is as bad as his scent is good. Of this fact he is perfectly aware, but he always makes the most of his natural gifts. For instance, when he lies down he invariably does so with his head down wind. Thus, if any enemy crosses his wind he will still be able to escape, or attack him; and if, on the other hand, the danger approaches up wind he will at least have a chance of seeing it. Otherwise, by walking delicately, one might actually kick him up like a partridge, if only the advance was made up wind.

===Chinese===
“‘天哪！’我暗想，‘它又要跑了；’可是不，它站着瞪了一两分钟，重又卧下了。

“这下我可进退两难了。你们知道，犀牛是个高度近视的畜生，它的眼力之坏，正如同它的嗅觉之好。这一点它自己也一清二楚，但它总把天生的本钱用到十足。譬如说，它卧下时，必定把头朝着下风。这样，若有敌人横穿它的风路，它仍来得及逃走或迎击；反过来，若危险从上风来，它至少还有一眼看见的机会。要不然，只要从上风轻手轻脚地走过去，人当真能像惊起一只鹧鸪似的把它踢起来。

===Original===
“Well, the point was, how on earth should I get within shot of this rhinoceros? After much deliberation I determined to try a side approach, thinking that in this way I might get a shoulder shot. Accordingly we started in a crouching attitude, I first, Gobo holding on to my coat tails, and the other boy on to Gobo’s *moocha*. I always adopt this plan when stalking big game, for if you follow any other system the bearers will get out of line. We arrived within three hundred yards safely enough, and then the real difficulties began. The grass had been so closely eaten off by game that there was scarcely any cover. Consequently it was necessary to go on to our hands and knees, which in my case involved laying down the eight-bore at every step and then lifting it up again. However, I wriggled along somehow, and if it had not been for Gobo and his friend no doubt everything would have gone well. But as you have, I dare say, observed, a native out stalking is always of that mind which is supposed to actuate an ostrich—so long as his head is hidden he seems to think that nothing else can be seen. So it was in this instance, Gobo and the other boy crept along on their hands and toes with their heads well down, but, though unfortunately I did not notice it till too late, bearing the fundamental portions of their frames high in the air. Now all animals are quite as suspicious of this end of mankind as they are of his face, and of that fact I soon had a proof. Just when we had got within about two hundred yards, and I was congratulating myself that I had not had this long crawl with the sun beating on the back of my neck like a furnace for nothing, I heard the hissing note of the rhinoceros birds, and up flew four or five of them from the brute’s back, where they had been comfortably employed in catching tics. Now this performance on the part of the birds is to a rhinoceros what the word ‘cave’ is to a schoolboy—it puts him on the qui vive at once. Before the birds were well in the air I saw the grass stir.

“‘Down you go,’ I whispered to the boys, and as I did so the rhinoceros got up and glared suspiciously around. But he could see nothing, indeed if we had been standing up I doubt if he would have seen us at that distance; so he merely gave two or three sniffs and then lay down, his head still down wind, the birds once more settling on his back.

===Chinese===
“好，问题在于：我究竟怎样才能进入打得到这头犀牛的距离？再三盘算之后，我决定试试从侧面接近，想着这样或许能得一个打肩膀的角度。于是我们弓着身子出发，我在前，戈博揪着我的衣摆，另一个小伙子揪着戈博的*moocha*（缠腰布）。潜近大猎物时我一向用这套办法，因为用别的法子，挑夫们准会走出队列。我们顺顺当当地进到三百码内，真正的难处才开始。草被猎物啃得太短，几乎无处藏身。于是只好手脚并用爬着走，我这儿就更麻烦：每挪一步都得放下八号枪，再提起来。好歹我一路蹭了过去，若不是戈博和他的朋友，无疑一切都会顺顺当当。可是你们谅必也留意过，土人出来潜猎时，心眼儿永远跟鸵鸟一般——只要脑袋藏住了，他似乎便觉得其余部分都看不见。眼下正是如此：戈博和那小伙子手脚并用地爬，头压得很低，不幸的是我发觉时已经迟了——他们身体的根本部位高高撅在空中。而一切动物对人这一端的疑心，丝毫不亚于对他的脸，这一点我马上就有了实证。刚进到约两百码，我正暗自庆幸，这一路长爬没有白爬——太阳像火炉一样烤着我的后脖颈——忽听得犀牛鸟一声嘶鸣，四五只从那畜生背上飞起，它们方才正在那儿舒舒服服地捉虱虫。鸟儿这一手对犀牛的意义，就如同‘留神’这个词对学童——立刻叫它警觉起来。鸟还没飞稳，我已看见草在动了。

“‘快趴下，’我低声对两个小伙子说；就在这时，犀牛站起来，狐疑地四下瞪视。可它什么也看不见——说真的，哪怕我们站着，隔这么远我也怀疑它看得见我们；于是它只喷了两三下鼻子，重又卧下，头仍朝着下风，鸟儿们也重新落回它背上。

===Original===
“But it was clear to me that he was sleeping with one eye open, being generally in a suspicious and unchristian frame of mind, and that it was useless to proceed further on this stalk, so we quietly withdrew to consider the position and study the ground. The results were not satisfactory. There was absolutely no cover about except the ant-heap, which was some three hundred yards from the rhinoceros upon his upwind side. I knew that if I tried to stalk him in front I should fail, and so I should if I attempted to do so from the further side—he or the birds would see me; so I came to a conclusion: I would go to the ant-heap, which would give him my wind, and instead of stalking him I would let him stalk me. It was a bold step, and one which I should never advise a hunter to take, but somehow I felt as though rhino and I must play the hand out.

“I explained my intentions to the men, who both held up their arms in horror. Their fears for my safety were a little mitigated, however, when I told them that I did not expect them to come with me.

“Gobo breathed a prayer that I might not meet Fate walking about, and the other one sincerely trusted that my spirit might look my way when the rhinoceros charged, and then they both departed to a place of safety.

===Chinese===
“但我看得分明，它是睁着一只眼睡觉的——它素来一副多疑的、毫无基督徒心肠的心性——这潜猎再往前走也是白搭，于是我们悄悄退下来，商量形势，察看地形。结果不能令人满意。四下里全无遮蔽，只有那个蚁冢，在犀牛上风一侧约三百码处。我知道从正面潜近必败，从那一侧下手也是一样——不叫它看见就叫鸟看见；于是我拿定主意：到蚁冢去，把自己的风喂给它，与其我潜猎它，不如让它来潜猎我。这是大胆的一步，我绝不建议哪个猎人这么干，可不知怎么，我总觉得这头犀牛和我，非得把这手牌打完不可。

“我把打算对两人一说，他们都惊恐地举起双手。不过，待我说明并不指望他们同去之后，他们对我安危的忧虑才算减轻了些。

“戈博默祷了一回，求我别撞上游荡中的命运女神；另一位则诚心指望犀牛冲来时，我的守护灵能朝我这边看一眼。祷告已毕，两人便一同躲到安全的地方去了。

===Original===
“Taking my eight-bore, and half-a-dozen spare cartridges in my pocket, I made a detour, and reaching the ant-heap in safety lay down. For a moment the wind had dropped, but presently a gentle puff of air passed over me, and blew on towards the rhinoceros. By the way, I wonder what it is that smells so strong about a man? Is it his body or his breath? I have never been able to make out, but I saw it stated the other day, that in the duck decoys the man who is working the ducks holds a little piece of burning turf before his mouth, and that if he does this they cannot smell him, which looks as though it were the breath. Well, whatever it was about me that attracted his attention, the rhinoceros soon smelt me, for within half a minute after the puff of wind had passed me he was on his legs, and turning round to get his head up wind. There he stood for a few seconds and sniffed, and then he began to move, first of all at a trot, then, as the scent grew stronger, at a furious gallop. On he came, snorting like a runaway engine, with his tail stuck straight up in the air; if he had seen me lie down there he could not have made a better line. It was rather nervous work, I can tell you, lying there waiting for his onslaught, for he looked like a mountain of flesh. I determined, however, not to fire till I could plainly see his eye, for I think that rule always gives one the right distance for big game; so I rested my rifle on the ant-heap and waited for him, kneeling. At last, when he was about forty yards away, I saw that the time had come, and aiming straight for the middle of the chest I pulled.

“*Thud* went the heavy bullet, and with a tremendous snort over rolled the rhinoceros beneath its shock, just like a shot rabbit. But if I had thought that he was done for I was mistaken, for in another second he was up again, and coming at me as hard as ever, only with his head held low. I waited till he was within ten yards, in the hope that he would expose his chest, but he would do nothing of the sort; so I just had to fire at his head with the left barrel, and take my chance. Well, as luck would have it, of course the animal put its horn in the way of the bullet, which cut clean through it about three inches above the root and then glanced off into space.

===Chinese===
“我带上八号枪，口袋里揣了六发备用弹，兜了个圈子，平安到达蚁冢，卧了下来。有一阵风息了，但不多时一股软风从我身上拂过，朝犀牛那边吹去。顺便说一句，我一直纳闷，人身上到底是什么东西气味这么大？是身体还是呼吸？我始终没弄明白，不过前些天我看到一种说法：在诱鸭的隐蔽棚里，放鸭的人嘴里衔着一小块燃着的泥炭，这样鸭子就闻不出他来——看起来，该是呼吸的缘故。罢了，无论是我身上的哪一点引它注意，犀牛很快闻到了我：那阵风过我身不过半分钟，它已经站起，转身把头对准上风。它立在那儿嗅了几秒，随即开动——先是小跑，气味愈浓，便改作狂怒的飞奔。它来了，像一列脱缰的火车头似的喷着鼻息，尾巴笔直竖在空中；哪怕它亲眼看见我卧在那里，路线也选不出更直的了。老实说，卧在那儿等它扑来，这差事相当熬人，因为它俨然一座肉山。但我拿定主意，不见到它清清楚楚的眼睛绝不开枪——我始终认为，这条规矩总能叫人拿捏出打大猎物的合适距离；于是我把枪架在蚁冢上，跪着等它。终于，它离约四十码时，我知道时候到了，直瞄胸口正中，扣了扳机。

“*噗*的一声，沉重的子弹命中，随着一声震天的喷鼻，犀牛被这一击掀得翻滚出去，活像只中枪的兔子。但我若以为它完了，那就错了：转眼间它又爬起来，照旧拼死朝我冲，只是头压得低低的。我等到它进至十码之内，指望它露出胸口，它偏偏不露；我只好用左枪管照头打，听天由命。唉，也真叫运气，这畜生偏偏把角挡在子弹路上，子弹把角齐齐削断——离角根约三寸——然后一偏，飞到空处去了。

===Original===
“After that things got rather serious. My gun was empty and the rhinoceros was rapidly arriving, so rapidly indeed that I came to the conclusion that I had better make way for him. Accordingly I jumped to my feet and ran to the right as hard as I could go. As I did so he arrived full tilt, knocked my friendly ant-heap flat, and for the third time that day went a most magnificent cropper. This gave me a few seconds’ start, and I ran down wind—my word, I did run! Unfortunately, however, my modest retreat was observed, and the rhinoceros, as soon as he had found his legs again, set to work to run after me. Now no man on earth can run so fast as an irritated rhinoceros can gallop, and I knew that he must soon catch me up. But having some slight experience of this sort of thing, luckily for myself, I kept my head, and as I fled I managed to open my rifle, get the old cartridges out, and put in two fresh ones. To do this I was obliged to steady my pace a little, and by the time that I had snapped the rifle to I heard the beast snorting and thundering away within a few paces of my back. I stopped, and as I did so rapidly cocked the rifle and slued round upon my heel. By this time the brute was within six or seven yards of me, but luckily his head was up. I lifted the rifle and fired at him. It was a snap shot, but the bullet struck him in the chest within three inches of the first, and found its way into his lungs. It did not stop him, however, so all I could do was to bound to one side, which I did with surprising activity, and as he brushed past me to fire the other barrel into his side. That did for him. The ball passed in behind the shoulder and right through his heart. He fell over on to his side, gave one more awful squeal—a dozen pigs could not have made such a noise—and promptly died, keeping his wicked eyes wide open all the time.

“As for me, I blew my nose, and going up to the rhinoceros sat on his head, and reflected that I had done a capital morning’s shooting.”

===Chinese===
“打那以后，局面就相当严重了。我的枪空了，犀牛正飞速赶到，快到我认为最好还是给它让路。于是我跳起来，撒腿朝右，拼死力地跑。就在此时它一头撞到，把我那座好心的蚁冢撞了个平，当天第三次结结实实栽了个漂亮跟头。这给了我几秒的先手，我顺着下风跑——老天作证，我跑得可真快！不幸的是，我这场谦逊的撤退被它瞧见了，犀牛一找回自己的腿，立刻着手追赶。世上没有人跑得过一头被激怒的犀牛的四蹄，我知道它很快就会追上。所幸我对这类事多少有点经验，头脑不曾慌，一边逃一边居然退开枪膛，退出旧弹壳，装进两发新弹。做这些不得不稍稍放慢脚步，等我把枪合上，已听得那畜生在我背后几步之内又是喷鼻又是打雷。我猛地站定，顺势迅速扳起枪机，脚跟一旋转过身来。这时那畜生离我只有六七码，幸而头是抬着的。我举枪就打。那是仓促的一枪，但子弹打中它的胸口，离头一枪不足三寸，钻进了它的肺。可这仍拦不住它，我唯一能做的是往旁一纵——我纵跳之敏捷，连自己都吃惊——趁它擦身而过，把另一枪管的火力送进它的肋侧。这一下要了它的命。弹丸从肩后打进，正正穿透心脏。它侧身倒下，又发出一声骇人的尖叫——十二头猪也闹不出那么大的动静——随即死去，那双凶眼自始至终睁得溜圆。

“至于我，擤了擤鼻子，走到犀牛跟前，一屁股坐在它头上，心想：今天早晨这一场打猎，实在漂亮。”

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. The Editor would have been inclined to think that in relating this incident Mr. Quatermain was making himself interesting at the expense of the exact truth, did it not happen that a similar incident has come within his knowledge. —﻿Editor ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 若不是恰巧有一桩类似的遭遇，也落在编者的见闻之内，编者本来要倾向于认为，夸特梅因先生讲述这件事，是为了把自己说得引人入胜，而不惜牺牲一点事实的确凿。——编者 ↩︎

===Original===
2. For the satisfaction of any who may be so disbelieving as to take this view of Mr. Quatermain’s story, the Editor may state that a gentleman with whom he is acquainted, and whose veracity he believes to be beyond doubt, not long ago described to him how he chanced to kill *four* African elephants with four consecutive bullets. Two of these elephants were charging him simultaneously, and out of the four three were killed with the head shot, a very uncommon thing in the case of the African elephant. —﻿Editor ↩︎

===Chinese===
2. 为叫凡是疑心到这步田地、对夸特梅因先生的故事作此想法的人心服，编者可以说明：有一位编者相识的绅士——其诚实，编者信得过，无可置疑——不久前向他描述过，自己如何碰巧用连发四弹，打死了*四头*非洲象。其中两头同时向他猛冲；而四头之中，有三头是头部一枪毙命——对非洲象来说，这是极罕见的事。——编者 ↩︎

===Original===
3. The largest elephant tusk of which the Editor has any certain knowledge scaled one hundred and fifty pounds. ↩︎

===Chinese===
3. 编者确切知道的、最大的一支象牙，称重一百五十磅。↩︎

===Original===
4. About one hundred and twenty miles. —﻿Editor ↩︎

===Chinese===
4. 约一百二十英里。——编者 ↩︎

## I Gobo Strikes / 一 戈博抗命

===Original===

One day﻿—it was about a week after Allan Quatermain told me his story of the “Three Lions,” and of the moving death of Jim-Jim﻿—he and I were walking home together on the termination of a day’s shooting. He owned about two thousand acres of shooting round the place he had bought in Yorkshire, over a hundred of which were wood. It was the second year of his occupation of the estate, and already he had reared a very fair head of pheasants, for he was an all-round sportsman, and as fond of shooting with a shotgun as with an eight-bore rifle. We were three guns that day, Sir Henry Curtis, Old Quatermain, and myself; but Sir Henry was obliged to leave in the middle of the afternoon in order to meet his agent, and inspect an outlying farm where a new shed was wanted. However, he was coming back to dinner, and going to bring Captain Good with him, for Brayley Hall was not more than two miles from the Grange.

We had met with very fair sport, considering that we were only going through outlying cover for cocks. I think that we had killed twenty-seven, a woodcock and a leash of partridges which we secured out of a driven covey. On our way home there lay a long narrow spinney, which was a very favourite “lie” for woodcocks, and generally held a pheasant or two as well.

“Well, what do you say?” said old Quatermain, “shall we beat through this for a finish?”

===Chinese===

有一天——大约在艾伦·夸特梅因（Allan Quatermain）给我讲完他那个“三头狮子”的故事、讲到吉姆-吉姆（Jim-Jim）感人之死以后一个星期——一天猎事收场，我和他结伴步行回家。他在约克郡置下的那处宅子周围，拥有约两千英亩猎场，其中一百多英亩是林地。那是他住领这份产业的第二个年头，已经养出相当像样的一群雉鸡；他是位样样在行的猎家，玩猎枪同玩八号口径步枪一样兴致勃勃。那天我们一共三支枪：亨利·柯蒂斯爵士（Sir Henry Curtis）、老夸特梅因，还有我；只是柯蒂斯爵士午后中途告退，去会他的经纪人，还要去看一处外围田庄，那边要添盖一座新棚。不过他打算回来吃晚饭，还要把古德上尉（Captain Good）一道带来，因为布雷利庄园（Brayley Hall）离格兰奇田庄（the Grange）不过两英里路。

考虑到我们不过是在外围树丛里寻寻山鹬，这天的收获已算相当像样。我记得我们打死了二十七只，外加一只山鹬，还有从被驱起的一群鹑鸟里稳稳拿下的一小串三只灰山鹑。回家的路上横着一条狭长的小树林，那是山鹬最中意的“落脚地”，通常也总藏着一只两只雉鸡。

“那么，你说呢？”老夸特梅因开口道，“赶完这片林子收工，好不好？”

===Original===

I assented, and he called to the keeper who was following with a little knot of beaters, and told him to beat the spinney.

“Very well, sir,” answered the man, “but it’s getting wonderful dark, and the wind’s rising a gale. It will take you all your time to hit a woodcock if the spinney holds one.”

“You show us the woodcocks, Jeffries,” answered Quatermain quickly, for he never liked being crossed in anything to do with sport, “and we will look after shooting them.”

===Chinese===

我表示赞成，他便唤过身后带着一小队赶猎人的看守人，吩咐他去赶那片小树林。

“好的，先生，”那人应道，“只是天黑得出奇，风也眼看要刮成狂风。这林子里若真藏着山鹬，您得拿出全部本事，才碰得着它一根毛。”

“你只管把山鹬给我们轰出来，杰弗里斯（Jeffries），”夸特梅因立刻回敬道——凡是沾着打猎的事，他从不耐烦人顶撞——“打的事，自有我们来照管。”

===Original===

The man turned and went rather sulkily. I heard him say to the under-keeper, “He’s pretty good, the master is, I’m not saying he isn’t, but if he kills a woodcock in this light and wind, I’m a Dutchman.”

I think that Quatermain heard him too, though he said nothing. The wind was rising every minute, and by the time the beat begun it blew big guns. I stood at the right-hand corner of the spinney, which curved round somewhat, and Quatermain stood at the left, about forty paces from me. Presently an old cock pheasant came rocketing over me, looking as though the feathers were being blown out of his tail. I missed him clean with the first barrel, and was never more pleased with myself in my life than when I doubled him up with the second, for the shot was not an easy one. In the faint light I could see Quatermain nodding his head in approval, when through the groaning of the trees I heard the shouts of the beaters, “Cock forward, cock to the right.” Then came a whole volley of shouts, “Woodcock to the right,” “Cock to the left,” “Cock over.”

===Chinese===

那人转过身，怏怏地走了。我听见他对副看守说：“主人是有两下子，这我不否认；可这样的天色加这样的风，他要是能打下一只山鹬，我就不配当英国人。”

我想夸特梅因也听见了，只是没作声。风一分钟紧过一分钟，等到赶猎开始，已经刮起了狂风。我站在这片微呈弧形的小树林右手一角，夸特梅因站在左手一头，离我约莫四十步。不多时，一只老公雉火箭似的从我头顶蹿过，那副模样，仿佛尾上的羽毛都要给风拔了去。我头一管打了个空，第二管把它打了个正着——一辈子也没对自己这么得意过，因为那一枪委实不易。朦胧的微光里，我望得见夸特梅因点头赞许；就在这时，透过林木的呻吟，我听见赶猎人喊：“山鹬来了，山鹬往右。”跟着便是一整排喊声：“山鹬往右！”“山鹬往左！”“山鹬过顶！”

===Original===

I looked up, and presently caught sight of one of the woodcocks coming down the wind upon me like a flash. In that dim light I could not follow all his movements as he zigzagged through the naked treetops; indeed I could see him when his wings flitted up. Now he was passing me﻿—*bang*, and a flick of the wing, I had missed him; *bang* again. Surely he was down; no, there he went to my left.

“Cock to you,” I shouted, stepping forward so as to get Quatermain between me and the faint angry light of the dying day, for I wanted to see if he would “wipe my eye.” I knew him to be a wonderful shot, but I thought that cock would puzzle him.

I saw him raise his gun ever so little and bend forward, and at that moment out flashed two woodcocks into the open, the one I had missed to his right, and the other to his left.

At the same time a fresh shout arose of, “Woodcock over,” and looking down the spinney I saw a third bird high up in the air, being blown along like a brown and whirling leaf straight over Quatermain’s head. And then followed the prettiest little bit of shooting that I ever saw. The bird to the right was flying low, not ten yards from the line of a hedgerow, and Quatermain took him first because he would become invisible the soonest of any. Indeed, nobody who had not his hawk’s eyes could have seen to shoot at all. But he saw the bird well enough to kill it dead as a stone. Then turning sharply, he pulled on the second bird at about forty-five yards, and over he went. By this time the third woodcock was nearly over him, and flying very high, straight down the wind, a hundred feet up or more, I should say. I saw him glance at it as he opened his gun, threw out the right cartridge and slipped in another, turning round as he did so. By this time the cock was nearly fifty yards away from him, and travelling like a flash. Lifting his gun he fired after it, and, wonderful as the shot was, killed it dead. A tearing gust of wind caught the dead bird, and blew it away like a leaf torn from an oak, so that it fell a hundred and thirty yards off or more.

===Chinese===

我抬头望去，不多时便望见一只山鹬乘风而下，闪电般直朝我来。在那样昏沉的光线里，它在光秃秃的树梢间左盘右旋，我没法盯死它的每个动作；说真的，只有翅膀翻闪的一瞬，才看得见它的影。这会儿它正打我身边掠过——砰！翅膀一抖，我打了空；又是砰！这回总该落了吧——没有，它又蹿到我左手去了。

“山鹬归你了！”我喊道，同时往前跨了一步，好把夸特梅因挡在我同残日那一线微弱而愠怒的天光之间，因为我想看看，他能不能替我“扳回这一城”。我深知他枪法如神，可我料定那只山鹬够他受的。

我见他极轻微地端起枪，身子前倾；说时迟那时快，两只山鹬一齐闪进了空旷处：一只——正是我方才打失的那只——在他右手，另一只在左手。

与此同时，又起了一阵喊：“山鹬过顶！”顺着林子望过去，我看见第三只鸟高高在上，像一片打着旋的褐叶，被风径直吹向夸特梅因的头顶。接着，便是我平生所见最漂亮的一段枪法。右手那只飞得低，离一道树篱的连线不到十码，夸特梅因先打它，因为它最先会隐没不见。说真的，若没有他那一双鹰眼，谁也根本谈不上去瞄准。可他把那鸟瞧得清清楚楚，一枪打得它像块石头似的直坠下来。随即他猛一转身，朝第二只扣动扳机，距离约四十五码，那鸟应声而倒。这时第三只山鹬差不多已到他头顶，飞得极高，乘风直下，我估摸离地足有一百英尺开外。我看见他推开枪膛时朝它瞟了一眼，退出右管的弹壳，顺手换上另一发，人也跟着转了过来。这时那鹬已离他将近五十码，疾如一道闪光。他端起枪，朝它背后就是一枪——那一枪神到极处，把它打得当场毙命。一阵狂风兜住死鸟，把它像一片从橡树上撕下的叶子般刮了出去，落在一百三十码开外。

===Original===

“I say, Quatermain,” I said to him when the beaters were up, “do you often do this sort of thing?”

“Well,” he answered, with a dry smile, “the last time I had to load three shots as quickly as that was at rather larger game. It was at elephants. I killed them all three as dead as I killed those woodcocks; but it very nearly went the other way, I can tell you; I mean that they very nearly killed me.”

===Chinese===

赶猎人赶到跟前时，我对他说：“我说夸特梅因，这种事你常做么？”

“唔，”他带着干巴巴的笑答道，“上一回要我那样一口气装三发子弹，打的可是个头大得多的猎物——是大象。那三头象同这三只山鹬一样，全叫我打得死死的；可是事情的结局差点儿掉了个个儿，这我告诉你；我的意思是，差点儿它们把我给打死了。”

===Original===

Just at that moment the keeper came up. “Did you happen to get one of them there cocks, sir?” he said, with the air of a man who did not in the least expect an answer in the affirmative.

“Well, yes, Jeffries,” answered Quatermain; “you will find one of them by the hedge, and another about fifty yards out by the plough there to the left﻿—”

The keeper had turned to go, looking a little astonished, when Quatermain called him back.

“Stop a bit, Jeffries,” he said. “You see that pollard about one hundred and forty yards off? Well, there should be another woodcock down in a line with it, about sixty paces out in the field.”

“Well, if that bean’t the very smartest bit of shooting,” murmured Jeffries, and departed.

===Chinese===

正在这时，看守人走上前来。“先生，那几只山鹬您当真打着了么？”他说，那副神气，压根不像指望得到一句“打着了”。

“呃，着了，杰弗里斯，”夸特梅因答道，“一只在树篱边上，还有一只在左边犁地的那边，出去五十码上下——”

看守人已然转身要走，脸上带着几分惊讶，夸特梅因又把他叫了回来。

“慢着点，杰弗里斯，”他说，“你瞧见那棵截头树么，一百四十码开外？嗯，同它成一条直线、往地里去六十步的样子，还该落着一只山鹬。”

“好家伙，这可真是没挑的枪法，”杰弗里斯咕哝一声，走了。

===Original===

After that we went home, and in due course Sir Henry Curtis and Captain Good arrived for dinner, the latter arrayed in the tightest and most ornamental dress-suit I ever saw. I remember that the waistcoat was adorned with five pink coral buttons.

It was a very pleasant dinner. Old Quatermain was in an excellent humour; induced, I think, by the recollection of his triumph over the doubting Jeffries. Good, too, was full of anecdotes. He told us a most miraculous story of how he once went shooting ibex in Kashmir. These ibex, according to Good, he stalked early and late for four entire days. At last on the morning of the fifth day he succeeded in getting within range of the flock, which consisted of a magnificent old ram with horns so long that I am afraid to mention their measure, and five or six females. Good crawled upon his stomach, painfully taking shelter behind rocks, till he was within two hundred yards; then he drew a fine bead upon the old ram. At this moment, however, a diversion occurred. Some wandering native of the hills appeared upon a distant mountain top. The females turned, and rushing over a rock vanished from Good’s ken. But the old ram took a bolder course. In front of him stretched a mighty crevasse at least thirty feet in width. He went at it with a bound. Whilst he was in midair Good fired, and killed him dead. The ram turned a complete somersault in space, and fell in such fashion that his horns hooked themselves upon a big projection of the opposite cliffs. There he hung, till Good, after a long and painful detour, gracefully dropped a lasso over him and fished him up.

This moving tale of wild adventure was received with undeserved incredulity.

“Well,” said Good, “if you fellows won’t believe my story when I tell it﻿—a perfectly true story mind﻿—perhaps one of you will give us a better; I’m not particular if it is true or not.” And he lapsed into a dignified silence.

===Chinese===

此后我们便回了家。到得时候，亨利·柯蒂斯爵士和古德上尉来赴晚宴，后一位身穿我平生所见最紧、最花哨的一身晚礼服。我记得那件马甲上缀着五枚粉红的珊瑚纽扣。

那顿晚饭吃得极其惬意。老夸特梅因兴致极高，我想，多半是想起自己在满腹狐疑的杰弗里斯面前大获全胜的缘故。古德也是一肚皮的趣闻。他给我们讲了个神乎其神的故事，说他有一回在克什米尔猎北山羊。照古德的说法，为了这群羊，他从早到晚整整潜近了四天。到第五天早晨，他总算蹭进了羊群的射程——那是一头气度不凡的老公羊，领着五六只母羊，公羊的角长得吓人，长得我都怕报出它们的尺寸。古德肚皮贴地匍匐而行，辛辛苦苦借一块块岩石藏身，直到离羊群不到两百码；然后他对老公羊端端正正瞄了个准。偏偏在这时岔出了事。山里某个游荡的土人出现在远处的山头上。母羊们掉头涌过一块岩石，从古德的视野里消失了。倒是那头老公羊胆气更壮：它面前横着一道大得吓人的裂罅，少说也有三十英尺宽。它一个纵身扑了过去。趁它悬在半空，古德开枪，把它当场打死。公羊在空中翻了一个整整齐齐的跟头，落下去时，两只角不偏不倚，钩住了对面峭壁上一块凸岩。它就那么挂着，直到古德绕了一大段艰苦的远路，风度翩翩地抛出一条套索，套住它，把它钓了上来。

这一段感人的荒野奇遇，得到的却是它不该得的满座狐疑。

“好吧，”古德说，“我亲口讲的故事你们偏不信——提醒你们，这可是个千真万确的故事——那也许哪一位乐意，讲个更好的出来；是真是假，我倒不计较。”说罢，他摆出一副庄严的沉默。

===Original===

“Now, Quatermain,” I said, “don’t let Good beat you, let us hear how you killed those elephants you were talking about this evening just after you shot the woodcocks.”

“Well,” said Quatermain, dryly, and with something like a twinkle in his brown eyes, “it is very hard fortune for a man to have to follow on Good’s ‘spoor.’ Indeed if it were not for that running giraffe which, as you will remember, Curtis, we saw Good bowl over with a Martini rifle at three hundred yards, I should almost have said that this was an impossible tale.”

Here Good looked up with an air of indignant innocence.

“However,” he went on, rising and lighting his pipe, “if you fellows like, I will spin you a yarn. I was telling one of you the other night about those three lions and how the lioness finished my unfortunate *voorlooper*, Jim-Jim, the boy whom we buried in the bread-bag.

===Chinese===

“好了，夸特梅因，”我说，“别让古德把你压下去；给我们讲讲那几头象你是怎么打的——傍晚你打下山鹬之后提起的那档子事。”

“唔，”夸特梅因干巴巴地说，棕色眼睛里隐隐透出一点笑意，“一个人偏偏得踩着古德的‘兽迹’往下讲，这运气象话么。说真的，若不是有那头奔跑的长颈鹿——柯蒂斯，你总记得吧，我们亲眼见古德用马蒂尼步枪在三百码外把它放倒——我几乎要说，这故事根本不可能。”

古德在这里抬起头，一脸又无辜又愤慨的神气。

“不过，”他接着说，一面起身点起烟斗，“你们几位要是乐意，我就给你们扯一段。前几天晚上我跟你们里头一位讲过那三头狮子，讲过那头母狮怎样结果了我那倒霉的赶牛童（voorlooper）吉姆-吉姆——就是那个我们裹在面包袋里埋掉的孩子。”

===Original===

“Well, after this little experience I thought that I would settle down a bit, so I entered upon a venture with a man who, being of a speculative mind, had conceived the idea of running a store at Pretoria upon strictly cash principles. The arrangement was that I should find the capital and he the experience. Our partnership was not of a long duration. The Boers refused to pay cash, and at the end of four months my partner had the capital and I had the experience. After this I came to the conclusion that store-keeping was not in my line, and having four hundred pounds left, I sent my boy Harry to a school in Natal, and buying an outfit with what remained of the money, started upon a big trip.

“This time I determined to go further afield than I had ever been before; so I took a passage for a few pounds in a trading brig that ran between Durban and Delagoa Bay. From Delagoa Bay I marched inland accompanied by twenty porters, with the idea of striking up north, towards the Limpopo, and keeping parallel to the coast, but at a distance of about one hundred and fifty miles from it. For the first twenty days of our journey we suffered a good deal from fever, that is, my men did, for I think that I am fever proof. Also I was hard put to it to keep the camp in meat, for although the country proved to be very sparsely populated, there was but little game about. Indeed, during all that time I hardly killed anything larger than a waterbuck, and, as you know, waterbuck’s flesh is not very appetising food. On the twentieth day, however, we came to the banks of a largish river, the Gonooroo it was called. This I crossed, and then struck inland towards a great range of mountains, the blue crests of which we could see lying on the distant heavens like a shadow, a continuation, as I believe, of the Drakensberg range that skirts the coast of Natal. From this main range a great spur shoots out some fifty miles or so towards the coast, ending abruptly in one tremendous peak. This spur I discovered separated the territories of two chiefs named Nala and Wambe, Wambe’s territory being to the north, and Nala’s to the south. Nala ruled a tribe of bastard Zulus called the Butiana, and Wambe a much larger tribe, called the Matuku, which presents marked Bantu characteristics. For instance, they have doors and verandahs to their huts, work skins perfectly, and wear a waistcloth and not a *moocha*. At this time the Butiana were more or less subject to the Matuku, having been surprised by them some twenty years before and mercilessly slaughtered down. The tribe was now recovering itself, however, and as you may imagine, it did not love the Matuku.

===Chinese===

“呃，经过这段小小的阅历，我想我该安分些时候了，于是同一个人合伙做起生意。此人天生一副投机的脑筋，想出一条章程，要在比勒陀利亚开一家铺子，严格现钱交易。讲定我出本钱，他出经验。我们这段合伙为期不长。布尔人不肯付现钱，到四个月头上，本钱归了伙计，经验归了我。此后我得出结论：开铺子非我所长。手里还剩四百镑，我把儿子哈里送进纳塔尔的一所学校，又拿余下的钱置办了行装，动身作一趟大远行。”

“这一回我决意走得比以往哪次都远，于是花几镑钱，搭上一条跑德班与德拉瓜湾一线的商船。从德拉瓜湾起岸，我带着二十名挑夫向内陆进发，打算一路往北，朝林波波河方向打过去，与海岸保持平行，但离海岸约一百五十英里。旅程头二十天，我们深受热病之苦——说准确些，吃苦的是我的人，因为我自己觉得天生不染热病。要让营地顿顿见肉，也够我搜肠刮肚：这地方人烟固然稀少得出奇，野物却也不多。说真的，那些日子里，我打下的大家伙，几乎没有一只大过水羚，而你们知道，水羚的肉实在开不了胃口。可是到第二十天，我们来到一条不小的河边，河名叫戈诺鲁（Gonooroo）。渡过这条河，我便朝一道大山的方向折进内陆；那山的蓝色峰顶远远浮在天际，像一片影子，我相信，它是环着纳塔尔海岸的德拉肯斯堡山脉的延续。从这条主脉探出一道大山梁，朝海岸方向绵延五十英里上下，到一座巍峨的孤峰处戛然而止。我发现，这道山梁隔开了两位酋长的地界：一位名叫纳拉（Nala），一位名叫万贝（Wambe）；万贝的地界在北，纳拉的在南。纳拉治下是一支祖鲁混血的部族，唤作布蒂亚纳（Butiana）；万贝治下的部族大得多，唤作马图库（Matuku），班图特征十分鲜明。比如，他们的屋子有门有廊，皮子鞣得极精，腰间系的是一块缠腰布，而不是祖鲁人的皮围裙（moocha）。那时候，布蒂亚纳人多少臣服于马图库人——大约二十年前，他们被马图库人偷袭，杀得血流成河。不过这族如今正缓过元气来，你们可以想见，他们爱马图库人是爱不到哪里去的。

===Original===

“Well, I heard as I went along that elephants were very plentiful in the dense forests which lie upon the slopes and at the foot of the mountains that border Wambe’s territory. Also I heard a very ill report of that worthy himself, who lived in a kraal upon the side of the mountain, which was so strongly fortified as to be practically impregnable. It was said that he was the most cruel chief in this part of Africa, and that he had murdered in cold blood an entire party of English gentlemen, who, some seven years before, had gone into his country to hunt elephants. They took an old friend of mine with them as guide, John Every by name, and often had I mourned over his untimely death. All the same, Wambe or no Wambe, I determined to hunt elephants in his country. I never was afraid of natives, and I was not going to show the white feather now. I am a bit of a fatalist, as you fellows know, so I came to the conclusion that if it was fated that Wambe should send me to join my old friend John Every, I should have to go, and there was an end of it. Meanwhile, I meant to hunt elephants with a peaceful heart.

“On the third day from the date of our sighting the great peak, we found ourselves beneath its shadow. Still following the course of the river which wound through the forests at the base of the peak, we entered the territory of the redoubtable Wambe. This, however, was not accomplished without a certain difference of opinion between my bearers and myself, for when we reached the spot where Wambe’s boundary was supposed to run, the bearers sat down and emphatically refused to go a step further. I sat down too, and argued with them, putting my fatalistic views before them as well as I was able. But I could not persuade them to look at the matter in the same light. ‘At present,’ they said, ‘their skins were whole; if they went into Wambe’s country without his leave they would soon be like a water-eaten leaf. It was very well for me to say that this would be Fate. Fate no doubt might be walking about in Wambe’s country, but while they stopped outside they would not meet him.’

===Chinese===

“一路行去，我听说在万贝地界边缘那些山的山坡和山脚，密林里大象多得很。关于那位贵人本人的风评，我听来也坏得很：他住在山坡上一座克拉尔（kraal）里，那寨子工事坚固，实际上根本攻不破。据说他是非洲这一带最残暴的酋长，七年前，曾把整整一队进他地界猎象的英国绅士杀得一个不留。他们请了一位我的旧识做向导，名叫约翰·埃弗里（John Every）；他这番横死，我不知哀悼过多少回。话虽如此，管他万贝不万贝，我打定了主意，要到他地界里去猎象。我从不怕土人，这回也不打算临阵露怯。你们几位都知道，我多少算个宿命论者，于是我得出结论：倘若命里注定万贝要送我去同老朋友约翰·埃弗里作伴，那我只得上路，如此而已。在这以前，我且安安心心猎我的象。”

“望见那座大孤峰之后第三天，我们已置身峰影之下。我仍旧循着那条河走——它在孤峰脚下的林间蜿蜒——就这样进了威名远播的万贝的地界。不过这一步迈得并不顺当，我的挑夫同我之间起了一场不小的龃龉：因为走到据说万贝地界线所经之处，挑夫们便一屁股坐下，斩钉截铁地声言，再不肯朝前挪动一步。我也坐下，同他们讲理，尽力把我的宿命论摊给他们听。可我没法叫他们用同样的眼光看待这件事。‘眼下，’他们说，‘他们的皮肉还囫囵着；若不得到万贝许可便闯进他的地界，不出几时，他们就会像一片泡烂在水里的叶子。我自管说这就是命运——命运女神呢，毫无疑问，兴许正在万贝的地界里四处游荡；可只要他们守在外头，就不会同她打照面。’”

===Original===

“ ‘Well,’ I said to Gobo, my head man, ‘and what do you mean to do?’

“ ‘We mean to go back to the coast, Macumazahn,’ he answered insolently.

“ ‘Do you?’ I replied, for my bile was stirred. ‘At any rate, Mr. Gobo, you and one or two others will never get there; see here, my friend,’ and I took a repeating rifle and sat myself comfortably down, resting my back against a tree﻿—‘I have just breakfasted, and I had as soon spend the day here as anywhere else. Now if you or any of those men walk one step back from here, and towards the coast, I shall fire at you; and you know that I don’t miss.’

===Chinese===

“‘那么，’我对我的头人戈博（Gobo）说，‘你们打算怎么办？’”

“‘我们打算回海边去，马库马赞（Macumazahn），’他答得放肆无礼。”

“‘是么？’我答道，一股无名火顶了上来。‘那也使得，戈博先生，只是你，还有那么一两位，这辈子是到不了海边了；瞧着，我的朋友，’说着我取过一支连发步枪，舒舒服服地坐定，脊背靠着一棵树——‘我早饭才下肚，在这儿消磨一天，同在哪儿消磨都一样。现在听着：你，或者他们里头随便哪一个，只要从这里朝回走、朝海边方向迈出一步，我就开枪；我枪下没有活口，这你们是知道的。’”

===Original===

“The man fingered the spear he was carrying﻿—luckily all my guns were stacked against the tree﻿—and then turned as though to walk away, the others keeping their eyes fixed upon him all the while. I rose and covered him with the rifle, and though he kept up a brave appearance of unconcern, I saw that he was glancing nervously at me all the time. When he had gone about twenty yards I spoke very quietly﻿—

“ ‘Now, Gobo,’ I said, ‘come back, or I shall fire.’

“Of course this was taking a very high hand; I had no real right to kill Gobo or anybody else because they objected to run the risk of death by entering the territory of a hostile chief. But I felt that if I wished to keep up any authority it was absolutely necessary that I should push matters to the last extremity short of actually shooting him. So I sat there, looking fierce as a lion, and keeping the sight of my rifle in a dead line for Gobo’s ribs. Then Gobo, feeling that the situation was getting strained, gave in.

===Chinese===

那人捻了捻手里提着的那支矛——幸而我所有的枪都码在那棵树根旁——随即转过身，作势要走；其余的人自始至终把眼睛钉在他身上。我站起身，用枪口罩住他；他虽强撑出一副若无其事的硬气，我却看见，他一直提心吊胆地拿眼角瞟我。他走出去约莫二十码，我极其平静地开了口——

“‘听着，戈博，’我说，‘回来，不然我就开枪。’”

当然，这是十足的霸道手段。人家不肯冒死闯进一个敌对酋长的地界，我原没有真权利为这个杀掉戈博或者随便什么人。但我明白，我若想维持半点威信，就必须把事态逼到极限——只差真开枪的那一步。于是我坐在那里，凶得像头狮子，枪上的准星死死咬在戈博的肋条上。戈博觉得局面越绷越紧，终于软了下来。

===Original===

“ ‘Don’t shoot, Boss,’ he shouted, throwing up his hand, ‘I will come with you.’

“ ‘I thought you would,’ I answered quietly; ‘you see Fate walks about outside Wambe’s country as well as in it.’

“After that I had no more trouble, for Gobo was the ringleader, and when he collapsed the others collapsed also. Harmony being thus restored, we crossed the line, and on the following morning I began shooting in good earnest.”

===Chinese===

“‘别开枪，老板！’他扬起一只手喊道，‘我同您去。’”

“‘我料到你会的，’我不动声色地答道，‘你瞧，命运女神不光在万贝的地界里游荡，外头也一样游荡。’”

从此再没出过乱子，因为戈博是带头闹事的，他一塌，其余的人也跟着塌了。太平既复，我们跨过界线；次日清晨，我便放开手脚，认认真真打起猎来。

## VIII Maiwa Is Avenged / 八 迈瓦大仇得报

===Original===
“The impi formed up; alas, an hour before it had been stronger by a third than it was now. Then Nala detached two hundred men to collect and attend to the injured, and at my suggestion issued a stringent order that none of the enemy’s wounded, and above all no women or children, were to be killed, as is the savage custom among African natives. On the contrary, they were to be allowed to send word to their women that they might come in to nurse them and fear nothing, for Nala made war upon Wambe the tyrant, and not on the Matuku tribe.

“Then we started with some four hundred men for the chief’s kraal. Very soon we were there. It was, as I have said, placed against the mountain side, but within the fortified lines, and did not at all cover more than an acre and a half of ground. Outside was a tiny reed fence, within which, neatly arranged in a semicircular line, stood the huts of the chief’s principal wives. Maiwa of course knew every inch of the kraal, for she had lived in it, and led us straight to the entrance. We peeped through the gateway﻿—not a soul was to be seen. There were the huts and there was the clear open space floored with a concrete of lime, on which the sun beat fiercely, but nobody could we see or hear.

“ ‘The jackal has gone to earth,’ said Maiwa; ‘he will be in the cave behind his hut,’ and she pointed with her spear towards another small and semicircular enclosure, over which a large hut was visible, that had the cliff itself for a background. I stared at this fence; by George! it was true, it was entirely made of tusks of ivory planted in the ground with their points bending outwards. The smallest ones, though none were small, were placed nearest to the cliff on either side, but they gradually increased in size till they culminated in two enormous tusks, which, set up so that their points met, something in the shape of an inverted V, formed the gateway to the hut. I was dumbfounded with delight; and indeed, where is the elephant-hunter who would not be, if he suddenly saw five or six hundred picked tusks set up in a row, and only waiting for him to take them away? Of course the stuff was what is known as ‘black’ ivory; that is, the exterior of the tusks had become black from years or perhaps centuries of exposure to wind and weather, but I was certain that it would be none the worse for that. Forgetting the danger of the deed, in my excitement I actually ran right across the open space, and drawing my knife scratched vigorously at one of the great tusks to see how deep the damage might be. As I thought, it was nothing; there beneath the black covering gleamed the pure white ivory. I could have capered for joy, for I fear that I am very mercenary at heart, when suddenly I heard the faint echo of a cry for assistance. ‘Help!’ screamed a voice in the Sisutu dialect from somewhere behind the hut; ‘help! they are murdering me.’

===Chinese===
“战队整好了队列；唉，一个钟头之前，它还比眼下多三分之一的兵力。于是纳拉拨出两百人去收拢照看伤员，又依我的建议下了一道严令：敌军伤员一概不得杀害，妇女儿童更是万万不可——那原是非洲土人的蛮俗。非但如此，还须准许伤员捎信给自己的女人，叫她们进寨来看护，什么都不必怕，因为纳拉讨伐的是暴君万贝，不是马图库全族。

“随后我们带着约四百人向酋长的克拉尔进发，不多时便到了。如前所述，那寨子背倚山壁，却又在环寨防线之内，占地决计不过一英亩半。外面围着一道矮矮的芦苇栅，栅内是酋长众正妻的棚屋，排成整齐的半圆。迈瓦在寨里住过，对这里每一寸地方都了如指掌，径直把我们领到入口。我们朝寨门里张望——竟不见一个人影。棚屋俱在，那片用石灰夯出的空场也在，烈日直晒在场上，可我们既看不见人，也听不见声息。

“‘豺狼入洞了，’迈瓦说，‘他准是在自己棚屋后面的山洞里。’她举起长矛，指向另一处半圆的小围场，围场上首露出一座大棚屋，背衬便是峭壁本身。我死死盯着那道栅栏看——老天爷！果不其然：它整个儿是用象牙栽进地里做成的，牙尖一概向外弯去。最小的一些牙——其实哪支都不算小——排在最贴近崖壁的两侧，越往中间越是粗大，到顶头是两支硕大无朋的巨牙，竖得牙尖相抵，恰成一个倒写的 V 字，便是那棚屋的门户。我欢喜得呆了。说实在的，哪个猎象人若猛然见到五六百支上等象牙排成一列，专等他去搬走，能不失魂落魄？这些自然都是所谓的‘黑’象牙；就是说，牙身经年累月——也许几个世纪——风吹雨打，外皮已经发黑，但我断定这丝毫无损于成色。我一得意便忘了危险，竟当真一路小跑穿过空场，拔出刀来对着一支大牙使劲刮蹭，要看看损伤究竟多深。不出所料，一点没事：黑皮底下闪出纯白的象牙。我险些手舞足蹈起来——我怕自己骨子里本是个财迷——忽听得一声微弱的呼救飘了过来。‘救命啊！’棚屋后面什么地方有人用西苏图语尖叫，‘救命啊！他们要杀我！’

===Original===
“*I knew the voice*; it was John Every’s. Oh, what a selfish brute was I! For the moment that miserable ivory had driven the recollection of him out of my head, and now﻿—perhaps it was too late.

“Nala, Maiwa, and the soldiers had now come up. They too heard the voice and interpreted its tone, though they had not caught the words.

“ ‘This way,’ cried Maiwa, and we started at a run, passing round the hut of Wambe. Behind was the narrow entrance to a cave. We rushed through it heedless of the danger of the ambush, and this is what we saw, though very confusedly at first, owing to the gloom.

===Chinese===
“*那嗓音我听得出来*；是约翰·埃弗里的。唉，我是个多自私的畜生！方才那些可恶的象牙，竟把他从我脑子里赶了出去，而此刻——也许已经太迟了。

“这时纳拉、迈瓦和士兵们也赶了上来。他们也听见了那嗓音，也听出了那声调里的意思，虽然字句没有听清。

“‘这边走，’迈瓦喊道。我们撒腿就跑，绕过万贝的棚屋。屋后是一道狭窄的洞口。我们不顾埋伏的凶险，一头冲了进去，看到的便是这样一番景象——起初因洞中幽暗，只看得影影绰绰：

===Original===
“In the centre of the cave, and with either end secured to the floor by strong stakes, stood a huge double-springed lion trap edged with sharp and grinning teeth. It was set, and beyond the trap, indeed almost over it, a terrible struggle was in progress. A naked or almost naked white man, with a great beard hanging down over his breast, in spite of his furious struggles, was being slowly forced and dragged towards the trap by six or eight women. Only one man was present, a fat, cruel-looking man with small eyes and a hanging lip. It was the chief Wambe, and he stood by the trap ready to force the victim down upon it so soon as the women had dragged him into the necessary position.

“At this instant they caught sight of us, and there came a moment’s pause, and then, before I knew what she was going to do, Maiwa lifted the assegai she still held, and whirled it at Wambe’s head. I saw the flash of light speed towards him, and so did he, for he stepped backward to avoid it﻿—stepped backward right into the trap. He yelled with pain as the iron teeth of the ‘Thing that bites’ sprang up with a rattling sound like living fangs and fastened into him﻿—such a yell I have not often heard. Now at last he tasted of the torture which he had inflicted upon so many, and though I trust I am a Christian, I cannot say that I felt sorry for him.

===Chinese===
“山洞中央立着一具巨大的双簧狮阱，两端用粗壮的木桩牢牢钉死在地上，阱缘是一圈锋利而狰狞的牙齿，弹簧上得满满的。陷阱那头——几乎就在阱口上方——一场骇人的挣扎正在进行。一个赤裸的、或者说几乎赤裸的白人，胸前垂着一大把胡须，任凭他拼命挣扎，还是被六八个女人连拖带拽，一寸一寸往那陷阱挪去。在场的男人只有一个：肥头大耳，一脸凶相，小眼睛，耷拉着嘴唇。正是酋长万贝。他守在陷阱旁边，单等女人们把受难的人拖到位，便把他按下去。

“就在这一瞬，他们瞥见了我们，场中顿了一顿；我还没明白她要做什么，迈瓦已经举起手中仍握着的那支长矛，朝万贝的脑袋掷了出去。我只见一道白光向他飞去——他也看见了，慌忙向后一躲——正一脚踏进陷阱。‘咬人的东西’的铁齿咔啦啦弹起，像活生生的獠牙般咬进他的身子，他痛得惨嚎——那样惨烈的嚎叫，我生平少闻。如今他总算亲口尝到了自己施加给无数人的酷刑。我自信还算个基督徒，可我没法说自己为此难过。

===Original===
“The assegai sped on and struck one of the women who had hold of the unfortunate Every, piercing through her arm. This made her leave go, an example that the other women quickly followed, so that Every fell to the ground, where he lay gasping.

“ ‘Kill the witches,’ roared Nala, in a voice of thunder, pointing to the group of women.

“ ‘Nay,’ gasped Every, ‘spare them. He made them do it,’ and he pointed to the human fiend in the trap. Then Maiwa waved her hand to us to fall back, for the moment of her vengeance was come. We did so, and she strode up to her lord, and flinging the white robe from her stood before him, her fierce beautiful face fixed like stone.

===Chinese===
“那支长矛去势不减，扎中了攥着倒霉的埃弗里的一名妇人，穿透了她的胳膊。她吃痛松了手，别的妇人见状纷纷效尤，于是埃弗里跌倒在地，躺在那里直喘。

“‘杀了这些女巫，’纳拉雷鸣般吼道，手指那群妇人。

“‘不，’埃弗里喘着气道，‘饶了她们。是他逼她们干的。’他指着阱中那个人形的恶魔。迈瓦挥手叫我们退后，因为她报仇的时刻到了。我们依言退下。她大步走到她夫君跟前，把白袍从身上扯下，直挺挺站在他面前，那张凶悍而美丽的脸凝定如石。

===Original===
“ ‘Who am I?’ she cried in so terrible a voice that he ceased his yells. ‘Am I that woman who was given to thee for wife, and whose child thou slewest? Or am I an avenging spirit come to see thee die?

“ ‘What is this?’ she went on, drawing the withered baby-hand from the pouch at her side.

“ ‘Is it the hand of a babe? and how came that hand to be thus alone? What cut it off from the babe? and where is the babe? Is it a hand? or is it the vision of a hand that shall presently tear thy throat?

===Chinese===
“‘我是谁？’她喝道，声音森然可怕，他竟止住了惨嚎。‘我是那个奉与你为妻、被你亲手杀了孩子的女人？还是来亲眼看你死去的复仇之灵？

“‘这是什么？’她接着说，从腰间皮囊里抽出一只干瘪的婴儿小手。

“‘这是一只婴孩的手么？这只手怎会孤零零落得这般？是谁把它从婴孩身上剁下来的？婴孩又在哪里？这是一只手么？还是一只手的幻影，转眼便要撕开你的喉咙？

===Original===
“ ‘Where are thy soldiers, Wambe? Do they sleep and eat and go forth to do thy bidding? or are they perchance dead and scattered like the winter leaves?’

“He groaned and rolled his eyes while the fierce-faced woman went on.

“ ‘Art thou still a chief, Wambe? or does another take thy place and power, and say, Lord, what doest thou there? and what is that slave’s leglet upon thy knee?

===Chinese===
“‘你的士兵在哪里，万贝？他们还在酣睡饱食、听调听宣么？还是早死了、散了，像冬天的落叶一般？’

“他呻吟着，眼珠乱转，那神情凶悍的女人继续说下去。

“‘你还是酋长么，万贝？还是已有别人占了你的位子和权柄，说：主公，你在那里做什么？你膝上戴着的那条奴隶腿环，又是怎么一回事？

===Original===
“ ‘Is it a dream, Wambe, great lord and chief? or’﻿—and she lifted her clenched hands and shook them in his face﻿—‘hath a woman’s vengeance found thee out and a woman’s wit o’ermatched thy tyrannous strength? and art thou about to slowly die in torments horrible to think on, oh, thou accursed murderer of little children?’

“And with one wild scream she dashed the dead hand of the child straight into his face, and then fell senseless on the floor. As for the demon in the trap, he shrank back so far as its iron bounds would allow, his yellow eyes starting out of his head with pain and terror, and then once more began to yell.

“The scene was more than I could bear.

===Chinese===
“‘这是一场梦么，万贝，尊贵的大酋长？还是说’——她攥紧双拳，举到他脸前摇晃——‘一个女人的复仇寻上了你，一个女人的心智压倒了你的暴虐强梁？你就要在不堪设想的酷刑里慢慢死去，啊，你这残杀婴孩的该死的凶手？’

“随着一声狂呼，她把那只孩子的死手直掼在他脸上，随即昏死在地。至于阱中那个恶魔，他一味往铁齿容许的尽头里缩，一双黄眼睛因剧痛和惊怖凸出眶外，随即又嚎叫起来。

“这一幕我实在受不住。

===Original===
“ ‘Nala,’ I said, ‘this must stop. That man is a fiend, but he must not be left to die there. See thou to it.’

“ ‘Nay,’ answered Nala, ‘let him taste of the food wherewith he hath fed so many; leave him till death shall find him.’

“ ‘That I will not,’ I answered. ‘Let his end be swift; see thou to it.’

“ ‘As thou wilt, Macumazahn,’ answered the chief, with a shrug of the shoulders; ‘first let the white man and Maiwa be brought forth.’

“So the soldiers came forward and carried Every and the woman into the open air. As the former was borne past his tormentor, the fallen chief, so cowardly was his wicked heart, actually prayed him to intercede for him, and save him from a fate which, but for our providential appearance, would have been Every’s own.

===Chinese===
“‘纳拉，’我说，‘这可不能再拖了。那人是个恶魔，但不能任他死在那里。你去办了。’

“‘不，’纳拉答道，‘让他尝尝他喂过多少人的那道食；留他在那儿，等死神自己寻来。’

“‘我不答应，’我说，‘给他个痛快。你去办。’

“‘随你吧，马库马赞，’酋长耸耸肩答道，‘只是先让白人和迈瓦出来。’

“于是士兵们上前，把埃弗里和那妇人抬到露天去。前者被抬着打他那位刑主、那位倒台的酋长身边经过时——他那颗歹毒的心何其怯懦——竟开口求埃弗里代为说情，救他脱离那个下场；若不是我们天意一般及时赶到，那下场本来是埃弗里自己的。

===Original===
“So we went away, and in another moment one of the biggest villains on the earth troubled it no more. Once in the fresh air Every recovered quickly. I looked at him, and horror and sorrow pierced me through to see such a sight. His face was the face of a man of sixty, though he was not yet forty, and his poor body was cut to pieces with stripes and scars, and other marks of the torments which Wambe had for years amused himself with inflicting on him.

“As soon as he recovered himself a little he struggled on to his knees, burst into a paroxysm of weeping, and clasping my legs with his emaciated arms, would have actually kissed my feet.

“ ‘What are you about, old fellow?’ I said, for I am not accustomed to that sort of thing, and it made me feel uncomfortable.

===Chinese===
“我们于是走开；不多时，世上数一数二的大恶棍，便不再搅扰这个世界了。一到露天下，埃弗里很快缓过劲来。我看着他，恐惧与哀怜一齐刺穿我的心。那张脸是六十岁人的脸，其实他还不到四十；那可怜的身子，被鞭痕、伤疤，被万贝多年来拿折磨他取乐留下的种种印记，割裂得体无完肤。

“他稍稍定下神来，便挣扎着跪起，一阵失声痛哭，用枯瘦的胳膊搂住我的双腿，竟真要来吻我的脚。

“‘你这是干什么，老伙计？’我说。这种礼数我可受不惯，只觉得浑身不自在。

===Original===
“ ‘Oh, God bless you!’ he moaned, ‘God bless you! If only you knew what I have gone through; and to think that you should have come to help me, and at the risk of your own life! Well, you were always a true friend﻿—yes, yes, a true friend.’

“ ‘Bosh,’ I answered testily; ‘I’m a trader, and I came after that ivory,’ and I pointed to the stockade of tusks. ‘Did you ever hear of an elephant-hunter who would not have risked his immortal soul for them, and much more his carcase?’

“But he took no notice of my explanations, and went on God blessing me as hard as ever, till at last I bethought me that a nip of brandy, of which I had a flask full, might steady his nerves a bit. I gave it him, and was not disappointed in the result, for he brisked up wonderfully. Then I hunted about in Wambe’s hut, and found a kaross to put over his poor bruised shoulders, and he was quite a man again.

===Chinese===
“‘噢，上帝保佑你！’他呜咽道，‘上帝保佑你！你要是知道我受的是什么就好了；想想吧，你竟赶了来救我，还是拿自己的性命冒险！唉，你从来是个真朋友——是呀，是呀，真朋友。’

“‘废话，’我不耐烦地答道，‘我是个买卖人，我是冲着象牙来的，’说着朝那道牙栅一指，‘你几时听说过哪个猎象人，不肯为它们拿不朽的灵魂去冒险？更别说这百十来斤的皮囊了。’

“可他全不理会我的辩解，只管一个劲儿求上帝保佑我，闹到最后，我想起怀里还有满满一瓶白兰地，喝上一口也许能叫他定定神。我给他灌了下去，果然见效，他一下子精神大振。随后我在万贝的棚屋里翻捡，找到一件卡罗斯（兽皮披肩），给他那可怜的、青紫斑斑的肩膀披上，他顿时又像个人样了。

===Original===
“ ‘Now,’ I said, ‘why did the late lamented Wambe want to put you in that trap?’

“ ‘Because as soon as they heard that the fight was going against them, and that Maiwa was charging at the head of Nala’s impi, one of the women told Wambe that she had seen me write something on some leaves and give them to Maiwa before she went away to purify herself. Then of course he guessed that I had to do with your seizing the koppie and holding it while the impi rushed the place from the mountain, so he determined to torture me to death before help could come. Oh, heavens! what a mercy it is to hear English again.’

===Chinese===
“‘好了，’我说，‘方才那位不幸殒命的万贝，为什么要把你塞进那个陷阱？’

“‘因为战局不利的消息一传开——又听说迈瓦正带着纳拉的战队在阵前冲杀——有个女人便告诉万贝，说她亲眼见我在几张叶子上写字，在迈瓦动身去净身之前交给了她。他自然一下子就猜着了：抢占石丘、死守待援、放战队翻山袭寨，这些事都与我相干。于是他打定主意，要赶在救兵来到之前，先把我折磨死。噢，天哪！这会儿能再听见英国话，真是天大的福气。’

===Original===
“ ‘How long have you been a prisoner here, Every?’ I asked.

“ ‘Six years and a bit, Quatermain; I have lost count of the odd months lately. I came up here with Major Aldey and three other gentlemen and forty bearers. That devil Wambe ambushed us, and murdered the lot to get their guns. They weren’t much use to him when he got them, being breech-loaders, for the fools fired away all the ammunition in a month or two. However, they are all in good order, and hanging up in the hut there. They didn’t kill me because one of them saw me mending a gun just before they attacked us, so they kept me as a kind of armourer. Twice I tried to make a bolt of it, but was caught each time. Last time Wambe had me flogged very nearly to death﻿—you can see the scars upon my back. Indeed I should have died if it hadn’t been for the girl Maiwa, who nursed me by stealth. He got that accursed lion trap among our things also, and I suppose he has tortured between one and two hundred people to death in it. It was his favourite amusement, and he would go every day and sit and watch his victim till he died. Sometimes he would give him food and water to keep him alive longer, telling him or her that he would let him go if he lived till a certain day. But he never did let them go. They all died there, and I could show you their bones behind that rock.’

===Chinese===
“‘你在这地方当了几年囚徒，埃弗里？’我问。

“‘六年挂零，夸特梅因；零头的月份，近来我已经数不清了。我是同阿尔迪（Aldey）少校、另外三位先生和四十名挑夫上这儿来的。万贝那个魔鬼打了我们一记埋伏，为了抢枪，把那些人全杀了。枪到了他手里也没多大用处——都是后膛枪，那帮蠢货一两个月就把弹药打了个精光。不过枪都好好的，就挂在那边棚屋里。他们没杀我，是因为动手之前，他们里有个人见我在修一支枪，便把我留下，当个修枪匠使唤。我逃过两回，两回都叫逮了回来。上一回，万贝差点把我鞭打死——我背上的疤你看得见。说实在的，要不是迈瓦那姑娘偷偷护理我，我早没命了。那架可恶的狮阱，也是从我们的行装里抄了去的；我估摸他前前后后用它把一两百人活活折磨死了。那是他顶心爱的消遣，他每天都要去坐在那儿，看着受难的人咽气。有时他还给他送吃送喝，好叫他多撑几天，对他说——或者说对她说——只要活到某月某日，就放他走。可他一个也没放过。他们都死在那里，那块岩石后头的骸骨，我可以带你去认。’

===Original===
“ ‘The devil!’ I said, grinding my teeth. ‘I wish I hadn’t interfered; I wish I had left him to the same fate.’

“ ‘Well, he got a taste of it anyway,’ said Every; ‘I’m glad he got a taste. There’s justice in it, and now he’s gone to hell, and I hope there is another one ready for him there. By Jove! I should like to have the setting of it.’

“And so he talked on, and I sat and listened to him, wondering how he had kept his reason for so many years. But he didn’t talk as I have told it, in plain English. He spoke very slowly, and as though he had got something in his mouth, continually using native words because the English ones had slipped his memory.

===Chinese===
“‘魔鬼！’我咬牙切齿地说，‘我后悔不该多这个手；真该把他留在那儿，叫他受同样的死法。’

“‘哼，反正他尝着滋味了，’埃弗里说，‘他尝着滋味，我高兴。这里头有天理。如今他下了地狱，我盼着那边再给他备下一个。老天作证！那边的机关，我倒愿意亲手去安。’

“他就这么滔滔不绝讲下去，我坐在旁边听，心里纳罕，这些年他怎么竟没有失心疯。只是他讲话，并不像我转述的这样是流畅的英语。他说得极慢，嘴里像含着什么东西，又不住夹着土话，因为英语词早从他记忆里溜走了。

===Original===
“At last Nala came up and told us that food was made ready, and thankful enough we were to get it, I can tell you. After we had eaten we held a consultation. Quite a thousand of Wambe’s soldiers were put hors de combat, but at least two thousand remained hidden in the bush and rocks, and these men, together with those in the outlying kraals, were a source of possible danger. The question arose, therefore, what was to be done﻿—were they to be followed or left alone? I waited till everybody had spoken, some giving one opinion and some another, and then being appealed to I gave mine. It was to the effect that Nala should take a leaf out of the great Zulu T’Chaka’s book, and incorporate the tribe, not destroy it. We had a good many women among the prisoners. Let them, I suggested, be sent to the hiding-places of the soldiers and make an offer. If the men would come and lay down their arms and declare allegiance to Nala, they and their town and cattle should be spared. Wambe’s cattle alone would be seized as the prize of war. Moreover, Wambe having left no children, his wife Maiwa should be declared chieftainess of the tribe, under Nala. If they did not accept this offer by the morning of the second day it should be taken as a declaration that they wished to continue the war. Their town should be burned, their cattle, which our men were already collecting and driving in in great numbers, would be taken, and they should be hunted down.

“This advice was at once declared to be wise, and acted on. The women were despatched, and I saw from their faces that they never expected to get such terms, and did not think that their mission would be in vain. Nevertheless, we spent that afternoon in preparations against possible surprise, and also in collecting all the wounded of both parties into a hospital, which we extemporized out of some huts, and there attending to them as best we could.

===Chinese===
“末了纳拉过来告诉我们，饭食已经备好。不瞒你们说，这一顿我们吃得感激不尽。饭后我们商议军情。万贝的士兵足足有一千人失了战斗力，但至少还有两千人躲在灌木丛和岩石之间，这些人连同外围各克拉的人众，是个隐忧。于是问题来了：怎么办——是追击，还是由他们去？我等众人都说完了——这个一主张，那个一主张——末了问到头上，我也说了我的主张。大意是：纳拉不妨学学伟大的祖鲁王恰卡（T'Chaka）的成法，收编这个部族，而不要毁掉它。俘虏里有的是妇人。我建议派她们到士兵们藏身的地方去开条件：男人们只要肯来缴械投诚、宣誓效忠纳拉，他们的人丁、市镇和牛群一概保全；只没收万贝的牛，充作战争的犒赏。再者，万贝既然没有子嗣，他的妻子迈瓦便当立为这一族的女酋长，位居纳拉之下。若到第二天早晨还不接受这些条款，便算他们宣告再战：他们的市镇要烧，牛群要没收——我们的人已在成群地把牛赶拢来——人也要被穷追到底。

“这番主张当场公认为高见，随即施行。妇人们被打发出去了；我看她们的脸色便知，她们做梦也没想到能得这样的条件，并不疑心此行会白跑。尽管如此，那天下午我们仍做了防备偷袭的部署，又把两军的伤员统统收进一座医院——拿几座棚屋临时改的——在那里尽力救治。

===Original===
“That evening Every had the first pipe of tobacco that he had tasted for six years. Poor fellow, he nearly cried with joy over it. The night passed without any sign of attack, and on the following morning we began to see the effect of our message, for women, children, and a few men came in in little knots, and took possession of their huts. It was of course rather difficult to prevent our men from looting, and generally going on as natives, and for the matter of that white men too, are in the habit of doing after a victory. But one man who after warning was caught maltreating a woman was brought out and killed by Nala’s order, and though there was a little grumbling, that put a stop to further trouble.

“On the second morning the head men and numbers of their followers came in in groups, and about midday a deputation of the former presented themselves before us without their weapons. They were conquered, they said, and Wambe was dead, so they came to hear the words of the great lion who had eaten them up, and of the crafty white man, the jackal, who had dug a hole for them to fall in, and of Maiwa, Lady of War, who had led the charge and turned the fate of the battle.

“So we let them hear the words, and when we had done an old man rose and said, that in the name of the people he accepted the yoke that was laid upon their shoulders, and that the more gladly because even the rule of a woman could not be worse than the rule of Wambe. Moreover, they knew Maiwa, the Lady of War, and feared her not, though she was a witch and terrible to see in battle.

===Chinese===
“当天晚上，埃弗里抽上了六年来的头一斗烟。可怜的人，他险些为这一斗烟喜极而泣。一夜过去，毫无来犯的迹象；第二天早晨，我们的招抚开始见效：妇人孩子和少数男人三三两两进得寨来，各归各屋。要拦住我们的人不趁胜掳掠——土人打了胜仗向来是这一套，白人也好不到哪里去——自然颇费些周章。但有一个人，受了警告之后仍被拿住虐待一名妇人，便被拉出来，由纳拉下令处死。虽说底下有些嘀咕，乱子也就此绝了迹。

“第二天早晨，头人们领着成群的部属到了；晌午时分，前者推出一个代表团，不带兵器，来到我们面前。他们说，他们败了，万贝死了，所以前来听凭发落——听那头把他们吞噬殆尽的雄狮发落，听那给他们掘下陷坑的狡黠白人豺狼发落，听那位冲锋陷阵、扭转了战局乾坤的‘战争夫人’迈瓦发落。

“于是我们把话向他们讲明。话毕，一位老者起身，说他以族人之名，接受压在他们肩上的轭，而且甘愿之至，因为纵然是女人当政，也断断坏不过万贝的统治；况且他们识得迈瓦这位‘战争夫人’，并不怕她，尽管她是个巫女，战阵之上狰狞可怖。

===Original===
“Then Nala asked his daughter if she was willing to become chieftainess of the tribe under him.

“Maiwa, who had been very silent since her revenge was accomplished, answered yes, that she was, and that her rule should be good and gentle to those who were good and gentle to her, but the froward and rebellious she would smite with a rod of iron; which from my knowledge of her character I thought exceedingly probable.

“The head man replied that that was a good saying, and they did not complain at it, and so the meeting ended.

===Chinese===
“随后纳拉问女儿，愿不愿在他之下做这一族的女酋长。

“迈瓦自从大仇得报，一直缄默寡言，这时答说愿意；又说她的治道，必宽仁温厚以待善待她的人，至于桀骜悖逆之徒，她要以铁杖痛击——以我对她性情的了解，我想这话十有八九要应验。

“那头人回说，这话在理，他们并无怨言。会盟就此而散。

===Original===
“Next day we spent in preparations for departure. Mine consisted chiefly in superintending the digging up of the stockade of ivory tusks, which I did with the greatest satisfaction. There were some five hundred of them altogether. I made inquiries about it from Every, who told me that the stockade had been there so long that nobody seemed to know exactly who had collected the tusks originally. There was, however, a kind of superstitious feeling about them which had always prevented the chiefs from trying to sell this great mass of ivory. Every and I examined it carefully, and found that although it was so old its quality was really as good as ever, and there was very little soft ivory in the lot. At first I was rather afraid lest, now that my services had been rendered, Nala should hesitate to part with so much valuable property, but this was not the case. When I spoke to him on the subject he merely said, ‘Take it, Macumazahn, take it; you have earned it well,’ and, to speak the truth, though I say it who shouldn’t, I think I had. So we pressed several hundred Matuku bearers into our service, and next day marched off with the lot.

“Before we went I took a formal farewell of Maiwa, whom we left with a bodyguard of three hundred men to assist her in settling the country. She gave me her hand to kiss in a queenly sort of way, and then said,

===Chinese===
“第二天我们做出发的准备。我这一份，主要是督着人把那道象牙栅起出来，这活儿我干得有滋有味。象牙统共五百来支。我向埃弗里打听它的来历，他说那道栅立在那儿的年头太久，如今竟像没人说得清，最初是谁攒下的这些牙。倒是有一种迷信附着在这批牙上，历任酋长谁也不敢把这大宗象牙拿去发卖。埃弗里和我仔细验看，发现这批牙虽古，成色竟一如既往地好，里头软牙极少。起初我还提心吊胆，唯恐如今差事已经办完，纳拉会舍不得放手这许多值钱的物事。谁知全无此事。我向他提起，他只说：‘拿去吧，马库马赞，拿去吧；这是你挣下的。’说句老实话——虽说不该自己讲——我想也确是如此。于是我们征了几百名马图库挑夫当差，第二天便押着全部象牙开拔了。

“临行之前，我向迈瓦正式辞行。我们把她留下，拨了三百人做卫队，助她安顿这地方。她以一派王后的气度伸手给我吻了，随后说道，

===Original===
“ ‘Macumazahn, you are a brave man, and have been a friend to me in my need. If ever you want help or shelter, remember that Maiwa has a good memory for friend and foe. All I have is yours.

“And so I thanked her and went. She was certainly a very remarkable woman. A year or two ago I heard that her father Nala was dead, and that she had succeeded to the chieftainship of both tribes, which she ruled with great justice and firmness.

“I can assure you that we ascended the pass leading to Wambe’s town with feelings very different from those with which we had descended it a few days before. But if I was grateful for the issue of events, you can easily imagine what poor Every’s feelings were. When we got to the top of the pass, before the whole impi he actually flopped down upon his knees and thanked Heaven for his escape, the tears running down his face. But then, as I have said, his nerves were shaken﻿—though now that his beard was trimmed and he had some sort of clothes on his back, and hope in his heart, he looked a very different man from the poor wretch whom we had rescued from death by torture.

===Chinese===
“‘马库马赞，你是个勇士，在我危难之际做了我的朋友。往后你若需人相助、寻个庇身之所，请记得迈瓦对朋友和仇敌都记性极好。我所有的一切，都是你的。’

“我谢过她，便动身走了。她委实是位了不起的女人。一两年前我听说，她父亲纳拉去世了，她承袭了两个部族的酋长之位，治得极为公正严明。

“我敢担保，我们重新登上通往万贝镇子的那道山隘时，心情同几天前下山时已有天渊之别。不过，我若为事情的结局感激，那么可怜的埃弗里是什么心情，你们可想而知。到了隘口顶上，当着全战队的面，他扑通双膝跪倒，感谢上天使他逃出生天，眼泪顺着脸颊直淌。不过我说过，他的神经震伤未复——如今胡子修齐了，背上也有了件像样的衣裳，心里有了指望，同我们从酷刑死刑里救出的那个可怜虫相比，已是判若两人。

===Original===
“Well, we separated from Nala at the little stairway or pass over the mountain﻿—Every and I and the ivory going down the river which I had come up a few weeks before, and the chief returning to his own kraal on the further side of the mountain. He gave us an escort of a hundred and fifty men, however, with instructions to accompany us for six days’ journey, and to keep the Matuku bearers in order and then return. I knew that in six days we should be able to reach a district where porters were plentiful, and whence we could easily get the ivory conveyed to Delagoa Bay.”

“And did you land it up safe?” I asked.

“Well no,” said Quatermain, “we lost about a third of it in crossing a river. A flood came down suddenly just as the men were crossing and many of them had to throw down their tusks to save their lives. We had no means of dragging it up, and so we were obliged to leave it, which was very sad. However, we sold what remained for nearly seven thousand pounds, so we did not do so badly. I don’t mean that I got seven thousand pounds out of it, because, you see, I insisted upon Every taking a half share. Poor fellow, he had earned it, if ever a man did. He set up a store in the old colony on the proceeds and did uncommonly well.”

===Chinese===
“后来，我们在山间那道阶梯般的窄隘口同纳拉分手——埃弗里、我和象牙顺流而下，走我来时溯上的那条河；酋长自己翻过山去，回他山那边的克拉尔。不过他拨了一百五十人护送我们，嘱咐他们随行六天的路程，管束好马图库挑夫，然后回去。我知道，六天之内我们便能走到一处挑夫充足的地方，从那里不难雇人把象牙运到德拉瓜湾。”

“‘象牙平安运到了吗？’我问。

“‘唉，没有，’夸特梅因说，‘过河的时候损失了约莫三分之一。人正过河，山洪突然下来，许多人只得扔下象牙保命。我们没有办法把牙捞上来，只好由它去，伤心是真伤心。不过剩下的卖了将近七千镑，所以也不算栽。倒不是说七千镑全进了我的腰包——你们晓得，我硬叫埃弗里拿了一半去。可怜人，天下若有人挣得这份钱，就是他挣的。他拿这笔钱在老殖民地开了家铺子，生意兴旺得很。”

===Original===
“And what did you do with the lion trap?” asked Sir Henry.
