“Oh, I brought that away with me also, and when I reached Durban I put it in my house. But really I could not bear to sit opposite to it at nights as I smoked. Visions of that poor woman and the hand of her dead child would rise up in my mind, and also of all the horrors of which it had been the instrument. I began to dream at last that it held me by the leg. This was too much for my nerves, so I just packed it up and shipped it to its maker in England, whose name was stamped upon the steel, sending him a letter at the same time to tell him to what purpose the infernal machine had been put. I believe that he gave it to some museum or other.”

“And what became of the tusks of the three bulls which you shot! You must have left them at Nala’s kraal, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
“‘那那个狮阱，你又是怎么处置的？’亨利爵士问。

“‘噢，那个我也一并带了回来，到德班就摆在我屋里。可是夜里坐着抽烟，同它面面相对，实在消受不起。那可怜的女人、她死去孩子的那只手，还有它充过工具的那一切惨事，总要浮上我心头。到后来，我开始梦见它咬住我的腿。我的神经可吃不住了，索性把它打好包，寄还给英国造它的匠人——钢材上打着他的名号——又附了一封信，告诉他这台地狱机器派过什么用场。我记得他后来把它送给哪家博物馆去了。”

“‘那你打的那三头公象的象牙呢！我琢磨着，准是撂在纳拉的克拉尔了。”

===Original===
The old gentleman’s face fell at this question.

“Ah,” he said, “that is a very sad story. Nala promised to send them with my goods to my agent at Delagoa, and so he did. But the men who brought them were unarmed, and, as it happened, they fell in with a slave caravan under the command of a half-bred Portuguese, who seized the tusks, and what is worse, swore that he had shot them. I paid him out afterwards, however,” he added with a smile of satisfaction, “but it did not give me back my tusks, which no doubt have been turned into hair brushes long ago;” and he sighed.

“Well,” said Good, “that is a capital yarn of yours, Quatermain, but﻿—”

===Chinese===
老绅士听到这一问，脸上沉了下来。

“‘唉，’他说，‘那是个很伤心的故事。纳拉答应把它们连同我的货物，一并捎给我在德拉瓜湾的代理人，他也确实照办了。可押送的人没带枪，偏又撞上一支奴隶商队，领队的是个葡萄牙混血儿。那人夺了象牙还不算，更赌咒发誓说，象是他打的。不过，我后来到底收拾了他，’讲到这儿，他带着一脸得意的笑补了一句，‘可这换不回我的象牙；毫无疑问，它们早做成头发刷子了。’说罢他叹了口气。

“‘好了，’古德说，‘夸特梅因，你这故事讲得真是一流，可是——’

===Original===
“But what?” he asked sharply, foreseeing a draw.

“But I don’t think that it was so good as mine about the ibex﻿—it hasn’t the same *finish*.”

Mr. Quatermain made no reply. Good was beneath it.

===Chinese===
“‘可是什么？’他厉声问，料到对方又要把比赛弄成平局。

“‘可是我看它比不上我那篇北山羊的故事——少那么一点*收束*。’

夸特梅因先生没有搭腔。古德还不值他一驳。

===Original===
“Do you know, gentlemen,” he said, “it is half-past two in the morning, and if we are going to shoot the big wood tomorrow we ought to leave here at nine-thirty sharp.”

“Oh, if you shoot for a hundred years you will never beat the record of those three woodcocks,” I said.

“Or of those three elephants,” added Sir Henry.

And then we all went to bed, and I dreamed that I had married Maiwa, and was much afraid of that attractive but determined lady.

===Chinese===
“‘诸位可知，’他说，‘现在两点半了；明天要去大林子打猎，九点半就得动身。’

“‘哼，你就是再打一百年，也破不了那三只山鹬的纪录。’我说。

“‘还有那三头象的纪录。’亨利爵士补了一句。

于是我们各自就寝。我梦见自己娶了迈瓦，对那位又迷人又说一不二的夫人，心里怕得很。

===Original===
It may be well to state that the incident of the “Thing that bites” recorded in this tale is not an effort of the imagination. On the contrary, it is “plagiarized.” Mandara, a well-known chief on the east coast of Africa, has such an article, *and uses it*. In the same way the wicked conduct attributed to Wambe is not without a precedent. T’Chaka, the Zulu Napoleon, never allowed a child of his to live. Indeed he went further, for on discovering that his mother, Unandi, was bringing up one of his sons in secret, like Nero he killed her, and with his own hand.

===Chinese===
或许应当说明一句：本篇故事里所记的“咬人的东西”（Thing that bites）那桩事迹，并非想象的产物；恰恰相反，它是“抄袭”来的。非洲东海岸一位有名的酋长曼达拉（Mandara）就有这么一件东西，而且*当真在用它*。同样，安到万贝（Wambe）头上的种种恶行，也并非没有先例。祖鲁人的拿破仑恰卡（T’Chaka）从不让自己的孩子活在世上。他做得更绝：发现母亲乌南迪（Unandi）在暗地里抚养自己的一个儿子时，他像尼禄一样杀了她——而且是亲手动的。

## VII The Attack / 七 进攻

===Original===
“Just where we halted ran a little stream of water. I looked at it, and an idea struck me: probably there would be no water on the koppie. I suggested this to our captain, and, acting on the hint, he directed all the men to drink what they could, and also to fill the seven or eight cooking pots which we carried with us with water. Then came the crucial moment. How were we to get possession of the koppie? When the captain asked me, I said that I thought that we had better march up and take it, and this accordingly we went on to do. When we came to the narrow gateway we were, as I expected, stopped by two soldiers who stood on guard there and asked our business. The captain answered that we had changed our minds, and would follow on to Wambe’s kraal. The soldiers said no, we must now wait.

“To this we replied by pushing them to one side and marching in single file through the gateway, which was not distant more than a hundred yards from the koppie. While we were getting through, the men we had pushed away ran towards the town calling for assistance, a call that was promptly responded to, for in another minute we saw scores of armed men running hard in our direction. So we ran too, for the koppie. As soon as they understood what we were after, which they did not at first, owing to the dimness of the light, they did their best to get there before us. But we had the start of them, and with the exception of one unfortunate man who stumbled and fell, we were well on to the koppie before they arrived. This man they captured, and when fighting began on the following morning, and he refused to give any information, they killed him. Luckily they had no time to torture him, or they would certainly have done so, for these Matuku people are very fond of torturing their enemies.

“When we reached the koppie, the base of which covers about half an acre of ground, the soldiers who had been trying to cut us off halted, for they knew the strength of the position. This gave us a few minutes before the light had quite vanished to reconnoitre the place. We found that it was unoccupied, fortified with a regular labyrinth of stone walls, and contained three large caves and some smaller ones. The next business was to post the soldiers to such advantage as time would allow. My own men I was careful to place quite at the top. They were perfectly useless from terror, and I feared that they might try to escape and give information of our plans to Wambe. So I watched them like the apple of my eye, telling them that should they dare to stir they would be shot.

===Chinese===
“我们停脚的地方恰好淌着一条小溪。我看了一眼，心里闪过一个念头：石丘上多半没有水。我把这意思对队长一说，他心领神会，传令所有人能喝多少喝多少，又把我们随身带的七八口锅全灌满了水。接着便是紧要关头：我们怎样才能把石丘拿到手？队长问我时，我说依我看，最好干脆开上去夺下来，于是我们便这么办了。到了那道窄窄的寨门跟前，不出我所料，两名守卫拦住我们，问来意。队长答说我们改了主意，要继续赶去万贝的克拉尔。士兵说不行，现在就得等着。

“对这回答，我们的做法是把他们推到一边，鱼贯穿过寨门——门离石丘不过一百来码。我们穿门的时候，被推开的那两个朝城里跑去搬救兵，而救兵来得飞快：转眼间我们便看见几十名武装汉子朝我们这方向猛跑。于是我们也跑，朝石丘跑。起初因为天色昏暗，他们没闹清我们要干什么；等弄明白了，便拼命要抢在我们前头赶到。可我们占了先手，除一个倒霉蛋绊了一跤摔倒外，全员赶在他们到达之前上了石丘。那人被他们捉了去；第二天早晨战斗打响后，他不肯吐露半个字，他们便杀了他。还算走运，他们来不及拷问他，否则必不会放过——这些马图库（Matuku）人最爱折磨他们的敌人。

“我们登上石丘——丘基占地约半英亩——那些想截断我们的士兵便停住了，因为他们知道这地势的厉害。这给了我们几分钟，趁天光未灭把地方侦察了一遍。我们发现丘上无人，一道道石墙盘错成一座真正的迷宫，还有三个大山洞和几个小的。下一件事，是在时间容许的范围内把士兵们布置到最有利的位置。我自己的人，我特意安置在最顶上：他们早吓成一团废物，我又怕他们逃跑，把我们的计划泄漏给万贝。于是我拿他们当眼珠子一样盯着，放话说谁敢挪一挪就枪毙。

===Original===
“Then it grew quite dark, and presently out of the darkness I heard a voice—it was that of the leader of the soldiers who had escorted us—calling us to come down. We replied that it was too dark to move, we should hit our feet against the stones. He insisted upon our descending, and we flatly refused, saying that if any attempt was made to dislodge us we would fire. After that, as they had no real intention of attacking us in the dark, the men withdrew, but we saw from the fires which were lit around that they were keeping a strict watch upon our position.

“That night was a wearing one, for we never quite knew how the situation was going to develop. Fortunately we had some cooked food with us, so we did not starve. It was lucky, however, that we drunk our fill before coming up, for, as I had anticipated, there was not a drop of water on the koppie.

“At length the night wore away, and with the first tinge of light I began to go my rounds, and stumbling along the stony paths, to make things as ready as I could for the attack, which I felt sure would be delivered before we were two hours older. The men were cramped and cold, and consequently low-spirited, but I exhorted them to the best of my ability, bidding them remember the race from which they sprang, and not to show the white feather before a crowd of Matuku dogs. At length it began to grow light, and presently I saw long columns of men advancing towards the koppie. They halted under cover at a distance of about a hundred and fifty yards, and just as the dawn broke a herald came forward and called to us. Our captain stood up upon a rock and answered him.

===Chinese===
“随后天完全黑了。不多时，黑暗里传来一个声音——是护送我们的那队士兵的头领——喊我们下去。我们回答说天太黑走不得，脚要在石头上磕出伤来。他执意要我们下，我们断然拒绝，说谁想赶我们走，我们就开枪。此后，他们既本无意摸黑进攻，便退了下去；但从四下点起的火堆看得出，他们对我们的阵地守得极严。

“那一夜难熬，因为我们始终吃不准局势会怎么发展。幸好带着些熟食，总算没挨饿。不过，上山前喝了个饱实属万幸，因为正如我所料，石丘上一滴水也没有。

“夜终于熬了过去。天刚透出第一线光，我便开始巡营，深一脚浅一脚地在石头路上走，尽力把守御之事安排妥当——我拿得准，不出两个钟头，进攻就会来。士兵们蜷了一夜，又冷又僵，士气也就低落；我竭力鼓舞他们，叫他们别忘了自己出身的是什么种族，别在一群马图库狗面前当孬种。终于天亮起来了，不久我望见长长的队伍朝石丘开过来。他们在一百五十码外借着遮蔽停住；破晓时分，一名传令官上前喊话。我们的队长站上一块岩石，应答他。

===Original===
“‘These are the words of Wambe,’ the herald said. ‘Come forth from the koppie, and give over the evildoers, and go in peace, or stay in the koppie and be slain.’

“‘It is too early to come out as yet,’ answered our man in fine diplomatic style. ‘When the sun sucks up the mist then we will come out. Our limbs are stiff with cold.’

“‘Come forth even now,’ said the herald.

“‘Not if I know it, my boy,’ said I to myself; but the captain replied that he would come out when he thought proper, and not before.

“‘Then make ready to die,’ said the herald, for all the world like the villain of a transpontine piece, and majestically stalked back to the soldiers.

===Chinese===
“‘这是万贝的话，’传令官说。‘走出石丘，交出作恶的人，可保平安离去；不然留在丘上，便叫你们死。’

“‘现在出来还太早，’我们这位以出色的外交辞令答道。‘等太阳收了雾气，我们自会出来。我们的手脚都冻僵了。’

“‘现在就出来，’传令官说。

“‘想得美，小子，’我心里说；但队长答道，他自会挑个合适的时辰出来，早了免谈。

“‘那就准备受死吧，’传令官说，那做派活像通俗剧里的反派，大摇大摆地踱回本阵去了。

===Original===
“I made my final arrangements, and looked anxiously at the mountain crest a couple of miles or so away, from which the mist was now beginning to lift, but no column of smoke could I see. I whistled, for if the attacking force had been delayed or made any mistake, our position was likely to grow rather warm. We had barely enough water to wet the mouths of the men, and when once it was finished we could not hold the place for long in that burning heat.

“At length, just as the sun rose in glory over the heights behind us, the Matuku soldiers, of whom about fifteen hundred were now assembled, set up a queer whistling noise, which ended in a chant. Then some shots were fired, for the Matuku had a few guns, but without effect, though one bullet passed just by a man’s head.

===Chinese===
“我做了最后的部署，焦灼地望向两英里外的山脊——雾正从那儿开始散去——却望不见一柱烟。我打了个呼哨：倘若助攻的队伍耽搁了、或出了什么差错，我们这阵地怕要变得相当烫手。我们的水刚够润一润众人的嘴，一旦喝光，在那火烤般的酷热里是守不了多久的。

“终于，正当太阳壮丽地在我们身后的群峰上升起时，马图库士兵——如今已聚了约一千五百人——发出一种古怪的呼哨，哨声末了化作一阵战歌。接着放了几枪；马图库人也有几支枪，但毫无效果，只有一颗子弹擦着一个人的头飞了过去。

===Original===
“‘Now they are going to begin,’ I thought to myself, and I was not far wrong, for in another minute the body of men divided into three companies, each about five hundred strong, and, heralded by a running fire, charged at us on three sides. Our men were now all well under cover, and the fire did us no harm. I mounted on a rock so as to command a view of as much of the koppie and plain as possible, and yelled to our men to reserve their fire till I gave the word, and then to shoot low and load as quickly as possible. I knew that, like all natives, they were sure to be execrable shots, and that they were armed with weapons made out of old gas-pipes, so the only chance of doing execution was to let the enemy get right on to us.

“On they came with a rush; they were within eighty yards now, and as they drew near the point of attack, I observed that they closed their ranks, which was so much the better for us.

“‘Shall we not fire, my father?’ sung out the captain.

“‘No, confound you!’ I answered.

“‘Sixty yards—fifty—forty—thirty. Fire, you scoundrels!’ I yelled, setting the example by letting off both barrels of my elephant gun into the thickest part of the company opposite to me.

===Chinese===
“‘这就要开始了，’我心里想，而且想得不差：转眼间那群人分成三队，每队约五百人，在一阵行进射击的开路下，从三面向我们扑来。我们的人此刻都已藏得妥帖，枪火伤不着我们。我登上一块岩石，好尽量俯瞰石丘与平原的全貌，冲我们的人吼叫：等我口令再开火，然后要压低了打，装弹越快越好。我知道，他们同所有土人一样，枪法必然糟糕透顶，手里的家伙又是旧煤气管子改的，所以唯一能奏效的法子，就是放敌人一直欺到眼前再打。

“他们蜂拥而来；已进到八十码内了，临近攻击点时，我见他们收拢了队列——这对我们再好不过。

“‘我们还不开火么，我的父亲？’队长高声问。

“‘不开，你们这班该死的！’我答道。

“‘六十码——五十——四十——三十。开火，你们这班坏种！’我吼道，同时身先士卒，把猎象枪两个枪管的火力一齐送进对面那队人最密的地方。

===Original===
“Instantly the place rang out with the discharge of two hundred and odd guns, while the air was torn by the passage of every sort of missile, from iron pot legs down to slugs and pebbles coated with lead. The result was very prompt. The Matukus were so near that we could not miss them, and at thirty yards a lead-coated stone out of a gas-pipe is as effective as a Martini rifle, or more so. Over rolled the attacking soldiers by the dozen, while the survivors, fairly frightened, took to their heels. We plied them with shot till they were out of range—I made it very warm for them with the elephant gun, by the way—and then we loaded up in quite a cheerful frame of mind, for we had not lost a man, whereas I could count more than fifty dead and wounded Matukus. The only thing that damped my ardour was that, stare as I would, I could see no column of smoke upon the mountain crest.

“Half an hour elapsed before any further steps were taken against us. Then the attacking force adopted different tactics. Seeing that it was very risky to try to rush us in dense masses, they opened out into skirmishing order and ran across the open space in lots of five and six. As it happened, right at the foot of the koppie the ground broke away a little in such fashion that it was almost impossible for us to search it effectually with our fire. On the hither side of this dip Wambe’s soldiers were now congregating in considerable numbers. Of course we did them as much damage as we could while they were running across, but this sort of work requires good shots, and that was just what we had not got. Another thing was, that so many of our men would insist upon letting off the things they called guns at every little knot of the enemy that ran across. Thus, the first few lots were indeed practically swept away, but after that, as it took a long while to load the gas-pipes and old flint muskets, those who followed got across in comparative safety. For my own part, I fired away with the elephant gun and repeating carbine till they grew almost too hot to hold, but my individual efforts could do nothing to stop such a rush, or perceptibly to lessen the number of our enemies.

===Chinese===
“霎时间，两百多支枪一齐炸响，空中被各式各样的弹丸撕扯着——从铁锅腿直到铅弹和裹了铅的卵石。效果立竿见影。马图库人近得我们想打不中都难，而三十码上，一颗煤气管子射出的裹铅石头，杀伤力不亚于马蒂尼步枪，甚至犹有过之。攻来的士兵成打成打地翻滚倒地，没死的着实吓破了胆，掉头便逃。我们一路用弹丸送他们出射程——顺带一说，我那猎象枪可叫他们好好暖和了一阵——然后大家心境相当轻快地装弹，因为我们一人未折，而马图库人死伤的，我数得出五十开外。唯一叫我心头不踏实的，是我瞪眼望穿了山脊，仍看不见一柱烟。

“过了半个钟头，他们才有下一步动作。这回攻方换了战术：眼见密队冲锋风险太大，他们散开成散兵序列，五六个一组地跑过开阔地。不巧，石丘脚下恰好有一小片洼下去的地形，我们几乎无法用火力有效扫荡。在这洼地靠我们这一侧，万贝的士兵正大批聚集。他们跑过开阔地时，我们自然尽力杀伤，可这种打法要的是好枪法，而那恰恰是我们没有的。还有一层，我们这边总有人坚持对每一小股跑过的敌人都放他们所谓的枪。于是头几股确实几乎被扫光，可打那以后，煤气管子和老燧发枪装一趟弹要好半天，后头的人便相对平安地跑了过来。至于我自己，猎象枪和连发卡宾枪一路放个不停，直放到枪身烫得几乎握不住；但我一个人的努力，既拦不住这样的人潮，也叫敌人的数目见不了少。

===Original===
“At length there were at least a thousand men crowded into the dip of ground within a few yards of us, whence those of them who had guns kept up a continued fusillade upon the koppie. They killed two of my bearers in this way, and wounded a third, for being at the top of the koppie these men were most exposed to the fire from the dip at its base. Seeing that the situation was growing most serious, at length, by the dint of threats and entreaties, I persuaded the majority of our people to cease firing useless shots, to reload, and prepare for the rush. Scarcely had I done so when the enemy came for us with a roar. I am bound to say that I should never have believed that Matukus had it in them to make such a determined charge. A large party rushed round the base of the koppie, and attacked us in flank, while the others swarmed wherever they could get a foothold, so that we were taken on every side.

“‘*Fire!*’ I cried, and we did with terrible effect. Many of their men fell, but though we checked we could not stop them. They closed up and rushed the first fortification, killing a good number of its defenders. It was almost all cold steel work now, for we had no time to reload, and that suited the Butiana habits of fighting well enough, for the stabbing assegai is a weapon which they understand. Those of our people who escaped from the first line of walls took refuge in the second, where I stood myself, encouraging them, and there the fight raged fiercely. Occasionally parties of the enemy would force a passage, only to perish on the hither side beneath the Butiana spears. But still they kept it up, and I saw that, fight as we would, we were doomed. We were altogether outnumbered, and to make matters worse, fresh bodies of soldiers were pouring across the plain to the assistance of our assailants. So I made up my mind to direct a retreat into the caves, and there expire in a manner as heroic as circumstances would allow; and while mentally lamenting my hard fate and reflecting on my sins I fought away like a fiend. It was then, I remember, that I shot my friend the captain of our escort of the previous day. He had caught sight of me, and making a vicious dig at my stomach with a spear (which I successfully dodged), shouted out, or rather began to shout out, one of his unpleasant allusions to the ‘Thing that—’ He never got as far as ‘bites,’ because I shot him after ‘that.’

===Chinese===
“终于，至少一千人挤进了离我们只有几码的洼地，其中带枪的对准石丘不停施放。他们就这样打死了我两名挑夫，打伤第三名——这些人位于丘顶，正对着丘脚洼地里的火力，暴露得最厉害。眼看局势严重起来，末了我连吓带求，总算说动我们这边大多数人停止无效放枪，重新装弹，准备迎接冲锋。我这边刚安排停当，敌人便吼叫着扑了上来。我必须说，我本绝想不到马图库人竟有本事发起这般坚决的冲锋。一大股绕过丘脚攻我们侧翼，其余的哪里立得住脚就往哪里蜂拥，我们四面受敌。

“‘*开火！*’我喊道；我们开了，效果可怕。他们倒下许多人，但我们只能遏阻，不能止住。他们收拢队伍，冲进第一道工事，杀死了不少守军。眼下几乎全是白刃的活儿了，因为我们没有工夫装弹——而这倒颇合布蒂亚纳（Butiana）人的战法，捅刺的长矛正是他们使得烂熟的兵器。从第一线石墙逃出的人退进第二线，我便亲自立在那一线上给他们打气，战斗在那里打得火热。敌人不时有小股杀开一条通路，却只在墙这边丧命于布蒂亚纳的长矛之下。但他们仍不肯罢休，我看明白了：任我们怎样打，我们是注定了。人家以多凌寡，更糟的是，新到的队伍正源源越过平原来增援我们的攻击者。于是我打定主意，指挥众人退进山洞，在那里以环境所能容许的最像样的英雄姿态了此一生；一面在心里哀叹自己命苦、反省自己的罪愆，一面像个恶鬼一样厮杀。就在那时——我记得——我一枪打发了我们的朋友、前一天护送我们的那位队长。他瞧见了我，挺矛朝我的肚子狠狠一刺（我堪堪躲过），张口便喊，或者不如说刚开口要喊，他那句难听的‘那件咬人的东西——’。‘咬人的东西’几个字他永远没能说出口，因为刚说完‘那件’，我就开枪了。

===Original===
“Well, the game was about up. Already I saw one man throw down his spear in token of surrender—which act of cowardice cost him his life, by the way—when suddenly a shout arose.

“‘Look at the mountain,’ they cried; ‘there is an impi on the mountain side.’

“I glanced up, and there sure enough, about halfway down the mountain, nearing the first fortification, the long-plumed double line of Nala’s warriors was rushing down to battle, the bright light of the morning glancing on their spears. Afterwards we discovered that the reason of their delay was that they had been stopped by a river in flood, and could not reach the mountain crest by dawn. When they did reach it, however, they saw instantly that the fight was already going on, was ‘in flower,’ as they put it, and so advanced at once without waiting to light signal-fires.

===Chinese===
“唉，这局棋差不多到了终盘。我已经看见有人扔下长矛表示投降——顺带一说，这一怯懦举动送了他的命——就在这时，忽地响起一声呐喊。

“‘看山上，’他们喊道；‘山腰上有战队（impi）！’

“我抬眼一望，千真万确：纳拉（Nala）的战士们，那插着长羽饰的双列长阵，正从半山直冲而下，扑向第一道工事，清晨的亮光在他们的矛尖上闪烁。后来我们才知道，他们之所以来迟，是被一条涨水的河拦住，没能赶在破晓前到达山脊。待他们到达时，一眼看出战斗已经打响——用他们的话说，已经‘开花’了——便立即推进，不再等点信号火。

===Original===
“Meanwhile they had been observed from the town, and parties of soldiers were charging up the steep side of the hill, to occupy the *schanses*, and the second line of fortifications behind them. The first line they did not now attempt to reach or defend; Nala pressed them too close. But they got to the *schanses* or pits protected with stone walls, and constructed to hold from a dozen to twenty men, and soon began to open fire from them, and from isolated rocks. I turned my eyes to the gates of the town, which were placed to the north and south. Already they were crowded with hundreds of fugitive women and children flying to the rocks and caves for shelter from the foe.

“As for ourselves, the appearance of Nala’s impi produced a wonderful change for the better in our position. The soldiers attacking us turned, realizing that the town was being assailed from the rear, and clambering down the koppie streamed off to protect their homes against this new enemy. In five minutes there was not a man left except those who would move no more, or were too sorely wounded to escape. I felt inclined to ejaculate ‘*Saved!*’ like the gentleman in the play, but did not because the occasion was too serious. What I did do was to muster all the men and reckon up our losses. They amounted to fifty-one killed and wounded, sixteen men having been killed outright. Then I sent men with the cooking-pots to the stream of water, and we drank. This done I set my bearers, being the most useless part of the community, from a fighting point of view, to the task of attending the injured, and turned to watch the fray.

===Chinese===
“与此同时，城里也发现了他们，一队队士兵正向陡峭的山坡上冲，去占*散斯*（schanses，带石墙的坑垒）和它后面的第二道工事。第一道工事他们如今既不去夺也不去守了；纳拉逼得太近。但他们抢进了散斯——那些围着石墙、修来容纳十来个到二十人的坑——很快便从坑里和孤立的岩石后开了火。我又把目光转向城门——城门开在南、北两面——早已挤满了数以百计逃难的妇女儿童，他们正逃向岩石和山洞躲避敌人。

“至于我们自己，纳拉战队的出现使我们的处境大为改观。攻击我们的士兵掉头就走了——他们明白城池正被人从背后攻打——纷纷爬下石丘，川流而去，保卫各自的家小，对付这个新来的敌人。五分钟后，丘上除了再也动弹不了的、和伤重走不动的，一个不剩。我很想像戏文里那位先生一样喊一声‘*得救了！*’，但没喊，因为场合太严肃。我实际做的是集合所有人清点损失：死伤共五十一人，其中当场阵亡十六人。然后我派人提着锅去溪边取水，众人喝了。喝罢，我把挑夫们——从打仗的角度看，这是全员里最没用的一批——派去照看伤员，自己转过身观战。

===Original===
“By this time Nala’s impi had climbed the first line of fortifications without opposition, and was advancing in a long line upon the *schanses* or pits which were scattered about between it and the second line, singing a war chant as it came. Presently puffs of smoke began to start from the *schanses*, and with my glasses I could see several of our men falling over. Then as they came opposite a *schanse* that portion of the long line of warriors would thicken up and charge it with a wild rush. I could see them leap on to the walls and vanish into the depths beneath, some of their number falling backward on each occasion, shot or stabbed to death.

“Next would come another act in the tragedy. Out from the hither side of the *schanse* would pour such of its defenders as were left alive, perhaps three or four and perhaps a dozen, running for dear life, with the war dogs on their tracks. One by one they would be caught, then up flashed the great spear and down fell the pursued—dead. I saw ten of our men leap into one large *schanse*, but though I watched for some time nobody came out. Afterwards we inspected the place and found these men all dead, together with twenty-three Matukus. Neither side would give in, and they had fought it out to the bitter end.

===Chinese===
“这时，纳拉的战队已毫无阻挡地翻过第一道工事，排成长长的横列，向散布在它与第二道工事之间的那些散斯坑垒推进，一路唱着战歌。不久，散斯里升起一团团硝烟，我用望远镜看得见我们这边不时有人栽倒。每当长阵行到一座散斯对面，那一段战士便会密集起来，狂冲过去。我看见他们跳上墙头，消失在墙内的深处，每一回都有几个人仰面倒下，中弹的、挨矛的，非死即伤。

“接着便是这出惨剧的下一幕。散斯靠我们这一侧会涌出还活着的守军——或三四个，或十来个——没命地奔逃，身后战犬似的追兵咬定不放。他们会一个接一个被追上，大矛一闪，被追的应声倒地——死了。我看见我们十个人跳进一座大散斯，但我盯了半天，没有一个人出来。事后我们察看那地方，发现这十人全部战死，同他们躺在一起的还有二十三个马图库人。双方谁也不肯屈服，就这么血战到了最后一人。

===Original===
“At last they neared the second line of fortifications, behind which the whole remaining Matuku force, numbering some two thousand men, was rapidly assembling. One little pause to get their breath, and Nala’s men came at it with a rush and a long wild shout of ‘*Bulala Matuku*’ (kill the Matuku) that went right through me, thrilling every nerve. Then came an answering shout, and the sounds of heavy firing, and presently I saw our men retreating, somewhat fewer in numbers than they had advanced. Their welcome had been a warm one for the Matuku fight splendidly behind walls. This decided me that it was necessary to create a diversion; if we did not do so it seemed very probable that we should be worsted after all. I called to the captain of our little force, and rapidly put the position before him.

“Seeing the urgency of the occasion, he agreed with me that we must risk it, and in two minutes more, with the exception of my own men, whom I left to guard the wounded, we were trotting across the open space and through the deserted town towards the spot where the struggle was taking place, some seven hundred yards away. In six or eight minutes we reached a group of huts—it was a head man’s kraal, that was situated about a hundred and twenty yards behind the fortified wall, and took possession of it unobserved. The enemy was too much engaged with the foe in front of him to notice us, and besides, the broken ground rose in a hogback shape between. There we waited a minute or two and recovered our breath, while I gave my directions. So soon as we heard the Butiana impi begin to charge again, we were to run out in a line to the brow of the hogback and pour our fire into the mass of defenders behind the wall. Then the guns were to be thrown down and we must charge with the assegai. We had no shields, but that could not be helped; there would be no time to reload the guns, and it was absolutely necessary that the enemy should be disconcerted at the moment when the main attack was delivered.

“The men, who were as plucky a set of fellows as ever I saw, and whose blood was now thoroughly up, consented to this scheme, though I could see that they thought it rather a large order, as indeed I did myself. But I knew that if the impi was driven back a second time the game would be played, and for me at any rate it would be a case of the ‘Thing that bites,’ and this sure and certain knowledge filled my breast with valour.

===Chinese===
“终于，他们逼近了第二道工事；工事后面，马图库全军残部——约两千人——正迅速集结。略停一停喘口气，纳拉的人便一声悠长狂野的呐喊——‘*布拉拉·马图库*’（Bulala Matuku，杀马图库人）——冲了上去，那喊声穿透我的胸膛，根根神经都为之战栗。接着传来应战的呐喊和密集的枪声，不久我便看见我们的人退了下来，比冲上去时少了一些。他们受到的接待够热的——马图库人凭墙作战是极出色的。这使我认定，必须别开一场战局来牵制；否则到头来我们很可能会一败涂地。我唤来我们这支小小队伍的队长，把局势三言两语摆给他。

“他见事势急迫，同意我的看法：这险必须冒。两分钟后，除留下我自己的人看守伤员外，我们一路小跑穿过开阔地、穿过已无人迹的城，奔向七八百码外交战的地方。六八分钟后我们抵达一群茅屋——那是一处头人的克拉尔，坐落在工事墙后约一百二十码——神不知鬼不觉地占了它。敌人正忙于应付当面的死敌，无暇注意我们；何况中间还横着一道隆起如猪背的破碎坡地。我们在那儿歇了一两分钟缓气，我一面布置方略：一听见布蒂亚纳战队再度冲锋，我们便成一列跑上猪背脊的脊顶，把火力泼向墙后的大群守军；随后扔下枪，持矛冲锋。我们没有盾，那也无可奈何；届时没有重新装弹的工夫，而务必叫敌人在总攻落下的那一瞬乱掉阵脚。

“这班人是极有胆气，如今又个个热血沸腾，便答应了这套方案，虽然我看得出他们觉得这差事未免太重——说实话我自己也这么觉得。但我知道，战队若被第二次打退，全局就完了；而且无论如何，等待我的少不得是‘那件咬人的东西’——这一确定无疑的认识，把我的胸膛灌满了勇气。

===Original===
“We had not long to wait. Presently we heard the Butiana war-song swelling loud and long; they had commenced their attack. I made a sign, and the hundred and fifty men, headed by myself, poured out of the kraal, and getting into a rough line ran up the fifty or sixty yards of slope that intervened between ourselves and the crest of the hogbacked ridge. In thirty seconds we were there, and immediately beyond us was the main body of the Matuku host waiting the onslaught of the enemy with guns and spears. Even now they did not see us, so intent were they upon the coming attack. I signed to my men to take careful aim, and suddenly called out to them to fire, which they did with a will, dropping thirty or forty Matukus.

“‘*Charge!*’ I shouted, again throwing down my smoking rifle and drawing my revolver, an example which they followed, snatching up their spears from the ground where they had placed them while they fired. The men set up a savage whoop, and we started. I saw the Matuku soldiers wheel around in hundreds, utterly taken aback at this new development of the situation. And looking over them, before we had gone twenty yards I saw something else. For of a sudden, as though they had risen from the earth, there appeared above the wall hundreds of great spears, followed by hundreds of savage faces shadowed with drooping plumes. With a yell they sprang upon the wall shaking their broad shields, and with a yell they bounded from it straight into our astonished foes.

“*Crash!* we were in them now, and fighting like demons. *Crash!* from the other side. Nala’s impi was at its work, and still the spears and plumes appeared for a moment against the brown background of the mountain, and then sprang down and rushed like a storm upon the foe. The great mob of men turned this way and turned that way, astonished, bewildered, overborne by doubt and terror.

===Chinese===
“我们没等多久。不久便听见布蒂亚纳战歌昂扬起来，又长又亮；他们开始进攻了。我打个手势，一百五十名汉子随我涌出克拉尔，排成松散的一列，跑上横在我们与猪背脊顶之间的那五六十码坡。三十秒后我们到了脊顶，紧挨着我们面前的，便是马图库大军的主力，正持枪仗矛等候敌军来扑。直到这时他们还没看见我们——他们的心思全在即将到来的攻击上。我示意众人仔细瞄准，突然一声令下开火，他们痛痛快快地放了，放倒三四十个马图库人。

“‘*冲锋！*’我大喝一声，再次扔下冒烟的步枪，拔出左轮手枪；众人学我的样，从放枪时搁在地上的矛堆里抄起长矛。汉子们发出一声野性的呐喊，我们出发了。我看见成百的马图库士兵霍然回身，被局势这新的一变惊得目瞪口呆。而越过他们头顶望过去，还没跑出二十码，我又看见了另一番景象：忽然间，仿佛从地里冒出来一般，墙头上现出成百根大矛，矛后跟着成百张凶悍的脸，低垂的羽饰给脸投下阴影。他们呐喊着跳上墙头，摇动宽大的盾；又呐喊着从墙上直跃下去，跳进我们那些目瞪口呆的敌阵。

“*轰！*我们杀进敌阵，像恶魔一样厮杀。*轰！*另一面也响了。纳拉的战队开了工，矛尖和羽饰仍不断在山峦棕色的背景上闪现片刻，随即跃下，如暴风骤雨般扑向敌人。那一大群人转向这边、又转向那边，惊愕，惶惑，被疑惧压倒。

===Original===
“Meanwhile the slayers stayed not their hands, and on every side spears flashed, and the fierce shout of triumph went up to heaven. There too on the wall stood Maiwa, a white garment streaming from her shoulders, an assegai in her hand, her breast heaving, her eyes flashing. Above all the din of battle I could catch the tones of her clear voice as she urged the soldiers on to victory. But victory was not yet. Wambe’s soldiers gathered themselves together, and bore our men back by the sheer weight of numbers. They began to give, then once more they rallied, and the fight hung doubtfully.

“‘Slay, you war-whelps,’ cried Maiwa from the wall. ‘Are you afraid, you women, you chickenhearted women! Strike home, or die like dogs! What—you give way! Follow me, children of Nala.’ And with one long cry she leapt from the wall as leaps a stricken antelope, and holding the spear poised rushed right into the thickest of the fray. The warriors saw her, and raised such a shout that it echoed like thunder against the mountains. They massed together, and following the flutter of her white robe crashed into the dense heart of the foe. Down went the Matuku before them like trees before a whirlwind. Nothing could stand in the face of such a rush as that. It was as the rush of a torrent bursting its banks. All along their line swept the wild desperate charge; and there, straight in the forefront of the battle, still waved the white robe of Maiwa.

“Then they broke, and, stricken with utter panic, Wambe’s soldiers streamed away a scattered crowd of fugitives, while after them thundered the footfall of the victors.

===Chinese===
“与此同时，杀伐者手下不停。四面矛光闪烁，狂猛的凯歌直上云天。迈瓦（Maiwa）也立在那墙上，一袭白衣从肩头飘扬下来，手握一支长矛，胸口起伏，目光如电。在满场杀声之上，我仍能捕捉到她清亮的嗓音，听她激励士兵去夺取胜利。但胜利尚未到手。万贝的士兵重整旗鼓，凭人数的绝对优势把我们的战士压了回来。我们的战士开始退让，随即又稳住阵脚，战局悬而未决。

“‘杀呀，你们这群战争崽子，’迈瓦在墙上喊道。‘你们怕了么，你们这些娘们，你们这些胆小如鸡的娘们！往死里刺，不然就像狗一样死去！怎么——你们竟退了！跟我来，纳拉的孩子们。’随着一声长啸，她跃下墙来，如一只中箭的羚羊，挺着蓄势待发的长矛，直冲进战况最密的核心。战士们望见了她，爆发出一声呐喊，如雷般撞在群山上。他们聚拢成团，追着那袭白袍的翻飞，撞进敌阵最稠密的心脏。马图库人在他们面前纷纷倒下，如旋风前的树木。那样的冲锋面前，没有什么站得住。那好比决堤的洪流。狂猛决绝的冲击横扫了他们整条战线；而在那里，在战场的最前锋，迈瓦的白袍仍在飘扬。

“随后他们崩溃了。万贝的士兵被彻骨的恐慌击中，化作一股四散的逃难人流涌了出去，而胜利者的脚步声如雷鸣般追在他们身后。

===Original===
“The fight was over, we had won the day; and for my part I sat down upon a stone and wiped my forehead, thanking Providence that I had lived to see the end of it. Twenty minutes later Nala’s warriors began to return panting. ‘Wambe’s soldiers had taken to the bush and the caves,’ they said, ‘where they had not thought it safe to follow them,’ adding significantly, that many had stopped on the way.

“I was utterly dazed, and now that the fight was over my energy seemed to have left me, and I did not pay much attention, till presently I was aroused by somebody calling me by my name. I looked up, and saw that it was the chief Nala himself, who was bleeding from a flesh wound in his arm. By his side stood Maiwa panting, but unhurt, and wearing on her face a proud and terrifying air.

“‘They are gone, Macumazahn,’ said the chief; ‘there is little to fear from them, their heart is broken. But where is Wambe the chief?—and where is the white man thou camest to save?’

===Chinese===
“战斗结束了，我们大获全胜；我呢，在一块石头上坐下，擦了擦额头，感谢上天让我活着看到了结局。二十分钟后，纳拉的战士们喘着气回来了。‘万贝的兵钻了灌木丛和山洞，’他们说，‘跟进去怕不安全’；又意味深长地补了一句：许多人半路上就‘停下了’。

“我整个人都懵了。战斗既毕，我的气力仿佛也随之而去，心不在焉，直到有人唤我的名字，我才回过神来。我抬头一看，是纳拉酋长本人，臂上一处皮肉伤还在淌血。他身边立着喘息未定的迈瓦，毫发无伤，脸上带着一种骄傲而又可畏的神情。

“‘他们跑了，马库马赞（Macumazahn），’酋长说；‘他们成不了什么气候，他们的心已经碎了。可是万贝酋长在哪里？——你又来找救的那个白人在哪里？’

===Original===
“‘I know not,’ I answered.

“Close to where we stood lay a Matuku, a young man who had been shot through the fleshy part of the calf. It was a trifling wound, but it prevented him from running away.

“‘Say, thou dog,’ said Nala, stalking up to him and shaking his red spear in his face, ‘say, where is Wambe? Speak, or I slay thee. Was he with the soldiers?’

===Chinese===
“‘我不知道，’我答道。

“离我们站的地方不远躺着一个马图库人，是个小伙子，子弹穿过了他小腿的肉。伤不重，却叫他跑不成了。

“‘说，你这狗，’纳拉大步走到他跟前，红矛直晃到他脸上，‘说，万贝在哪里？开口，不然我宰了你。他跟兵在一起么？’

===Original===
“‘Nay, lord, I know not,’ groaned the terrified man, ‘he fought not with us; Wambe has no stomach for fighting. Perchance he is in his kraal yonder, or in the cave behind the kraal,’ and he pointed to a small enclosure on the hillside, about four hundred yards to the right of where we were.

“‘Let us go and see,’ said Nala, summoning his soldiers.”

===Chinese===
“‘不，主上，我不知道，’那吓坏的人哼哼道，‘他没跟我们打；万贝没有打仗的胃口。他多半在他那边的克拉尔里，或者在克拉尔后面的山洞里，’说着他指向山坡上一处小小的围院，在我们所立之处右侧约四百码。

“‘我们去看看，’纳拉说着，召集起他的战士。”

## III The First Round / 三 第一回合

===Original===
“After this, as it was now midday, and I had killed enough meat, we marched back triumphantly to camp, where I proceeded to concoct a stew of buffalo beef and compressed vegetables. When this was ready we ate the stew, and then I took a nap. About four o’clock, however, Gobo woke me up, and told me that the head man of one of Wambe’s kraals had arrived to see me. I ordered him to be brought up, and presently he came, a little, wizened, talkative old man, with a waistcloth round his middle, and a greasy, frayed kaross made of the skins of rock rabbits over his shoulders.

“I told him to sit down, and then abused him roundly. ‘What did he mean,’ I asked, ‘by disturbing me in this rude way? How did he dare to cause a person of my quality and evident importance to be awakened in order to interview his entirely contemptible self?’

“I spoke thus because I knew that it would produce an impression on him. Nobody, except a really great man, he would argue, would dare to speak to him in that fashion. Most savages are desperate bullies at heart, and look on insolence as a sign of power.

===Chinese===
“这事过后，时已正午，肉也打得够了，我们凯旋般开回营地，我便着手用牛肉和压缩蔬菜炖一锅菜。炖好之后我们吃了，随后我打了个盹。可是约莫四点钟，戈博把我叫醒，说万贝的一处克拉尔（kraal，班图人的村寨）的头人求见。我吩咐带他上来，不多时他就来了：一个小小的、干瘪的、话多的老头，腰间围一块腰布，肩上披一件油腻破烂的卡罗斯（kaross，兽皮披肩）——岩兔皮缝的。

“我叫他坐下，接着便痛痛快快把他骂了一顿。‘你是什么意思，’我问，‘这般无礼地搅扰我？你怎么敢为了会你这么个不值一提的货色，叫醒我这等身份、这等显而易见的重要人物？’

“我这样讲，因为我知道这会在他身上产生印象。他自会盘算：除非是真正的大人物，谁敢用这种口气同他说话。大多数野人骨子里都是十足的恶霸，把无礼看作权势的标记。

===Original===
“The old man instantly collapsed. He was utterly overcome, he said; his heart was split in two, and well realized the extent of his misbehaviour. But the occasion was very urgent. He heard that a mighty hunter was in the neighbourhood, a beautiful white man, how beautiful he could not have imagined had he not seen (this to me!), and he came to beg his assistance. The truth was, that three bull elephants such as no man ever saw had for years been the terror of their kraal, which was but a small place—a cattle kraal of the great chief Wambe’s, where they lived to keep the cattle. And now of late these elephants had done them much damage; but last night they had destroyed a whole patch of mealie land, and he feared that if they came back they would all starve next season for want of food. Would the mighty white man then be pleased to come and kill the elephants? It would be easy for him to do—oh, most easy! It was only necessary that he should hide himself in a tree, for there was a full moon, and then when the elephants appeared he would speak to them with the gun, and they would fall down dead, and there would be an end of their troubling.

“Of course I hummed and hawed, and made a great favour of consenting to his proposal, though really I was delighted to have such a chance. One of the conditions that I made was that a messenger should at once be despatched to Wambe, whose kraal was two days’ journey from where I was, telling him that I proposed to come and pay my respects to him in a few days, and to ask his formal permission to shoot in his country. Also I intimated that I was prepared to present him with *hongo*, that is, blackmail, and that I hoped to do a little trade with him in ivory, of which I heard he had a great quantity.

===Chinese===
“老头当即垮了下来。他说他完全折服了；他的心裂成了两半，深深明白自己罪过的分量。但事情实在紧急。他听说左近来了一位了不起的猎手，一位俊美的白人——不亲眼看见，简直无法想象有那么俊美（这话是对我说的！）——他是来恳求援手的。实情是：三头谁也没见过的公象，多年来一直是他们那座村寨的祸根。那不过是个小地方——大酋长万贝的一处牧牛寨，他们住在那里替他看牛。近来这些象屡屡糟害他们；昨夜里更毁掉整片玉米地，他生怕它们再来，来年青黄不接，全寨都要挨饿。那么，尊贵的白人大爷可肯赏光去把象打死？这对他是轻而易举的——啊，再容易不过！只消他藏到一棵树上，因为月圆，等象来了，他拿枪同它们说句话，它们便会应声倒毙，从此祸患永绝。

“我自然哼哼哈哈，摆足了架子才肯俯允所请，其实心里为有这等机会欢喜不迭。我提的条件之一，是即刻派一名信使去见万贝——他的克拉尔离我这里两天路程——告诉他过几天我打算登门拜望，并正式请求在他境内打猎的许可。我还示意，我准备奉上*洪戈*（hongo），也就是买路钱；又说希望能同他做一点象牙生意，听说他手里存货不少。

===Original===
“This message the old gentleman promised to despatch at once, though there was something about his manner which showed me that he was doubtful as to how it would be received. After that we struck our camp and moved on to the kraal, which we reached about an hour before sunset. This kraal was a collection of huts surrounded by a slight thorn-fence, perhaps there were ten of them in all. It was situated in a kloof of the mountain down which a rivulet flowed. The kloof was densely wooded, but for some distance above the kraal it was free from bush, and here on the rich deep ground brought down by the rivulet were the cultivated lands, in extent somewhere about twenty or twenty-five acres. On the kraal side of these lands stood a single hut, that served for a mealie store, which at the moment was used as a dwelling-place by an old woman, the first wife of our friend the head man.

“It appears that this lady, having had some difference of opinion with her husband about the extent of authority allowed to a younger and more amiable wife, had refused to dwell in the kraal any more, and, by way of marking her displeasure, had taken up her abode among the mealies. As the issue will show, she was, it happened, cutting off her nose to spite her face.

“Close by this hut grew a large baobab tree. A glance at the mealie grounds showed me that the old head man had not exaggerated the mischief done by the elephants to his crops, which were now getting ripe. Nearly half of the entire patch was destroyed. The great brutes had eaten all they could, and the rest they had trampled down. I went up to their spoor and started back in amazement—never had I seen such a spoor before. It was simply enormous, more especially that of one old bull, that carried, so said the natives, but a single tusk. One might have used any of the footprints for a hip-bath.

===Chinese===
“老先生答应即刻把口信发出去，只是他那做派里有点什么，让我看出发信的人对它会被如何接下心存疑虑。此后我们拔营，开到那座村寨去，日落前约一小时到了。这村寨是一群茅屋，围着一道单薄的荆棘篱，统共约有十座。它坐落在山间一道峡谷（kloof）里，一条小溪顺谷而下。峡谷里林木茂密，但在村寨上方一段却无灌木，就在这溪水冲积出的深厚沃土上，是他们的耕地，面积约二十到二十五英亩。耕地靠寨子的一侧立着一座孤零零的茅屋，原是存放玉米的仓房，眼下却住着一位老妇人——我们那位头人朋友的大老婆。

“原来这位夫人，为着丈夫许给一位更年轻也更和善的老婆多大的权柄，同他生了一场意见，从此不肯再住在寨里，为表不悦，索性搬到玉米中间住了下来。而后来的结果将表明，她这一着，恰是割了鼻子跟自己的脸过不去。

“茅屋近旁长着一棵高大的猴面包树。朝玉米地一望，我便知道老头人并没有夸大象群对他庄稼——庄稼正待成熟——的祸害：整片地毁掉了将近一半。那些巨兽吃饱了能吃的，余下的全踩倒了。我走到它们脚印前，惊得倒退——从未见过这样的蹄印：简直大得吓人，尤其是一头老公象的，土人说它只长一根牙。随便哪个脚印，都够拿来当坐浴盆使。

===Original===
“Having taken stock of the position, my next step was to make arrangements for the fray. The three bulls, according to the natives, had been spoored into the dense patch of bush above the kloof. Now it seemed to me very probable that they would return tonight to feed on the remainder of the ripening mealies. If so, there was a bright moon, and it struck me that by the exercise of a little ingenuity I might bag one or more of them without exposing myself to any risk, which, having the highest respect for the aggressive powers of bull elephants, was a great consideration to me.

“This then was my plan. To the right of the huts as you look up the kloof, and commanding the mealie lands, stands the baobab tree that I have mentioned. Into that baobab tree I made up my mind to go. Then if the elephants appeared I should get a shot at them. I announced my intentions to the head man of the kraal, who was delighted. ‘Now,’ he said, ‘his people might sleep in peace, for while the mighty white hunter sat aloft like a spirit watching over the welfare of his kraal what was there to fear?’

“I told him that he was an ungrateful brute to think of sleeping in peace while, perched like a wounded vulture on a tree, I watched for his welfare in wakeful sorrow; and once more he collapsed, and owned that my words were ‘sharp but just.’

===Chinese===
“把形势掂量过后，下一步便是布置这一场较量。照土人的说法，三头公象的踪迹已进了峡谷上方那片密灌木。我看它们今夜多半还要回来，吃那剩下的将熟的玉米。倘若如此，月色正好，我盘算着只消略施小计，便可收拾它们一头或几头，而不必自己冒什么风险——我对公象的攻击本领素来怀有最高的敬意，这一点对我可不是小事。

“我的计划是这样的：顺峡谷往上看，在茅屋右方、俯临玉米地的地方，立着我方才说的那棵猴面包树。我拿定主意就上那棵树去。这样象若来到，我便有枪可开。我把打算对寨里头人一说，他喜出望外。‘这下，’他说，‘他的人民可以安安稳稳睡觉了，因为尊贵的白人猎手高高在上，像神灵一样守护着他的村寨的福祉，还有什么可怕的呢？’

“我对他说，他真是个没良心的畜生：我在树上像一只负伤的秃鹫那样栖着，为他的福祉忧醒不寐，他倒想着安睡；于是他又一次垮了下去，承认我的话‘厉害，然而公道’。

===Original===
“However, as I have said, confidence was completely restored; and that evening everybody in the kraal, including the superannuated victim of jealousy in the little hut where the mealie cobs were stored, went to bed with a sense of sweet security from elephants and all other animals that prowl by night.

“For my part, I pitched my camp below the kraal; and then, having procured a beam of wood from the head man—rather a rotten one, by the way—I set it across two boughs that ran out laterally from the baobab tree, at a height of about twenty-five feet from the ground, in such fashion that I and another man could sit upon it with our legs hanging down, and rest our backs against the bole of the tree. This done I went back to the camp and ate my supper. About nine o’clock, half-an-hour before the moonrise, I summoned Gobo, who, thinking that he had seen about enough of the delights of big game hunting for that day, did not altogether relish the job; and, despite his remonstrances, gave him my eight-bore to carry, I having the .570-express. Then we set out for the tree. It was very dark, but we found it without difficulty, though climbing it was a more complicated matter. However, at last we got up and sat down, like two little boys on a form that is too high for them, and waited. I did not dare to smoke, because I remembered the rhinoceros, and feared that the elephants might wind the tobacco if they should come my way, and this made the business more wearisome, so I fell to thinking and wondering at the completeness of the silence.

“At last the moon came up, and with it a moaning wind, at the breath of which the silence began to whisper mysteriously. Lonely enough in the newborn light looked the wide expanse of mountain, plain, and forest, more like some vision of a dream, some reflection from a fair world of peace beyond our ken, than the mere face of garish earth made soft with sleep. Indeed, had it not been for the fact that I was beginning to find the log on which I sat very hard, I should have grown quite sentimental over the beautiful sight; but I will defy anybody to become sentimental when seated in the damp, on a very rough beam of wood, and halfway up a tree. So I merely made a mental note that it was a particularly lovely night, and turned my attention to the prospect of elephants. But no elephants came, and after waiting for another hour or so, I think that what between weariness and disgust, I must have dropped into a gentle doze. Presently I awoke with a start. Gobo, who was perched close to me, but as far off as the beam would allow—for neither white man nor black like the aroma which each vows is the peculiar and disagreeable property of the other—was faintly, very faintly clicking his forefinger against his thumb. I knew by this signal, a very favourite one among native hunters and gun-bearers, that he must have seen or heard something. I looked at his face, and saw that he was staring excitedly towards the dim edge of the bush beyond the deep green line of mealies. I stared too, and listened. Presently I heard a soft large sound as though a giant were gently stretching out his hands and pressing back the ears of standing corn. Then came a pause, and then, out into the open majestically stalked the largest elephant I ever saw or ever shall see. Heavens! what a monster he was; and how the moonlight gleamed upon his one splendid tusk—for the other was missing—as he stood among the mealies gently moving his enormous ears to and fro, and testing the wind with his trunk. While I was still marvelling at his girth, and speculating upon the weight of that huge tusk, which I swore should be my tusk before very long, out stepped a second bull and stood beside him. He was not quite so tall, but he seemed to me to be almost thicker-set than the first; and even in that light I could see that both his tusks were perfect. Another pause, and the third emerged. He was shorter than either of the others, but higher in the shoulder than No. 2; and when I tell you, as I afterwards learnt from actual measurement, that the smallest of these mighty bulls measured twelve feet one and a half inches at the shoulder, it will give you some idea of their size. The three formed into line and stood still for a minute, the one-tusked bull gently caressing the elephant on the left with his trunk.

“Then they began to feed, walking forward and slightly to the right as they gathered great bunches of the sweet mealies and thrust them into their mouths. All this time they were more than a hundred and twenty yards away from me (this I knew, because I had paced the distances from the tree to various points), much too far to allow of my attempting a shot at them in that uncertain light. They fed in a semicircle, gradually drawing round towards the hut near my tree, in which the corn was stored and the old woman slept.

===Chinese===
“然而，如我所说，人心是完完全全定下来了；那天晚上，寨里人人——连存放玉米棒子的小屋里那位妒火中烧的老牌受害者也算上——都怀着一种甜美的安全感上了床，觉得从此大象也好，夜里出没的其他走兽也好，都害不着他们了。

“至于我，我把营扎在寨子下方；然后向头人讨了一根木梁——顺带说一句，相当糟朽的一根——把它架在猴面包树横生的两根枝上，离地约二十五英尺高，架得我同另一个人可以坐在上面垂着腿，背靠树干。架好之后我回营吃了晚饭。约莫九点钟，月亮升起前半小时，我唤来戈博——他认定当天大猎的那份妙处已经见识得够多了，对这差事全无兴致；我不顾他的抗议，把八号枪交给他背，自己拿 .570 特快步枪。随后我们便向那棵树进发。天很黑，但我们没费事就找到了树，爬上去却费事些。不过我们终于上去了，坐下来，像两个坐在高得够不着地的条凳上的小男孩，等着。我不敢抽烟，因为记着犀牛的教训，生怕象若朝我来会闻见烟味，这使得这桩差事愈发无趣，我便胡思乱想起来，暗自诧异这寂静竟如此彻底。

“终于月亮上来了，随之而来一阵呜咽似的风，风息一吹，寂静开始神秘地窃窃私语。在新生的月光下，山峦、平原与森林的辽阔景象够多冷清的了，与其说是叫睡意软化了的、白晃晃的人间面目，不如说更像梦中的幻象，像我们视野之外某个美好的和平世界投来的倒影。说实话，若不是坐下的这根木梁渐渐硌得慌，这场美景真要叫我伤感起来；可是我倒要看看，谁能在潮湿里、在一根糙木头上、在半树高的地方伤感得起来。于是我仅在心里记下一笔：今夜月色格外可爱；便把心思转向大象上。可象没有来，又等了一个钟头上下，我想是一半困乏一半厌烦，我竟迷迷糊糊打起盹来。忽然我一惊而醒。戈博栖在我近旁——横梁容许的最远处，因为白人黑人彼此都嫌对方的气味，各自咬定那是对方独有而又讨嫌的物事——正极轻极轻地用食指叩着拇指。我凭这个信号——土人猎手和背枪人最爱用的一种——知道他必定看见了或听见了什么。我看他的脸，见他正兴奋地瞪着玉米那道深绿线外灌木的模糊边缘。我也瞪过去，也听。不久我听见一阵又软又大的声响，仿佛一个巨人轻轻伸出双手，把立着的玉米穗压向后方。然后一停；然后，踱进开阔地里来的，是我平生所见、也必是此生将见的最最大的一头象，步态庄严。天哪！好一个庞然大物；它立在玉米间，巨大的耳朵轻轻前后扇动，鼻子试探着风，月光在那根独牙上流转生辉——另一根没有了。我还在惊叹它的腰身，还在掂量那根巨牙的分量——我发誓不久它就得是我的牙——第二头公象迈出来，立在它旁边。它不及第一头高，但在我看来几乎比第一头还粗壮；即便在那样的光线下我也看得出，它两根牙齐全完好。再一顿，第三头也出来了。它比前两头都矮，肩膀却比二号还高；我告诉你们——这是我后来实地量得的——这三头巨象中最小的一头，肩高十二英尺一英寸半，你们由此可以想见它们的个头。三头排成一行，静静立了约一分钟，独牙象用鼻子轻轻爱抚着左边那头。

“随后它们开始进食，一边向前、稍稍偏右地移动，一边卷起一大把一大把甜玉米塞进嘴里。这期间它们始终离我一百二十多码（这我知道，因为我从树下步测过到各点的距离），在那样的微光里，太远了，开枪是没有把握的。它们沿着一条半圆弧吃过去，渐渐兜向我树旁那座茅屋——屋里存着玉米，睡着那位老妇人。

===Original===
“This went on for between an hour and an hour and a half, till, what between excitement and hope, that maketh the heart sick, I grew so weary that I was actually contemplating a descent from the tree and a moonlight stalk. Such an act in ground so open would have been that of a stark staring lunatic, and that I should even have been contemplating it will show you the condition of my mind. But everything comes to him who knows how to wait, and sometimes too to him who doesn’t, and so at last those elephants, or rather one of them, came to me.

“After they had fed their fill, which was a very large one, the noble three stood once more in line some seventy yards to the left of the hut, and on the edge of the cultivated lands, or in all about eighty-five yards from where I was perched. Then at last the one with a single tusk made a peculiar rattling noise in his trunk, just as though he were blowing his nose, and without more ado began to walk deliberately toward the hut where the old woman slept. I made my rifle ready and glanced up at the moon, only to discover that a new complication was looming in the immediate future. I have said that a wind rose with the moon. Well, the wind brought rain-clouds along its track. Several light ones had already lessened the light for a little while, though without obscuring it, and now two more were coming up rapidly, both of them very black and dense. The first cloud was small and long, and the one behind big and broad. I remember noticing that the pair of them bore a most comical resemblance to a dray drawn by a very long rawboned horse. As luck would have it, just as the elephant arrived within twenty-five yards or so of me, the head of the horse-cloud floated over the face of the moon, rendering it impossible for me to fire. In the faint twilight which remained, however, I could just make out the gray mass of the great brute still advancing towards the hut. Then the light went altogether and I had to trust to my ears. I heard him fumbling with his trunk, apparently at the roof of the hut; next came a sound as of straw being drawn out, and then for a little while there was complete silence.

===Chinese===
“这样过了约莫一个到一个半钟头，等到兴奋与希望——望得人心头发苦的东西——把我熬得实在倦了，我竟当真盘算起下树去月下潜猎来。在这样开阔的地上做这种事，只有十足的发疯汉才干得出，而我竟然会动这念头，也可见我当时的心境了。不过，凡事总会降临到懂得等待的人头上，有时也降临到不懂等待的人头上；于是那些象，或者不如说其中一头，终于朝我来了。

“它们吃饱之后——那一顿委实不小——尊贵的三位再次排成一线，在茅屋左侧约七十码、耕地边缘的地方，换句话说，离我栖身处统共约八十五码。终于，独牙的那头在鼻子里弄出一阵奇特的咯咯声，活像在擤鼻子，接着二话不说，从容不迫地朝老妇人睡的茅屋走去。我把枪装好，抬头看月，却发现新的乱子已迫在眉睫。我说过，风是随月同起的。这不，风一路把雨云也捎了来。几片薄云已经使月光暗过一阵——还不至于遮没——如今又有两片正快速上来，都又黑又厚。头一片云小而长，后一片大而宽。我记得当时就留意到，这两片云活脱脱像一匹瘦骨嶙峋的长杆马拉着一辆运货车，滑稽得很。偏偏不巧，大象进到离我约二十五码时，那匹‘马’的脑袋恰好飘到月亮脸上，叫我没法开枪。不过在余下的微明里，我还辨得出那巨兽灰色的身影仍在向茅屋推进。随后光亮整个没了，我只好凭耳朵。我听见它用鼻子摸索，像是在茅屋顶上；接着传来一股抽柴草般的声响，再往后，有那么一小会儿，万籁俱寂。

===Original===
“The cloud began to pass; I could see the outline of the elephant; he was standing with his head quite over the top of the hut. But I could not see his trunk, and no wonder, for it was *inside the hut*. He had thrust it through the roof, and, attracted no doubt by the smell of the mealies, was groping about with it inside. It was growing light now, and I got my rifle ready, when suddenly there was a most awful yell, and I saw the trunk reappear, and in its mighty fold the old woman who had been sleeping in the hut. Out she came through the hole like a periwinkle on the point of a pin, still wrapped up in her blanket, and with her skinny arms and legs stretched to the four points of the compass, and as she did so, gave that most alarming screech. I really don’t know who was the most frightened, she, or I, or the elephant. At any rate the last was considerably startled; he had been fishing for mealies—the old woman was a mere accident, and one that greatly discomposed his nerves. He gave a sort of trumpet, and threw her away from him right into the crown of a low mimosa tree, where she stuck shrieking like a metropolitan engine. The old bull lifted his tail, and flapping his great ears prepared for flight. I put up my eight-bore, and aiming hastily at the point of his shoulder (for he was broadside on), I fired. The report rang out like thunder, making a thousand echoes in the quiet hills. I saw him go down all of a heap as though he were stone dead. Then, alas! whether it was the kick of the heavy rifle, or the excited bump of that idiot Gobo, or both together, or merely an unhappy coincidence, I do not know, but the rotten beam broke and I went down too, landing flat at the foot of the tree upon a certain humble portion of the human frame. The shock was so severe that I felt as though all my teeth were flying through the roof of my mouth, but although I sat slightly stunned for a few seconds, luckily for me I fell light, and was not in any way injured.

“Meanwhile the elephant began to scream with fear and fury, and, attracted by his cries, the other two charged up. I felt for my rifle; it was not there. Then I remembered that I had rested it on a fork of the bough in order to fire, and doubtless there it remained. My position was now very unpleasant. I did not dare to try and climb the tree again, which, shaken as I was, would have been a task of some difficulty, because the elephants would certainly see me, and Gobo, who had clung to a bough, was still aloft with the other rifle. I could not run because there was no shelter near. Under these circumstances I did the only thing feasible, clambered round the trunk as softly as possible, and keeping one eye on the elephants, whispered to Gobo to bring down the rifle, and awaited the development of the situation. I knew that if the elephants did not see me—which, luckily, they were too enraged to do—they would not smell me, for I was upwind. Gobo, however, either did not, or, preferring the safety of the tree, would not hear me. He said the former, but I believed the latter, for I knew that he was not enough of a sportsman to really enjoy shooting elephants by moonlight in the open. So there I was behind my tree, dismayed, unarmed, but highly interested, for I was witnessing a remarkable performance.

===Chinese===
“云开始过去；我看得见象的轮廓了；它立着，头完全探在茅屋顶上。可我看不见它的鼻子——也难怪，鼻子*在屋里*。它把鼻子从屋顶捅了进去，多半是叫玉米的气味引的，正在里面四处摸索。天光渐渐亮起来，我把枪端好，忽然间发出一声骇人的尖叫，我看见那鼻子重新出现，而它有力的卷曲里，是那位一直在屋里睡觉的老妇人。她从窟窿里被拎了出来，像针尖上挑起的一只螺蛳，毯子还裹在身上，枯瘦的四肢朝着东南西北四个方向张成一个大字，而出窝的这一刻，她发出了那声惊天动地的尖叫。我实在说不上谁吓得更狠：她，我，还是象。至少最后那位是着实吃了一惊；它本来是在钓玉米——老妇人纯属意外，而这意外大大搅了它的神经。它喇叭似的一声，把她朝外一甩，不偏不倚甩进一株矮金合欢树的树冠，她挂在那儿尖叫，活像伦敦街头的蒸汽救火机。老公象竖起尾巴，扇着大耳，准备逃了。我举起八号枪，匆匆瞄准它的肩点（因为它正侧身对着我们），开了火。枪声如雷，在寂静的群山里激起千重回响。我看见它猛然瘫作一团，仿佛死透了。可是，唉！不知是重型步枪的后坐，还是戈博那蠢货兴奋之下的一撞，还是两者一起，又或只是不幸的巧合——那根糟朽的木梁断了，我也落了下去，平平地摔在树脚下，着地的是人体上一个颇为卑微的部位。这一下震得我好比满口牙齿都要从上膛飞出去；不过虽然我懵坐了几秒钟，幸而我摔得轻，一点儿也没伤着。

“与此同时，那头象又怕又怒，尖叫起来；另两头闻声冲了上来。我摸枪；枪不在了。这才想起开枪前我把它架在树枝的一个杈上，此刻多半还在那儿。我的处境现在很不美妙。我不敢试着再爬上树去——我摔得七荤八素，爬树本就是件难事——何况象必会看见我；而抱住一根枝子的戈博还高高在上，带着另一支枪。我又不能跑，因为近处没有遮蔽。处此情形，我只做了唯一可行的事：尽可能轻地绕到树干后面，一只眼睛盯着象，低声叫戈博把枪送下来，静候局势发展。我知道象若看不见我——幸好它们气昏了头，看不见——便闻不着我，因为我在上风。可是戈博要么没听见，要么贪恋树上的安全，装没听见。他说是前者，我却信是后者，因为我知道，他还算不上那样的猎手，能真心享受月下旷野打象的乐趣。于是我就在我的树后待着，狼狈，赤手空拳，却兴致极高，因为眼前正上演一幕了不得的场面。

===Original===
“When the two other bulls arrived the wounded elephant on the ground ceased to scream, but began to make a low moaning noise, and to gently touch the wound near his shoulder, from which the blood was literally spouting. The other two seemed to understand; at any rate, they did this. Kneeling down on either side, they placed their trunks and tusks underneath him, and, aided by his own efforts, with one great lift got him on to his feet. Then leaning against him on either side to support him, they marched off at a walk in the direction of the village.1 It was a pitiful sight, and even then it made me feel a brute.

“Presently, from a walk, as the wounded elephant gathered himself together a little, they broke into a trot, and after that I could follow them no longer with my eyes, for the second black cloud came up over the moon and put her out, as an extinguisher puts out a dip. I say with my eyes, but my ears gave me a very fair notion of what was going on. When the cloud came up the three terrified animals were heading directly for the kraal, probably because the way was open and the path easy. I fancy that they grew confused in the darkness, for when they came to the kraal fence they did not turn aside, but crashed straight through it. Then there were ‘times,’ as the Irish servant-girl says in the American book. Having taken the fence, they thought that they might as well take the kraal also, so they just ran over it. One hive-shaped hut was turned quite over on to its top, and when I arrived upon the scene the people who had been sleeping there were bumbling about inside like bees disturbed at night, while two more were crushed flat, and a third had all its side torn out. Oddly enough, however, nobody was hurt, though several people had a narrow escape of being trodden to death.

“On arrival I found the old head man in a state painfully like that favoured by Greek art, dancing about in front of his ruined abodes as vigorously as though he had just been stung by a scorpion.

===Chinese===
“另两头公象赶到时，地上那头伤象止住了尖叫，改为一声低低的哀鸣，又轻轻去碰肩旁的伤口，血简直是喷涌而出的。那两头像是懂得；至少，它们这样做了。它们在它两侧跪下，把鼻子和牙伸到它身下，靠它自己也使着劲，猛一抬，把它抬上了腿。然后一左一右倚着它、撑着它，迈开步子，朝村寨的方向去了。1那景象令人心恻，连我当时都觉得自己像个畜生。

“不久，伤象稍稍缓过一点元气，三头便由走改小跑，再往后我的眼睛就追不上它们了——第二片黑云漫上月亮，像灭烛罩掐灭一支小蜡烛似的把她罩熄了。我说眼睛追不上，耳朵却把动静摸了个大概。云上来时，三头惊惶的巨兽正对着村寨直去，多半因为那条道开阔好走。我料它们在黑暗里慌了神，因为到了寨篱跟前它们竟不拐弯，径直撞了过去。于是就有了‘热闹’，正如美国书里那位爱尔兰女佣说的。篱笆既已拿下，它们索性把村寨也一并拿下，干脆从上面碾了过去。一座蜂巢形的茅屋整个翻了个底朝天，等我赶到现场时，原本睡在里面的人还在屋里嗡嗡乱转，像夜里被搅了的蜜蜂；另有两座被碾得平平整整，第三座被撕去了整整一面墙。然而说来奇怪，竟没有一个人受伤，尽管有几个人差一点就被踩死。

“到达之后，我看见老头人正处在一种同希腊艺术钟爱的姿态像得叫人不忍的状态里：在他那些塌了的住所跟前蹦来跳去，劲头十足，仿佛刚被蝎子蜇了一下。

===Original===
“I asked him what ailed him, and he burst out into a flood of abuse. He called me a Wizard, a Sham, a Fraud, a Bringer of bad luck! I had promised to kill the elephants, and I had so arranged things that the elephants had nearly killed him, etc.

“This, still smarting, or rather aching, as I was from that most terrific bump, was too much for my feelings, so I just made a rush at my friend, and getting him by the ear, I banged his head against the doorway of his own hut, which was all that was left of it.

“‘You wicked old scoundrel,’ I said, ‘you dare to complain about your own trifling inconveniences, when you gave me a rotten beam to sit on, and thereby delivered me to the fury of the elephant’ (*bump! bump! bump!*), ‘when your own wife’ (*bump!*) ‘has just been dragged out of her hut’ (*bump!*) ‘like a snail from its shell, and thrown by the Earth-shaker into a tree’ (*bump! bump!*).

===Chinese===
“我问他哪里不自在，他便破口大骂起来。他管我叫巫师、骗子、冒牌货、丧门星！我答应打死大象，结果我把事情安排得叫大象差点打死他，云云。

“我那一跤摔得着实厉害，还正在又辣又痛——不如说又酸又痛——之际，这番话实在叫人忍无可忍，我便朝我这位朋友直冲过去，一把揪住他的耳朵，拿他的脑袋去撞他自己茅屋的门框——门框差不多是那屋剩下的全部了。

“‘你这老恶棍，’我说，‘你塞给我一根糟木头坐，把我交给大象去发威’（*咚！咚！咚！*），‘如今你自己老婆’（*咚！*）‘刚被人从屋里拖出来’（*咚！*）‘像蜗牛出了壳，还叫撼地者甩上了树’（*咚！咚！*），‘你倒敢抱怨你这点鸡毛蒜皮的不痛快？’

===Original===
“‘Mercy, my father, mercy!’ gasped the old fellow. ‘Truly I have done amiss—my heart tells me so.’

“‘I should hope it did, you old villain’ (*bump!*).

“‘Mercy, great white man! I thought the log was sound. But what says the unequalled chief—is the old woman, my wife, indeed dead? Ah, if she is dead all may yet prove to have been for the very best;’ and he clasped his hands and looked up piously to heaven, in which the moon was once more shining brightly.

===Chinese===
“‘饶命，我的父亲，饶命！’老家伙喘着气说。‘我实在错了——我的心这样告诉我。’

“‘我巴不得它告诉你了，你这老坏蛋’（*咚！*）。

“‘开恩吧，伟大的白人！我原以为那根木头是结实的。只是无与伦比的酋长怎么说——那老太婆，我的老婆，当真死了么？啊，她若是死了，这一切倒可能再好不过；’说着他合起双手，虔诚地仰望上天，天上月亮又亮堂堂地照着。

===Original===
“I let go his ear and burst out laughing, the whole scene and his devout aspirations for the decease of the partner of his joys, or rather woes, were so intensely ridiculous.

“‘No, you old iniquity,’ I answered; ‘I left her in the top of a thorn-tree, screaming like a thousand bluejays. The elephant put her there.’

“‘Alas! alas!’ he said, ‘surely the back of the ox is shaped to the burden. Doubtless, my father, she will come down when she is tired;’ and without troubling himself further about the matter, he began to blow at the smouldering embers of the fire.

===Chinese===
“我松开他的耳朵，放声大笑：这整个场面，连同他对他那位欢乐伴侣——不如说苦难伴侣——魂归西天的虔诚祈愿，实在是滑稽透顶。

“‘没有，你这老祸害，’我答道；‘我把她留在一棵刺树顶上了，叫得像一千只蓝松鸦。是象把她放上去的。’

“‘哎呀！哎呀！’他说，‘牛背生来就是驮担子的。不用愁，我的父亲，她累了自然会下来；’说罢他便不再为这事操心，俯身去吹火堆里将熄的余烬了。

===Original===
“And, as a matter of fact, she did appear a few minutes later, considerably scratched and startled, but none the worse.

“After that I made my way to my little camp, which, fortunately, the elephants had not walked over, and wrapping myself up in a blanket, was soon fast asleep.

“And so ended my first round with those three elephants.”

===Chinese===
“而事实上，几分钟后她果然出现了，擦伤了不少地方，也着实受惊不小，但并无大碍。

“此后我回到我的小营地——幸好象群没有从上面走过——裹上毯子，很快便睡熟了。

“我与那三头象的第一回合，就这样收了场。”

## IV The Last Round / 四 最后一轮

===Original===
“On the morrow I woke up full of painful recollections, and not without a certain feeling of gratitude to the Powers above that I was there to wake up. Yesterday had been a tempestuous day; indeed, what between buffalo, rhinoceros, and elephant, it had been very tempestuous. Having realized this fact, I next bethought me of those magnificent tusks, and instantly, early as it was, broke the tenth commandment. I coveted my neighbour’s tusks, if an elephant could be said to be my neighbour de jure, as certainly, so recently as the previous night, he had been de facto﻿—a much closer neighbour than I cared for, indeed. Now when you covet your neighbour’s goods, the best thing, if not the most moral thing, to do is to enter his house as a strong man armed, and take them. I was not a strong man, but having recovered my eight-bore I was armed, and so was the other strong man﻿—the elephant with the tusks. Consequently I prepared for a struggle to the death. In other words, I summoned my faithful retainers, and told them that I was now going to follow those elephants to the edge of the world, if necessary. They showed a certain bashfulness about the business, but they did not gainsay me, because they dared not. Ever since I had prepared with all due solemnity to execute the rebellious Gobo they had conceived a great respect for me.

“So I went up to bid adieu to the old head man, whom I found alternately contemplating the ruins of his kraal and, with the able assistance of his last wife, thrashing the jealous lady who had slept in the mealie hut, because she was, as he declared, the fount of all his sorrows.

“Leaving them to work a way through their domestic differences, I levied a supply of vegetable food from the kraal in consideration of services rendered, and left them with my blessing. I do not know how they settled matters, because I have not seen them since.

===Chinese===
“次日一觉醒来，满心痛苦的回忆；不过想到自己居然还能醒来，对头顶的上苍也不无感激。昨天是惊涛骇浪的一天；说真的，水牛、犀牛加大象，何止惊涛骇浪而已。明白了这一层，我接着便想起那几支漂亮之极的象牙。天色尽管还早，我立时就触犯了第十诫：我觊觎起邻舍的象牙来——若说一头大象在法理上也算我的邻舍的话；而不容分说，直到昨夜为止，它在事实上一直是——而且是近得叫我嫌烦的——邻舍。人一旦觊觎了邻舍的财物，最好的——虽说不是最有德的——办法，便是学那武装的壮士闯进他家，把东西拿了。我算不得壮士，但找回那支八号枪之后，我算有了武装；而另一位壮士，那长着象牙的大象，也同样武装齐备。于是我便着手准备一场殊死之战。换句话说，我召集起忠心的仆从，宣告这一回若有必要，我要跟着那几头象一直追到天涯海角。他们对这桩差使未免面有难色，却没有一个敢驳我——自从我那回郑重其事地准备处决反贼戈博，他们对我便敬畏有加。

“于是我上去向老头人辞行。只见他正两头忙：一头对着自家克拉尔的废墟出神，一头在他末任太太的得力协助下，痛打那位在玉米仓里过夜的醋坛子太太——因为照他的说法，那女人是他一切灾祸的源头。

“我把他们留在那儿慢慢料理家事，自己以劳务酬劳的名义，向克拉尔征了一批口粮，又祝福了他们一番，便动身了。这事后来如何了结，我不知道，因为打那以后再没见过他们。

===Original===
“Then I started on the spoor of the three bulls. For a couple of miles or so below the kraal﻿—as far, indeed, as the belt of swamp that borders the river﻿—the ground is at this spot rather stony, and clothed with scattered bushes. Rain had fallen towards the daybreak, and this fact, together with the nature of the soil, made spooring a very difficult business. The wounded bull had indeed bled freely, but the rain had washed the blood off the leaves and grass, and the ground being so rough and hard did not take the footmarks so clearly as was convenient. However, we got along, though slowly, partly by the spoor, and partly by carefully lifting leaves and blades of grass, and finding blood underneath them, for the blood gushing from a wounded animal often falls upon their inner surfaces, and then, of course, unless the rain is very heavy, it is not washed away. It took us something over an hour and a half to reach the edge of the marsh, but once there our task became much easier, for the soft soil showed plentiful evidences of the great brutes’ passage. Threading our way through the swampy land, we came at last to a ford of the river, and here we could see where the poor wounded animal had lain down in the mud and water in the hope of easing himself of his pain, and could see also how his two faithful companions had assisted him to rise again. We crossed the ford, and took up the spoor on the further side, and followed it into the marsh-like land beyond. No rain had fallen on this side of the river, and the blood-marks were consequently much more frequent.

“All that day we followed the three bulls, now across open plains, and now through patches of bush. They seemed to have travelled on almost without stopping, and I noticed that as they went the wounded bull recovered his strength a little. This I could see from his spoor, which had become firmer, and also from the fact that the other two had ceased to support him. At last evening closed in, and having travelled some eighteen miles, we camped, thoroughly tired out.

===Chinese===
“于是我踏上三头公象的踪迹。克拉尔往下约莫两英里——直到河边那道沼泽带为止——这一带的地面石块颇多，间杂稀疏的灌木。破晓前后下过一场雨，加上土质的关系，追寻兽迹成了苦差。受伤的公象固然流了不少血，可雨水把叶面草叶上的血冲了个干净；地面又粗又硬，足印也印不真切，叫人难以辨认。好在我们仍旧推进，虽然慢：一半靠兽迹，一半靠小心掀起叶片草叶，查探底下的血——受伤野兽喷出的血，常常落在叶片的里侧，除非雨下得很大，不至于冲掉。我们花了一个半钟头挂零，才走到沼泽边上；一到那儿，差事便轻省多了，松软的泥土上满是那几头巨兽通过的痕迹。穿过沼泽地，我们终于到了河上一处渡口。在那儿，看得出那头可怜的受伤家伙，曾在泥水里躺过，想以此减轻痛楚；也看得出他那两个忠心的伙伴，怎样帮他重新站起。我们涉水过河，在对岸重新拾起兽迹，一路跟进那边沼泽般的土地。河这一边没下过雨，血迹于是密得多。

“那一整天，我们跟定三头公象，时而横过开阔的平野，时而穿过一丛丛灌木。他们差不多一路不曾停脚；我注意到，那头受伤的公象边走边稍稍缓过了些力气——这从兽迹上看得出，步子沉稳了；也从另外两头不再搀扶他这一点上看得出。暮色四合时，我们已走了约十八英里，便扎下营来，个个累得散了架。

===Original===
“Before dawn on the following day we were up, and the first break of light found us once more on the spoor. About half-past five o’clock we reached the place where the elephants had fed and slept. The two unwounded bulls had taken their fill, as the condition of the neighbouring bushes showed, but the wounded one had eaten nothing. He had spent the night leaning against a good-sized tree, which his weight had pushed out of the perpendicular. They had not long left this place, and could not be very far ahead, especially as the wounded bull was now again so stiff after his night’s rest that for the first few miles the other two had been obliged to support him. But elephants go very quick, even when they seem to be travelling slowly, for shrub and creepers that almost stop a man’s progress are no hindrance to them. The three had now turned to the left, and were travelling back again in a semicircular line toward the mountains, probably with the idea of working round to their old feeding grounds on the further side of the river.

“There was nothing for it but to follow their lead, and accordingly we followed with industry. Through all that long hot day did we tramp, passing quantities of every sort of game, and even coming across the spoor of other elephants. But, in spite of my men’s entreaties, I would not turn aside after these. I would have those mighty tusks or none.

===Chinese===
“第二天破晓之前我们便起身，天光初透，又踏上了兽迹。五点半左右，我们到了大象们觅食歇宿的地方。那两头没受伤的公象吃了个足——四邻灌木的状况便是明证——受伤的那头却什么也没吃。他靠着一棵相当粗大的树站了一夜，那树叫他沉重的身躯顶得歪出了直线。他们离开此地不久，前头不会太远；何况受伤的公象歇了一夜又僵了，起初几英里，另外两头还得一路架着他。不过大象走得极快，哪怕看着慢条斯理——那些几乎拦住人脚步的灌木藤蔓，于他们如若无物。这三头如今已折向左方，沿一条半圆的路线重又朝山里行去，多半是想兜回河对岸他们往日的觅食场。

“除了跟着他们走，别无他法，于是我们发愤跟随。整整一个漫长炎热的日子，我们一路跋涉，途中经过成群结队的各色野味，甚至撞见别的大象的踪迹。但任凭仆从们苦劝，我也不肯为这些岔开一步：我要么得到那几支巨牙，要么两手空空。

===Original===
“By evening we were quite close to our game, probably within a quarter of a mile, but the bush was dense, and we could see nothing of them, so once more we must camp, thoroughly disgusted with our luck. That night, just after the moon rose, while I was sitting smoking my pipe with my back against a tree, I heard an elephant trumpet, as though something had startled it, and not three hundred yards away. I was very tired, but my curiosity overcame my weariness, so, without saying a word to any of the men, all of whom were asleep, I took my eight-bore and a few spare cartridges, and steered toward the sound. The game path which we had been following all day ran straight on in the direction from which the elephant had trumpeted. It was narrow, but well trodden, and the light struck down upon it in a straight white line. I crept along it cautiously for some two hundred yards, when it opened suddenly into a most beautiful glade some hundred yards or more in width, wherein tall grass grew and flat-topped trees stood singly. With the caution born of long experience I watched for a few moments before I entered the glade, and then I saw why the elephant had trumpeted. There in the middle of the glade stood a large maned lion. He stood quite still, making a soft purring noise, and waving his tail to and fro. Presently the grass about forty yards on the hither side of him gave a wide ripple, and a lioness sprang out of it like a flash, and bounded noiselessly up to the lion. Reaching him, the great cat halted suddenly, and rubbed her head against his shoulder. Then they both began to purr loudly, so loudly that I believe that in the stillness one might have heard them two hundred yards or more away.

“After a time, while I was still hesitating what to do, either they got a whiff of my wind, or they wearied of standing still, and determined to start in search of game. At any rate, as though moved by a common impulse, they bounded suddenly away, leap by leap, and vanished in the depths of the forest to the left. I waited for a little while longer to see if there were any more yellow skins about, and seeing none, came to the conclusion that the lions must have frightened the elephants away, and that I had taken my stroll for nothing. But just as I was turning back I thought that I heard a bough break upon the further side of the glade, and, rash as the act was, I followed the sound. I crossed the glade as silently as my own shadow. On its further side the path went on. Albeit with many fears, I went on too. The jungle growth was so thick here that it almost met overhead, leaving so small a passage for the light that I could scarcely see to grope my way along. Presently, however, it widened, and then opened into a second glade slightly smaller than the first, and there, on the further side of it, about eighty yards from me, stood the three enormous elephants.

===Chinese===
“到傍晚，我们离猎物已经极近，恐怕不出四分之一英里；可灌木茂密，什么也看不见，只得再度扎营，晦气透了。那天夜里，月亮刚升起来，我正背靠一棵树坐着抽我的烟斗，忽听得一头象吼叫起来，像是受了什么惊吓，离着不到三百码。我累得很，可好奇心压倒了疲乏。我便一个字也没跟仆从们说——他们全睡了——提起那支八号枪和几发备用弹药，循声摸去。我们一整天循走的那条兽径笔直向前，正通向象吼传来的方向。径道窄，但踩得瓷实，月光直射下来，铺成一条白线。我顺着它悄悄爬行了约莫两百码，小径豁然张开，眼前是一片极美的林间草地，宽约百码开外，高草丛生，平顶的树一株一株兀自站着。凭着多年经验养成的谨慎，进草地之前我先观望了片刻，随即看明白了那象为何吼叫。草地中央立着一头硕大的鬃狮。他一动不动地站着，喉咙里发出轻柔的呼噜声，尾巴来回摆动。不多时，他这边约四十码外的草，忽地朝两边大幅翻伏，一头母狮像道闪光般跃出，几个无声的纵跳便到了狮子跟前。到了他身边，那头大猫猛地收住脚，把头往他肩上蹭了蹭。随后两个都大声呼噜起来——那声音之大，我相信静夜之中两百码外也听得见。

“过了一阵，我还拿不定主意，他们或是嗅到了我的气味，或是站腻了，决意动身觅食。总之，像被同一个念头驱使着，他们突然一跳一跳地窜了出去，消失在左首林木深处。我又多等了一会儿，看附近还有没有别的黄皮货，见没有，便断定狮子准是把象吓跑了，我这一趟是白走。可正要转身，我仿佛听得草地那头有一根树枝折断，便循声跟了上去——此举实在孟浪。我像自己的影子一般悄没声地穿过草地。草地那头，小径还在向前延伸。我怀着满腹疑惧，也跟了上去。这里草木密得几乎在头顶合了拢，留给光线的那一线缝隙小得我几乎要摸索着走。不多时小径却宽了，又敞开进入第二片草地，比第一片略小；就在草地那头，离我约八十码，立着三头巨象。

===Original===
“They stood thus:﻿—Immediately opposite and facing me was the wounded one-tusked bull. He was leaning his bulk against a dead thorn-tree, the only one in the place, and looked very sick indeed. Near him stood the second bull as though keeping a watch over him. The third elephant was a good deal nearer to me and broadside on. While I was still staring at them, this elephant suddenly walked off and vanished down a path in the bush to the right.

“There are now two things to be done﻿—either I could go back to the camp and advance upon the elephants at dawn, or I could attack them at once. The first was, of course, by far the wiser and safer course. To engage one elephant by moonlight and single-handed is a sufficiently rash proceeding; to tackle three was little short of lunacy. But, on the other hand, I knew that they would be on the march again before daylight, and there might come another day of weary trudging before I could catch them up, or they might escape me altogether.

===Chinese===
“他们是这样站立的：正对着我、面朝我这边的，是那头受伤的独牙公象。他把庞大的身躯靠在一棵枯死的荆树上——那是此地唯一的一棵——瞧上去委实病得不轻。他近旁站着第二头公象，仿佛在守护着他。第三头离我近得多，侧身向着这边。我正打量间，这头象忽然举步走开，消失在右边灌木中的一条小径里。

“眼下有两条路：或者回营去，等天亮再向这几头象进攻；或者立刻动手。前者自然是稳妥安全得多的路子。月光之下、孤身一人对付一头象，已经够冒失；同时招惹三头，简直近于发疯。可另一面我也知道，天亮之前他们必定又要上路，说不定还得再苦熬一整天才追得上，或者他们索性从我手心里走脱了。

===Original===
“ ‘No,’ I thought to myself, ‘faint heart never won fair tusk. I’ll risk it, and have a slap at them. But how?’ I could not advance across the open, for they would see me; clearly the only thing to do was to creep round in the shadow of the bush and try to come upon them so. So I started. Seven or eight minutes of careful stalking brought me to the mouth of the path down which the third elephant had walked. The other two were now about fifty yards from me, and the nature of the wall of bush was such that I could not see how to get nearer to them without being discovered. I hesitated, and peeped down the path which the elephant had followed. About five yards in, it took a turn round a shrub. I thought that I would just have a look behind it, and advanced, expecting that I should be able to catch a sight of the elephant’s tail. As it happened, however, I met his trunk coming round the corner. It is very disconcerting to see an elephant’s trunk when you expect to see his tail, and for a moment I stood paralyzed almost under the vast brute’s head, for he was not five yards from me. He too halted, threw up his trunk and trumpeted preparatory to a charge. I was in for it now, for I could not escape either to the right or left, on account of the bush, and I did not dare turn my back. So I did the only thing that I could do﻿—raised the rifle and fired at the black mass of his chest. It was too dark for me to pick a shot; I could only brown him, as it were.

“The shot rung out like thunder on the quiet air, and the elephant answered it with a scream, then dropped his trunk and stood for a second or two as still as though he had been cut in stone. I confess that I lost my head; I ought to have fired my second barrel, but I did not. Instead of doing so, I rapidly opened my rifle, pulled out the old cartridge from the right barrel and replaced it. But before I could snap the breech to, the bull was at me. I saw his great trunk fly up like a brown beam, and I waited no longer. Turning, I fled for dear life, and after me thundered the elephant. Right into the open glade I ran, and then, thank Heaven, just as he was coming up with me the bullet took effect on him. He had been shot right through the heart, or lungs, and down he fell with a *crash*, stone dead.

===Chinese===
“‘不行，’我心里想，‘胆小鬼得不到美象牙。豁出去，同他们碰一碰。可是怎么碰？’我不能从开阔地正面过去，他们会看见我；明摆着，唯一的法子是借着灌木的黑影绕过去，想办法那样接近他们。于是我出发了。七八分钟小心翼翼的潜行，把我带到第三头象走下去的那条小径的路口。另外两头这时离我约五十码，那道灌木之墙的形势，叫我没法再贴近而不叫他们察觉。我犹豫着，朝那头象走进去的小径里张望。进去约五码，小径在一丛灌木后拐了个弯。我想，不如到拐角后头看一眼，便往前走，指望能瞥见那象的尾巴。谁知不巧，迎面撞上的，却是他绕过拐角探出来的鼻子。盼着看象尾巴却看见象鼻子，那才叫人心慌意乱；一时间我僵在那庞然大物的头颅底下，几乎动弹不得——他离我不足五码。他也顿住了，扬起鼻子，先吼一声，蓄势欲扑。这一下我在劫难逃：两边灌木拦着，右也逃不脱，左也逃不脱，又不敢把后背亮给他。于是我做了唯一能做的事——端起枪，对准他那黑黢黢的胸膛放了一枪。天色太黑，拣不得打处，我只能算朝那团黑影里，胡乱轰他一枪。

“枪声像打雷一样，炸响在寂静的夜气里；那象应声发出一声尖叫，随后垂下鼻子，一动不动地站了一两秒，仿佛石头凿成的。我承认我乱了方寸；本该打第二枪的，却没有打。我反倒急急打开枪机，从右膛退出旧弹壳，换上一发新的。可不等我咔哒合上枪尾，那公象已经扑到我面前。我见他那只大鼻子像根棕色房梁般抡了起来，我便不再等了。转身没命地飞跑，身后那象如雷追赶。我一路跑进开阔的草地；这时——感谢上天——正赶上他要追到我，子弹在他身上发作了。他被打穿了心脏，要不就是肺，*轰隆*一声栽倒，死得透透的。

===Original===
“But in escaping from Scylla I had run into the jaws of Charybdis. I heard the elephant fall, and glanced round. Straight in front of me, and not fifteen paces away, were the other two bulls. They were staring about, and at that moment they caught sight of me. Then they came, the pair of them﻿—came like thunderbolts, and from different angles. I had only time to snap my rifle to, lift it, and fire, almost at haphazard, at the head of the nearest, the unwounded bull.

“Now, as you know, in the case of the African elephant, whose skull is convex, and not concave like that of the Indian, this is always a most risky and very frequently a perfectly useless shot. The bullet loses itself in the masses of bone, that is all. But there is one little vital place, and should the bullet happen to strike there, it will follow the channel of the nostrils﻿—at least I suppose it is that of the nostrils﻿—and reach the brain. And this was what happened in the present case﻿—the ball struck the fatal spot in the region of the eye and travelled to the brain. Down came the great bull all of a heap, and rolled on to his side as dead as a stone. I swung round at that instant to face the third, the monster bull with one tusk that I had wounded two days before. He was already almost over me, and in the dim moonlight seemed to tower above me like a house. I lifted the rifle and pulled at his neck. It would not go off! Then, in a flash, as it were, I remembered that it was on the half-cock. The lock of this barrel was a little weak, and a few days before, in firing at a cow eland, the left barrel had jarred off at the shock of the discharge of the right, knocking me backwards with the recoil; so after that I had kept it on the half-cock till I actually wanted to fire it.

===Chinese===
“可是躲开了斯库拉，又一头栽进卡律布狄斯的嘴里。我听见那头象倒地，回头一瞥。正前方，不出十五步远，是另外那两头公象。他们正四下张望，恰在此时瞧见了我。随即他们冲来了——两个一起——如两道雷霆，而且从两个不同的角度逼来。我只来得及合上枪机，举起枪来，几乎是听天由命地，朝近处那头、没受伤的公象的头上开了一枪。

“要知道，非洲象的头骨是拱凸的，不像印度象那样凹陷，所以对非洲象打头，向来是极冒险、而且十有八九全无用处的一枪——子弹陷进层层叠叠的骨头里，如此而已。但有一处小小的要害，子弹倘若碰巧打中那里，便会顺着鼻孔的通道——至少我猜是鼻孔的通道——直捣脑髓。这一回正是如此：弹丸打中眼睛近旁那致命的一点，一路通到脑子里。那头大公象猛然瘫作一堆，翻身倒向一侧，死得像块石头。我霎时转身对付第三头——就是两天前被我打伤的那头独牙巨兽。他几乎已经罩到我头顶上，昏沉的月光里，像一座房子般耸立在我面前。我举起枪，朝他脖颈扣动扳机。枪不响！刹那间，如同电光一闪，我想起来了：枪还停在半击发上。这一膛的枪机有点松，几天前打一头母大羚羊时，右膛一响，左膛竟被后坐震得走了火，把我掀了个仰面朝天；打那以后，不到真要开枪，我总让它停在半击发上。

===Original===
“I gave one desperate bound to the right, and, my lame leg notwithstanding, I believe that few men could have made a better jump. At any rate, it was none too soon, for as I jumped I felt the wind made by the tremendous downward stroke of the monster’s trunk. Then I ran for it.

“I ran like a buck, still keeping hold of my gun, however. My idea, so far as I could be said to have any fixed idea, was to bolt down the pathway up which I had come, like a rabbit down a burrow, trusting that he would lose sight of me in the uncertain light. I sped across the glade. Fortunately the bull, being wounded, could not go full speed; but wounded or no, he could go quite as fast as I could. I was unable to gain an inch, and away we went, with just about three feet between our separate extremities. We were at the other side now, and a glance served to show me that I had miscalculated and overshot the opening. To reach it now was hopeless; I should have blundered straight into the elephant. So I did the only thing I could do: I swerved like a course hare, and started off round the edge of the glade, seeking for some opening into which I could plunge. This gave me a moment’s start, for the bull could not turn as quickly as I could, and I made the most of it. But no opening could I see; the bush was like a wall. We were speeding round the edge of the glade, and the elephant was coming up again. Now he was within about six feet, and now, as he trumpeted or rather screamed, I could feel the fierce hot blast of his breath strike upon my head. Heavens! how it frightened me!

===Chinese===
“我拼死向右边一纵——尽管一条腿瘸着，我相信能跳得这么利落的没几个人。反正，这一跳也堪堪赶趟：我跃起的当儿，只觉得那巨兽的鼻子挟着劲风，当头劈了下来。接着我便撒腿逃命。

“我跑得像头羚羊，手里的枪却始终没撒手。我的主意——倘若说我真有什么固定的主意的话——是顺着我来时的小径一头钻进去，像兔子钻洞一般，指望他在明灭不定的月色里看丢我。我飞身掠过草地。所幸那头公象带着伤，跑不出全速；可带伤也罢，不带伤也罢，他也跑得同我一样快。我一寸也拉不开；我们一前一后狂奔，两下首尾相去不过三英尺。这会儿到了草地那头，一瞥之间我便明白，自己算错了步子，跑过了入口。再要奔入口是休想了，那会一头撞进大象怀里。我只剩一件事可做：我像被猎狗追急的野兔般猛地一拐，沿着草地边缘跑起来，寻一处能一头扎进去的豁口。这一拐替我争得了一线先机，因为公象掉头不比我灵便，我便拼命用足这一瞬。可哪里也找不见豁口；灌木密得像堵墙。我们贴着草地边缘飞奔，那象又逼了上来。这会儿他离我不过六英尺；他一吼——不如说一尖叫——我便觉出他鼻孔里喷出的灼热气流，扑在我头顶上。天哪，那真吓破人胆！

===Original===
“We were three parts round the glade now, and about fifty yards ahead was the single large dead thorn-tree against which the bull had been leaning. I spurted for it; it was my last chance of safety.
