But spurt as I would, it seemed hours before I got there. Putting out my right hand, I swung round the tree, thus bringing myself face to face with the elephant. I had not time to lift the rifle to fire, I had barely time to cock it, and run sideways and backward, when he was on to me. *Crash!* he came, striking the tree full with his forehead. It snapped like a carrot about forty inches from the ground. Fortunately I was clear of the trunk, but one of the dead branches struck me on the chest as it went down and swept me to the ground. I fell upon my back, and the elephant blundered past me as I lay. More by instinct than anything else I lifted the rifle with one hand and pulled the trigger. It exploded, and, as I discovered afterwards, the bullet struck him in the ribs. But the recoil of the heavy rifle held thus was very severe; it bent my arm up, and sent the butt with a *thud* against the top of my shoulder and the side of my neck, for the moment quite paralyzing me, and causing the weapon to jump from my grasp. Meanwhile the bull was rushing on. He travelled for some twenty paces, and then suddenly he stopped. Faintly I reflected that he was coming back to finish me, but even the prospect of imminent and dreadful death could not rouse me into action. I was utterly spent; I could not move.

“Idly, almost indifferently, I watched his movements. For a moment he stood still, next he trumpeted till the welkin rang, and then very slowly, and with great dignity, he knelt down. At this point I swooned away.

===Chinese===
“这时我们已绕过草地的四分之三；前方约五十码，便是公象方才靠着的那株孤零零的大枯荆树。我拼尽全力朝它冲刺——那是我最后一线生机。可任我怎样冲，到得树下竟像隔了几个钟头。我伸出右手，绕着树干猛一转身，这下便同那象对了脸。举枪瞄准已经来不及，我勉强来得及把击锤扳满，斜着身子、倒退着跑，他已经到了。*轰！*他一头撞将上来，前额正正撞在树干上。那树立刻像根胡萝卜似的，在离地约四十英寸处折断了。幸好我已让开树身，可一根枯枝倒下来时扫中我的胸口，把我刮倒在地。我仰面摔倒，大象从我躺着的地方，跌跌撞撞冲了过去。我多半是凭本能，单手举起枪，扣了扳机。枪响了；后来我才知道，子弹打中了他的肋骨。可是这样单手擎着这支沉重的步枪，后坐力凶猛之极：它把我一条胳膊生生拗弯，枪托*砰*地撞上我的肩头和颈侧，一时间把我打得瘫软，枪也从手里震脱了。这当口那公象兀自向前猛冲。他跑出约莫二十步，忽然站住了。我迷迷糊糊地想：他是要回来结果我的。可纵然惨死就在眼前，也提不动我一根汗毛。我彻底脱了力，动弹不得。

“我近乎痴呆地、几乎是漠然地，望着他的动静。他站了一瞬，随即一声声吼得满天回响，然后极慢、极庄重地，跪了下去。到这里，我便人事不知了。

===Original===
“When I came to myself again I saw from the moon that I must have been insensible for quite two hours. I was drenched with dew, and shivering all over. At first I could not think where I was, when, on lifting my head, I saw the outline of the one-tusked bull still kneeling some five-and-twenty paces from me. Then I remembered. Slowly I raised myself, and was instantly taken with a violent sickness, the result of overexertion, after which I very nearly fainted a second time. Presently I grew better, and considered the position. Two of the elephants were, as I knew, dead; but how about No. 3? There he knelt in majesty in the lonely moonlight. The question was, was he resting, or dead? I rose on my hands and knees, loaded my rifle, and painfully crept a few paces nearer. I could see his eye now, for the moonlight fell full upon it﻿—it was open, and rather prominent. I crouched and watched; the eyelid did not move, nor did the great brown body, or the trunk, or the ear, or the tail﻿—nothing moved. Then I knew that he must be dead.

“I crept up to him, still keeping the rifle well forward, and gave him a thump, reflecting as I did so how very near I had been to being thumped instead of thumping. He never stirred; certainly he was dead, though to this day I do not know if it was my random shot that killed him, or if he died from concussion of the brain consequent upon the tremendous shock of his contact with the tree. Anyhow, there he was. Cold and beautiful he lay, or rather knelt, as the poet neatly puts it. Indeed, I do not think that I have ever seen a sight more imposing in its way than that of the mighty beast crouched in majestic death, and shone upon by the lonely moon.

===Chinese===
“再醒过来时，我从月亮上看出，自己准是昏死了足足两个钟头。我浑身叫露水浸透，抖个不停。起初想不起身在何处；一抬头，望见那头独牙公象的轮廓，还在离我约二十五步的地方跪着。于是我记起来了。我慢慢撑起半身，立刻翻肠倒肚地大呕起来——那是用力过度的结果——呕完，又险些第二次晕过去。过了一会儿好受了些，我便估量眼下的局面。两头象，据我所知，是死了；可三号怎样？他就在那儿，威威严严地跪在一片孤月清光里。问题是：他是在歇息，还是死了？我双手双膝撑起身，装好弹药，忍着痛往前爬了几步。这时我看得见他的眼睛了——月光正照在上面——眼睛睁着，还挺凸出。我蹲着守望：眼皮不动，那庞大的棕色身躯也不动，鼻子、耳朵、尾巴，一概不动。于是我明白，他必定是死了。

“我爬到他跟前，枪仍旧端在前头，朝他擂了一拳，一边寻思：方才差一点儿，就不是我擂他、而是他擂我了。他纹丝不动；确实是死了。不过直到今天我也不知道，究竟是我那记瞎打瞎撞的枪弹结果了他，还是他一头撞树的那记大震荡震坏了脑子，送了他的命。反正，他就在那儿。清冷而美丽，他躺在那儿——或者照那位诗人的妙语，跪在那儿。说真的，我想我从未见过比这更有气派的景象：那庞然大物威严地伏尸而死，孤月照临其上。

===Original===
“While I stood admiring the scene, and heartily congratulating myself upon my escape, once more I began to feel sick. Accordingly, without waiting to examine the other two bulls, I staggered back to the camp, which in due course I reached in safety. Everybody in it was asleep. I did not wake them, but having swallowed a mouthful of brandy I threw off my coat and shoes, rolled myself up in a blanket, and was soon fast asleep.

“When I woke it was already light, and at first I thought that, like Joseph, I had dreamed a dream. At that moment, however, I turned my head, and quickly knew that it was no dream, for my neck and face were so stiff from the blow of the butt-end of the rifle that it was agony to move them. I collapsed for a minute or two. Gobo and another man, wrapped up like a couple of monks in their blankets, thinking that I was still asleep, were crouched over a little fire they had made, for the morning was damp and chilly, and holding sweet converse.

===Chinese===
“我正站在那儿赏玩这景象，由衷庆幸自己死里逃生，忽而又恶心起来。于是我顾不上查看另外两头公象，踉踉跄跄回营去了，一路平安到了营里。营里人人都在酣睡。我没有叫醒他们，灌下一口白兰地，脱掉外衣和鞋，裹紧毯子，很快便睡熟了。

“醒来时天已大亮，起初我还当同约瑟一样，是做了一场梦。可一转头，立刻知道不是梦：枪托撞过的地方，脖子和脸僵得很，一动就痛得钻心。我颓然倒了一两分钟。戈博和另一个人，裹在毯子里活像两个修士，只当我还没醒，正蹲在他们生的一小堆火边——早晨又湿又冷——说着体己话。

===Original===
“Gobo said that he was getting tired of running after elephants which they never caught. Macumazahn (that is, myself) was without doubt a man of parts, and of some skill in shooting, but also he was a fool. None but a fool would run so fast and far after elephants which it was impossible to catch, when they kept cutting the spoor of fresh ones. He certainly was a fool, but he must not be allowed to continue in his folly; and he, Gobo, had determined to put a stop to it. He should refuse to accompany him any further on so mad a hunt.

“ ‘Yes,’ the other answered, ‘the poor man certainly was sick in his head, and it was quite time that they checked his folly while they still had a patch of skin left upon their feet. Moreover, he for his part certainly did not like this country of Wambe’s, which really was full of ghosts. Only the last night he had heard the spooks at work﻿—they were out shooting, at least it sounded as though they were. It was very queer, but perhaps their lunatic of a master﻿—’

===Chinese===
“戈博说，追这些永远追不上的大象，他追腻了。马库马赞（就是我）无疑是个有本事的人，枪法上也有几分造诣，可同样无疑，是个傻子。放着一路横在脚下的新鲜象迹不追，偏去追那根本追不上的象——不是傻子，谁这么办？他确实是个傻子，可总不能由着他傻下去；他戈博打定了主意，要煞住这桩事。这样疯下去的追猎，他再不奉陪了。

“‘可不是，’另一个应道，‘那可怜人脑子准是有病，趁脚板上还剩得一块整皮，正该拦他一拦。再者，说句私心话，他也当真不喜欢万贝这地方——这地方实在闹鬼。就在昨儿夜里，他还听见那些鬼魂出来行猎——至少听着像是在放枪。真是邪门儿。不过，兴许咱们那位疯东家——’

===Original===
“ ‘Gobo, you scoundrel!’ I shouted out at this juncture, sitting bolt upright on the blankets, ‘stop idling there and make me some coffee.’

“Up sprang Gobo and his friend, and in half a moment were respectfully skipping about in a manner that contrasted well with the lordly contempt of their previous conversation. But all the time they were in earnest in what they said about hunting the elephants any further, for before I had finished my coffee they came to me in a body, and said that if I wanted to follow those elephants I must follow them myself, for they would not go.

“I argued with them, and affected to be much put out. The elephants were close at hand, I said; I was sure of it; I had heard them trumpet in the night.

“ ‘Yes,’ answered the men mysteriously, ‘they too had heard things in the night, things not nice to hear; they had heard the spooks out shooting, and no longer would they remain in a country so vilely haunted.’

===Chinese===
“‘戈博，你这混账东西！’我这时大喝一声，从毯子上笔直坐起，‘别在那儿磨牙了，给我煮咖啡去！’

“戈博和他的朋友一蹦而起，眨眼间便毕恭毕敬地碎步打转，同方才那副居高临下、鄙夷一切的腔调，恰成对照。只是关于再追大象的话，他们倒并非说着玩：我咖啡还没喝完，他们便成群结队来见我，说若我还想追那几头象，请我自己去追，他们是不去了。

“我同他们讲道理，又装出气恼已极的样子。我说，象就近在眼前，我有十分把握；夜里我还听见他们叫唤来着。

“‘是啊，’众人神神秘秘地答道，‘夜里我们也听见了些响动，不是好听的响动；我们听见鬼魂出来放枪了。这样恶鬼缠身的地方，我们再不待了。’

===Original===
“ ‘It was nonsense,’ I replied. ‘If ghosts went out shooting, surely they would use airguns and not black powder, and one would not hear an airgun. Well, if they were cowards, and would not come, of course I could not force them to, but I would make a bargain with them. They should follow those elephants for one half-hour more, then if we failed to come upon them I would abandon the pursuit, and we would go straight to Wambe, chief of the Matuku, and give him *hongo*.’

“To this compromise the men agreed readily. Accordingly about half-an-hour later we struck our camp and started, and notwithstanding my aches and bruises, I do not think that I ever felt in better spirits in my life. It is something to wake up in the morning and remember that in the dead of the night, single-handed, one has given battle to and overthrown three of the largest elephants in Africa, slaying them with three bullets. Such a feat to my knowledge had never been done before, and on that particular morning I felt a very ‘tall man of my hands’ indeed. The only thing I feared was, that should I ever come to tell the story nobody would believe it, for when a strange tale is told by a hunter, people are apt to think it is necessarily a lie, instead of being only probably so.2

===Chinese===
“‘一派胡言，’我答道，‘鬼魂出来打猎，自然使气枪，不会使黑火药；气枪是听不见响的。也罢，你们要是胆小鬼，不肯去，我自然强求不得；不过咱们不妨做笔交易：再跟那几头象半个钟头，若是还追不上，我便收手，咱们径直去见马图库人的酋长万贝，给他送上*洪戈*（贡金）。’

“这个折中办法，众人欣然应承。于是约莫半个钟头后，我们拔营动身。尽管周身酸痛、遍体青紫，我想我平生再没有比那天早晨更意气风发的了。清早一觉醒来，想起自己半夜三更孤身一人，同非洲数得着的三头巨象交手，并且尽数放翻，三颗子弹要了三条性命——这可不是小事。据我所知，这样的壮举前无古人；那天早晨，我自觉当真是个‘身手了得的人物’了。我唯一担心的是，有朝一日把这故事讲出来，没人肯信——猎人的奇谈，人们总以为必定是谎话，而不是仅仅多半是谎话。2

===Original===
“Well, we passed on till, having crossed the first glade where I had seen the lions, we reached the neck of bush that separated it from the second glade, where the dead elephants were. And here I began to take elaborate precautions, amongst others ordering Gobo to keep some yards ahead and look out sharp, as I thought that the elephants might be about. He obeyed my instructions with a superior smile, and pushed ahead. Presently I saw him pull up as though he had been shot, and begin to snap his fingers faintly.

“ ‘What is it?’ I whispered.

“ ‘The elephant, the great elephant with one tusk kneeling down.’

===Chinese===
“我们一路前行，穿过我见过狮子的头一片草地，到了隔开它和第二片草地的那道灌木颈口——死象就在那边草地上。到了这里，我开始周密设防，其中一项，是吩咐戈博走在前头隔开几码，留神瞭望，因为我想那几头象或许还在左近。他带着一丝居高临下的微笑，遵命赶上前去。不多时，我见他猛地煞住脚，像中了枪似的，轻轻打起响指来。

“‘什么事？’我低声问。

“‘象，那头跪着的大独牙象。’

===Original===
“I crept up beside him. There knelt the bull as I had left him last night, and there too lay the other bulls.

“ ‘Do these elephants sleep?’ I whispered to the astonished Gobo.

“ ‘Yes, Macumazahn, they sleep.’

===Chinese===
“我爬到他身旁。公象还跪在我昨夜离开时的老地方，另外两头也躺在那儿。

“‘这些象在睡觉么？’我悄声问，问那惊得目瞪口呆的戈博。

“‘在睡，马库马赞，他们在睡。’

===Original===
“ ‘Nay, Gobo, they are dead.’

“ ‘Dead? How can they be dead? Who killed them?’

“ ‘What do people call me, Gobo?’

===Chinese===
“‘不，戈博，他们死了。’

“‘死了？怎么会死？谁杀的？’

“‘人家都管你叫什么，戈博？’

===Original===
“ ‘They call you Macumazahn.’

“ ‘And what does Macumazahn mean?’

“ ‘It means the man who keeps his eyes open, the man who gets up in the night.’

===Chinese===
“‘管您叫马库马赞。’

“‘马库马赞是什么意思？’

“‘意思是睁着眼睛的人，夜里起身的人。’

===Original===
“ ‘Yes, Gobo, and I am that man. Look, you idle, lazy cowards; while you slept last night I rose, and alone I hunted those great elephants, and slew them by the moonlight. To each of them I gave one bullet and only one, and it fell dead. Look,’ and I advanced into the glade, ‘here is my spoor, and here is the spoor of the great bull charging after me, and there is the tree that I took refuge behind; see, the elephant shattered it in his charge. Oh, you cowards, you who would give up the chase while the blood spoor steamed beneath your nostrils, see what I did single-handed while you slept, and be ashamed.’

“ ‘*Ou!*’ said the men, ‘*ou! Koos! Koos y umcool!*’ (Chief, great Chief!) And then they held their tongues, and going up to the three dead beasts, gazed upon them in silence.

===Chinese===
“‘正是，戈博，我就是那个人。看吧，你们这些游手好闲的懒骨头、胆小鬼：昨夜你们睡觉的时候，我起来了；我独自一人去追那几头巨象，在月光底下宰了他们。他们每头，我只给了一颗子弹，仅仅一颗，便一头头倒了下去。看，’我一边走进草地，‘这是我的脚印，这是那大公象追赶我的脚印，那边是我藏身的那棵树——瞧，叫他一头撞得粉碎。哼，你们这些胆小鬼，血迹还在你们鼻子底下冒着热气，你们倒想撒手不追了！看看我趁你们睡觉时独力干下的事，羞也羞死了。’

“‘*Ou!*’众人喊道，‘*ou! Koos! Koos y umcool!*’（酋长，伟大的酋长！）说完他们便闭了口，走到三头死兽跟前，默默凝视。

===Original===
“But after that those men looked upon me with awe as being almost more than mortal. No mere man, they said, could have slain those three elephants alone in the nighttime. I never had any further trouble with them. I believe that if I had told them to jump over a precipice and that they would take no harm, they would have believed me.

“Well, I went up and examined the bulls. Such tusks as they had I never saw and never shall see again. It took us all day to cut them out; and when they reached Delagoa Bay, as they did ultimately, though not in my keeping, the single tusk of the big bull scaled one hundred and sixty pounds, and the four other tusks averaged ninety-nine and a half pounds﻿—a most wonderful, indeed an almost unprecedented, lot of ivory.3 Unfortunately I was forced to saw the big tusk in two, otherwise we could not have carried it.”

===Chinese===
“打那以后，那些人看我便带着敬畏，几乎当我不似凡人。他们说，凡人决计不能夜里独自一人，杀死那三头象。此后我再没同他们多费过唇舌。我相信，我若叫他们跳下悬崖，保他们毫发无伤，他们也会信的。

“随后我走上去，查看那几头公象。那样的象牙，我从未见过，往后也再见不着了。我们花了一整天，才把象牙剔出来。它们后来运到了德拉瓜湾——终究是运到了，虽不曾经我手——大公象那支独牙称得一百六十磅，另外四支平均九十九磅半：堪称稀世、几乎空前的一批象牙。3 不巧，那支大独牙我不得不锯成两截，不然我们没法搬运。

---

===Original===
“Oh, Quatermain, you barbarian!” I broke in here, “the idea of spoiling such a tusk! Why, I would have kept it whole if I had been obliged to drag it myself.”

“Oh yes, young man,” he answered, “it is all very well for you to talk like that, but if you had found yourself in the position which it was my privilege to occupy a few hours afterwards, it is my belief that you would have thrown the tusks away altogether and taken to your heels.”

“Oh,” said Good, “so that isn’t the end of the yarn? A very good yarn, Quatermain, by the way﻿—I couldn’t have made up a better one myself.”

===Chinese===
“‘噢，夸特梅因，你这个野人！’我在这儿忍不住插嘴，‘糟蹋这么一支象牙，亏你下得去手！换了是我，哪怕自己拽，也要把它囫囵个儿弄走。’

“‘噢，是啊，年轻人，’他答道，‘你说得倒轻巧。可你若处在几个钟头后、我有幸光顾的那个境地，我相信你会把象牙丢个精光，撒腿就跑。”

“‘哦，’古德说，‘这么说，故事还没完？顺便说一句，夸特梅因，这故事真不赖——连我自己也编不出更精彩的。’

===Original===
The old gentleman looked at Good severely, for it irritated him to be chaffed about his stories.

“I don’t know what you mean, Good. I don’t see that there is any comparison between a true story of adventure and the preposterous tales which you invent about ibex hanging by their horns. No, it is not the end of the story; the most exciting part is to come. But I have talked enough for tonight; and if you go on in that way, Good, it will be some time before I begin again.”

“Sorry I spoke, I’m sure,” said Good, humbly. “Let’s have a split to show that there is no ill-feeling.” And they did.

===Chinese===
老绅士狠狠瞪了古德一眼，因为拿他的故事打趣，最叫他着恼。

“‘我不知道你这话什么意思，古德。一段千真万确的冒险故事，同你编的那些什么北山羊倒挂着角一类的荒唐透顶的玩意儿，我看不出有什么可比之处。不，故事还没完；顶精彩的部分还在后头。不过今晚我讲得够多了；你要再这样，古德，可就得过些日子，我才肯往下讲。’

“‘怪我多嘴，怪我多嘴，’古德低眉顺眼地说，‘咱们分喝一瓶，表明彼此并无芥蒂吧。’他们当真喝了。

## V The Message of Maiwa / 五 迈瓦的信

===Original===

On the following evening we once more dined together, and Quatermain, after some pressure, was persuaded to continue his story﻿—for Good’s remark still rankled in his breast.

===Chinese===

第二天晚上，我们又聚在一处吃饭；夸特梅因让人劝了一阵，终于肯把故事讲下去——因为古德那句话，还哽在他心口。

---

===Original===

“At last,” he went on, “a few minutes before sunset, the task was finished. We had laboured at it all day, stopping only once for dinner, for it is no easy matter to hew out five such tusks as those which now lay before me in a white and gleaming line. It was a dinner worth eating, too, I can tell you, for we dined off the heart of the great one-tusked bull, which was so big that the man whom I sent inside the elephant to look for his heart was forced to remove it in two pieces. We cut it into slices and fried it with fat, and I never tasted heart to equal it, for the meat seemed to melt in one’s mouth. By the way, I examined the jaw of the elephant; it never grew but one tusk; the other had not been broken off, nor was it present in a rudimentary form.

“Well, there lay the five beauties, or rather four of them, for Gobo and another man were engaged in sawing the grand one in two. At last with many sighs I ordered them to do this, but not until by practical experiment I had proved that it was impossible to carry it in any other way. One hundred and sixty pounds of solid ivory, or rather more in its green state, is too great a weight for two men to bear for long across a broken country. I sat watching the job and smoking the pipe of contentment, when suddenly the bush opened, and a very handsome and dignified native girl, apparently about twenty years of age, stood before me, carrying a basket of green mealies upon her head.

“Although I was rather surprised to see a native girl in such a wild spot, and, so far as I knew, a long way from any kraal, the matter did not attract my particular notice; I merely called to one of the men, and told him to bargain with the woman for the mealies, and ask her if there were any more to be bought in the neighbourhood. Then I turned my head and continued to superintend the cutting of the tusk. Presently a shadow fell upon me. I looked up, and saw that the girl was standing before me, the basket of mealies still on her head.

===Chinese===

“到底，”他接着讲道，“日落前几分钟，活计完事了。我们为它忙了一整天，只停过一回吃午饭；要从象头上取出五支那样的象牙——它们此刻正白晃晃、亮晶晶地在我面前排成一行——原不是桩轻省活。那顿饭也真值得吃吃，我告诉你们：我们吃的是那头独牙老公象的心。那颗心大得出奇，我派进象肚子里找心的人，只得把它卸成两块，才取得出来。我们把它切成片，用油煎了；我平生没尝过那样好的心，那肉到了嘴里简直就化。顺便说一句，我查验过那头象的牙床：它天生只出这一支牙，另一支并非折断，也不曾以雏形藏在里头。

“喏，五件宝贝就躺在那儿——或者说四件，因为戈博同另一个人，正忙着把那支顶大的锯成两截。我叹息了半天，才终于吩咐他们这么办；不过在那之前，我已用实际试验证明，换任何别的法子都带不动它。一百六十磅实打实的象牙——鲜着称还要更重些——这样的分量，要两个人在崎岖地面上远扛，未免太沉。我坐着监工，吧嗒着心满意足的烟斗，忽然树丛一分，一个眉目俊秀、仪态庄重的土人姑娘站到了我面前，看年纪约莫二十岁，头顶着一篮青玉米。

“虽说在这样荒僻的所在见到一个土人姑娘——而且据我所知，此处离哪座克拉尔都远得很——颇为意外，这事却没引起我特别的留意；我不过唤过一个人，叫他同那女人讲价钱买玉米，再问问左近还有没有可买的。说完我便掉回头，接着督着锯象牙。不多时，一片影子罩到我身上。我抬头一看，那姑娘正立在我跟前，玉米篮子仍旧顶在头上。

===Original===

“ ‘*Marême*, *Marême*,’ she said, gently clapping her hands together. The word *Marême* among these Matuku (though she was no Matuku) answers to the Zulu *Koos*, and the clapping of hands is a form of salutation very common among the tribes of the Basutu race.

“ ‘What is it, girl?’ I asked her in Sisutu. ‘Are those mealies for sale?’

“ ‘No, great white hunter,’ she answered in Zulu, ‘I bring them as a gift.’

“ ‘Good,’ I replied; ‘set them down.’

===Chinese===

“‘马雷梅（Marême），马雷梅，’她轻轻拍着双手说道。在马图库人中间——虽说她并非马图库人——‘马雷梅’这个词相当于祖鲁语的‘库斯（Koos）’；拍手则是巴苏陀各族里极通行的一种见礼。

“‘什么事，姑娘？’我用西苏图语问她。‘这玉米是卖的么？’

“‘不卖，伟大的白人猎手，’她用祖鲁语答道，‘我是拿来当礼物送的。’

“‘好，’我说，‘放下吧。’

===Original===

“ ‘A gift for a gift, white man.’

“ ‘Ah,’ I grumbled, ‘the old story﻿—nothing for nothing in this wicked world. What do you want﻿—beads?’

“She nodded, and I was about to tell one of the men to go and fetch some from one of the packs, when she checked me.

“ ‘A gift from the giver’s own hand is twice a gift,’ she said, and I thought that she spoke meaningly.

===Chinese===

“‘礼物换礼物，白人。’”

“‘啊，’我咕哝道，‘老规矩——这万恶的世上，就没有白给的东西。你想要什么——珠子？’”

“她点了点头，我正要吩咐一个人到驮包里去取，她却把我拦住了。”

“‘亲手里交出的礼物，才抵得两份礼物，’她说；我觉得她这话别有深意。”

===Original===

“ ‘You mean that you want me to give them to you myself?’

“ ‘Surely.’

“I rose to go with her. ‘How is it that, being of the Matuku, you speak in the Zulu tongue?’ I asked suspiciously.

“ ‘I am not of the Matuku,’ she answered as soon as we were out of hearing of the men. ‘I am of the people of Nala, whose tribe is the Butiana tribe, and who lives there,’ and she pointed over the mountain. ‘Also I am one of the wives of Wambe,’ and her eyes flashed as she said the name.

“ ‘And how did you come here?’

===Chinese===

“‘你是说，要我亲手把珠子给你？’”

“‘正是。’”

我起身同她走去。“你既是马图库人，怎么说起祖鲁话来？”我满腹狐疑地问。

“‘我不是马图库人，’一出男人们的听力所及，她便答道，‘我是纳拉的人。他的部族是布蒂亚纳部族，就住在那边，’她朝山那边一指。‘我还是万贝的一名妻子。’说出这个名字时，她双眼放光。”

“‘那你是怎么到这里来的？’”

===Original===

“ ‘On my feet,’ she answered laconically.

“We reached the packs, and undoing one of them, I extracted a handful of beads. ‘Now,’ I said, ‘a gift for a gift. Hand over the mealies.’

“She took the beads without even looking at them, which struck me as curious, and setting the basket of mealies on the ground, emptied it.

“At the bottom of the basket were some curiously-shaped green leaves, rather like the leaves of the gutta-percha tree in shape, only somewhat thicker and of a more fleshy substance. As though by hazard, the girl picked one of these leaves out of the basket and smelt it. Then she handed it to me. I took the leaf, and supposing that she wished me to smell it also, was about to oblige her by doing so, when my eye fell upon some curious red scratches on the green surface of the leaf.

===Chinese===

“‘用这双脚走来的，’她答得干净利落。”

我们走到驮包跟前，解开其中一包，我抓出一把珠子。“好了，”我说，“礼物换礼物，玉米交出来吧。”

她接过珠子，看也不看一眼，这叫我暗暗称奇；她把玉米篮放到地上，将它倒空了。

篮底躺着一些形状古怪的绿叶，模样颇像古塔波胶树的叶子，只是略厚一些，也更多肉。那姑娘像出于偶然，从篮里拈起一片，闻了闻，然后递给我。我接过叶子，只当她要我领这个情、也闻一闻，正要照办，眼睛却落在叶面几道奇怪的红划痕上。

===Original===

“ ‘Ah,’ said the girl (whose name, by the way, was Maiwa), speaking beneath her breath, ‘read the signs, white man.’

“Without answering her I continued to stare at the leaf. It had been scratched or rather written upon with a sharp tool, such as a nail, and wherever this instrument had touched it, the acid juice oozing through the outer skin had turned a rusty blood colour. Presently I found the beginning of the scrawl, and read this in English, and covering the surface of the leaf and of two others that were in the basket.

===Chinese===

“‘啊，’那姑娘压低嗓子说（顺便交代一句，她名叫迈瓦），‘白人，读一读这些记号。’”

我没有答她，只顾盯住那片叶子。叶上被一种尖利的家伙——比如一枚钉子——划过，或者说写过；那铁器划到哪里，沁出外皮的酸汁便在哪里转成一种铁锈似的血色。不多时，我找到那篇歪斜字迹的开头，用英文读了下去；字迹写满了这一片叶子，也写满篮中另外两片。

===Original===

> “ ‘I hear that a white man is hunting in the Matuku country. This is to warn him to fly over the mountain to Nala. Wambe sends an *impi* at daybreak to eat him up, because he has hunted before bringing *hongo*. For God’s sake, whoever you are, try to help me. I have been the slave of this devil Wambe for nearly seven years, and am beaten and tortured continually. He murdered all the rest of us, but kept me because I could work iron. Maiwa, his wife, takes this; she is flying to Nala her father because Wambe killed her child. Try to get Nala to attack Wambe; Maiwa can guide them over the mountain. You won’t come for nothing, for the stockade of Wambe’s private kraal is made of elephants’ tusks. For God’s sake, don’t desert me, or I shall kill myself. I can bear this no longer.
> “ ‘John Every.’

===Chinese===

> “‘我听说有个白人正在马图库人的地界里打猎。特此警告他火速翻山，逃往纳拉处。万贝派出一支战队（impi），天一亮便来吞吃他，因为他未曾先行进献洪戈（hongo）便擅自行猎。看在上帝分上，不论你是谁，求你想办法救我。我给这个魔鬼万贝为奴，将满七年，挨打受刑，无日无之。他把我们其余的人尽数杀了，独独留下我，因为我能打铁。捎信的是他的妻子迈瓦；她正逃往她父亲纳拉那里，因为万贝杀了她的孩子。设法说动纳拉来攻万贝；迈瓦能引他们翻山。你此来决不落空，因为万贝私寨的栅栏，是拿大象牙做的。看在上帝分上，千万别丢下我，否则我便自杀。我实在受不住了。
> “‘约翰·埃弗里。’

===Original===

“ ‘Great heavens!’ I gasped. ‘Every!﻿—why, it must be my old friend.’ The girl, or rather the woman Maiwa, pointed to the other side of the leaf, where there was more writing. It ran thus﻿—‘I have just heard that the white man is called Macumazahn. If so, it must be my friend Quatermain. Pray Heaven it is, for I know he won’t desert an old chum in such a fix as I am. It isn’t that I’m afraid of dying, I don’t care if I die, but I want to get a chance at Wambe first.’

“ ‘No, old boy,’ thought I to myself, ‘it isn’t likely that I am going to leave you there while there is a chance of getting you out. I have played fox before now﻿—there’s still a double or two left in me. I must make a plan, that’s all. And then there’s that stockade of tusks. I am not going to leave that either.’ Then I spoke to the woman.

===Chinese===

“‘我的老天！’我倒抽一口气。‘埃弗里！——呀，这准是我那位老朋友。’那姑娘——或者不如说那位妇人迈瓦——指着叶子的另一面，那里还有字。是这样写的——‘我刚听说，那白人名叫马库马赞。若是如此，必是我的朋友夸特梅因。求上天保佑正是他，因为我知道，落到我这般田地的老伙计，他是决不会弃之不顾的。倒不是我怕死；死，我不在乎；我只是想先捞着一个亲手对付万贝的机会。’”

“‘不会的，老伙计，’我心里对自己说，‘只要有几分把你弄出来的指望，我就断不至于把你撂在那儿。我从前也扮过狐狸——肚里如今还剩得一两个回合。我只消定出一条计策来，如此而已。再说，还有那道象牙栅栏呢。那玩意儿我也不打算留下。’随后我便向那妇人开口。

===Original===

“ ‘You are called Maiwa?’

“ ‘It is so.’

“ ‘You are the daughter of Nala and the wife of Wambe?’

“ ‘It is so.’

“ ‘You fly from Wambe to Nala?’

“ ‘I do.’

===Chinese===

“‘你叫迈瓦？’”

“‘正是。’”

“‘你是纳拉的女儿、万贝的妻子？’”

“‘正是。’”

“‘你从万贝那里逃往纳拉？’”

“‘是。’”

===Original===

“ ‘Why do you fly? Stay, I would give an order,’﻿—and calling to Gobo, I ordered him to get the men ready for instant departure. The woman, who, as I have said, was quite young and very handsome, put her hand into a little pouch made of antelope hide which she wore fastened round the waist, and to my horror drew from it the withered hand of a child, which evidently had been carefully dried in the smoke.

“ ‘I fly for this cause,’ she answered, holding the poor little hand towards me. ‘See now, I bore a child. Wambe was its father, and for eighteen months the child lived and I loved it. But Wambe loves not his children; he kills them all. He fears lest they should grow up to slay one so wicked, and he would have killed this child also, but I begged its life. One day, some soldiers passing the hut saw the child and saluted him, calling him the “chief who soon shall be.” Wambe heard, and was mad. He smote the babe, and it wept. Then he said that it should weep for good cause. Among the things that he had stolen from the white men whom he slew is a trap that will hold lions. So strong is the trap that four men must stand on it, two on either side, before it can be opened.’ ”

===Chinese===

“‘你为什么要逃？且慢，我先交代一道命令，’——我唤过戈博，吩咐他把人立刻收拾停当，随时开拔。那妇人，如前所说，正当妙龄，又生得十分标致，这时把手伸进一只系在腰间的小袋——羚羊皮做的——竟从中摸出一只孩子的干瘪的手来，一望便知是细细烟熏过的，我见了大骇。

“‘我为这个缘故逃，’她答道，一面把那只可怜的小手举向我。‘你听着：我生过一个孩子，万贝是他的父亲。孩子活了十八个月，我爱他。可万贝不爱自己的孩子；他把他们统统杀掉。他怕他们长大了，会杀他这样一个恶人。这一个他本来也要杀，是我求下来这条命。有一天，几个士兵路过茅屋，看见了孩子，向他行礼，称他“不久就要即位的首领”。万贝听见了，登时发了疯。他打了那婴孩，孩子哭了。他便说，该叫孩子哭出个正经缘由来。在他从被害的白人们那里抢来的东西里，有一件捉得住狮子的铁夹。那夹子结实得很，得四个人踩在上头，一边两个，才掰得开。’”

---

===Original===

Here old Quatermain broke off suddenly.

“Look here, you fellows,” he said, “I can’t bear to go on with this part of the story, because I never could stand either seeing or talking of the sufferings of children. You can guess what that devil did, and what the poor mother was forced to witness. Would you believe it, she told me the tale without a tremor, in the most matter-of-fact way. Only I noticed that her eyelid quivered all the time.

===Chinese===

讲到这里，老夸特梅因猛地收住了话头。

“听我说，你们几位，”他说道，“这段我讲不下去了，因为孩子的苦难，我无论是看还是说，都受不住。那魔鬼干了些什么，那位可怜的母亲又被迫眼睁睁看着什么，你们自己去想。信不信由你们：她对我讲这段事，连一丝哆嗦都没有，平平常常，像说件家常。只是我留意到，她的眼皮自始至终一直在颤。”

===Original===

“ ‘Well,’ I said, as unconcernedly as though I had been talking of the death of a lamb, though inwardly I was sick with horror and boiling with rage, ‘and what do you mean to do about the matter, Maiwa, wife of Wambe?’

“ ‘I mean to do this, white man,’ she answered, drawing herself up to her full height, and speaking in tones as hard as steel and cold as ice﻿—‘I mean to work, and work, and work, to bring this to pass, and to bring that to pass, until at length it comes to pass that with these living eyes I behold Wambe dying the death that he gave to his child and my child.’

“ ‘Well said,’ I answered.

“ ‘Ay, well said, Macumazahn, well said, and not easily forgotten. Who could forget, oh, who could forget? See where this dead hand rests against my side; so once it rested when alive. And now, though it is dead, now every night it creeps from its nest and strokes my hair and clasps my fingers in its tiny palm. Every night it does this, fearing lest I should forget. Oh, my child! my child! ten days ago I held thee to my breast, and now this alone remains of thee,’ and she kissed the dead hand and shivered, but never a tear did she weep.

===Chinese===

“‘好，’我说，口气平常得像在讲一头羊羔的死——虽然我心里恶心得发慌，怒火直往上顶——‘这件事你打算怎么办，迈瓦，万贝的妻子？’”

“‘我打算这么办，白人，’她答道，把身子挺到十足的高度，声调硬得像钢，冷得像冰——‘我打算做下去，做下去，做下去，促成这一件，再促成那一件，直到最后，用我这双活着的眼睛，亲眼看万贝死去——死得同他加给他孩子、加给我孩子的死法一模一样。’”

“‘说得好，’我答道。”

“‘是啊，说得好，马库马赞，说得好，这些话轻易忘不掉。谁能忘掉呢，啊，谁能忘掉？瞧这只死手贴在我身侧的地方；它活着的时候，也是这样贴着的。如今它虽然死了，每到夜里，却会从它的窝里爬出来，摸我的头发，用它小小的手心攥住我的手指。它夜夜都这样做，生怕我忘掉。啊，我的孩子！我的孩子！十天前我还把你搂在胸口，如今你只剩下这一点点，’说着，她吻了吻那只死手，打了个寒噤，却一滴眼泪也没有落。

===Original===

“ ‘See now,’ she went on, ‘the white man, the prisoner at Wambe’s kraal, he was kind to me. He loved the child that is dead, yes, he wept when its father slew it, and at the risk of his life told Wambe, my husband﻿—ah, yes, my husband!﻿—that which he is! He too it was who made a plan. He said to me, “Go, Maiwa, after the custom of thy people, go purify thyself in the bush alone, having touched a dead one. Say to Wambe thou goest to purify thyself alone for fifteen days, according to the custom of thy people. Then fly to thy father, Nala, and stir him up to war against Wambe for the sake of the child that is dead.” This then he said, and his words seemed good to me, and that same night ere I left to purify myself came news that a white man hunted in the country, and Wambe, being mad with drink, grew very wrath, and gave orders that an impi should be gathered to slay the white man and his people and seize his goods. Then did the “Smiter of Iron” (Every) write the message on the green leaves, and bid me seek thee out, and show forth the matter, that thou mightest save thyself by flight; and behold, this thing have I done, Macumazahn, the hunter, the Slayer of Elephants.’

“ ‘Ah,’ I said, ‘I thank you. And how many men be there in the impi of Wambe?’

“ ‘A hundred of men and half a hundred.’

“ ‘And where is the impi?’

“ ‘There to the north. It follows on thy spoor. I saw it pass yesterday, but myself I guessed that thou wouldst be nigher to the mountain, and came this way, and found thee. Tomorrow at the daybreak the slayers will be here.’

“ ‘Very possibly,’ I thought to myself; ‘but they won’t find Macumazahn. I have half a mind to put some strychnine into the carcases of those elephants for their especial benefit though.’ I knew that they would stop to eat the elephants, as indeed they did, to our great gain, but I abandoned the idea of poisoning them, because I was rather short of strychnine.”

===Chinese===

“‘再听着，’她继续说道，‘那个白人，万贝克拉尔里的那个囚徒，他待我很好。他爱那个死了的孩子；是的，孩子的父亲下手杀他的时候，他还哭了；他还冒着性命的危险，对万贝、我的丈夫——啊，是啊，我的丈夫！——说出了他是个什么东西！出主意的也是他。他对我说：“去吧，迈瓦，照你族里的规矩，你碰了死人，独自到丛林里去洁净自己。对万贝说，你要照你族里的规矩，独自洁净十五天。然后逃到你父亲纳拉那里去，为了那个死了的孩子，鼓动他向万贝开战。”他就是这么说的，他的话在我听来很有道理。就在我动身去洁净自己的当夜，传来消息，说有个白人在这一带行猎；万贝正醉得发狂，勃然大怒，下令集合一支战队，去杀那白人和他的人，夺取他的财物。于是“打铁人”（埃弗里）把信写在绿叶上，叫我来寻你，把这件事挑明，好让你逃命；你瞧，这件事我办成了，马库马赞，猎手，屠象者。’”

“‘啊，’我说，‘多谢你。万贝的战队有多少人？’”

“‘一百个男人，再加半个一百。’”

“‘战队在哪里？’”

“‘在那边北面。它循着你的踪迹走。昨天我望见它经过；可我自家料定，你必定更贴近山这边，便走了这条道，寻着了你。明日天一亮，那些杀手就要到这里。’”

“‘多半会来，’我心里想；‘可他们休想寻见马库马赞。不过我倒有几分想把些马钱子碱下到那几头象的尸首里，专给他们受用。’我知道他们必停下来吃大象——后来也果真如此，大便宜了我们——可我还是打消了下毒的念头，因为我手头的马钱子碱不很宽裕。”

===Original===

“Or because you did not like to play the trick, Quatermain?” I suggested with a laugh.

“I said because I had not enough strychnine. It would take a great deal of strychnine to poison three elephants effectually,” answered the old gentleman testily.

I said nothing further, but I smiled, knowing that old Allan could never have resorted to such an artifice, however severe his strait. But that was his way; he always made himself out to be a most unmerciful person.

===Chinese===

“要不，是因为你不肯使这一手吧，夸特梅因？”我笑着插话道。

“我说的是马钱子碱不够。要把三头大象毒得透，得用许许多多马钱子碱。”老绅士没好气地答道。

我没有再说下去，只微微一笑，因为我知道，老艾伦无论落到怎样窘迫的境地，也决计使不出这等伎俩。不过他就是这个脾气：总把自己说成一副狠心肠。

---

===Original===

“Well,” he went on, “at that moment Gobo came up and announced that we were ready to march. ‘I am glad that you are ready,’ I said, ‘because if you don’t march, and march quick, you will never march again, that is all. Wambe has an impi out to kill us, and it will be here presently.’

“Gobo turned positively green, and his knees knocked together. ‘Ah, what did I say?’ he exclaimed. ‘Fate walks about loose in Wambe’s country.’

“ ‘Very good; now all you have to do is to walk a little quicker than he does. No, no, you don’t leave those elephant tusks behind﻿—I am not going to part with them I can tell you.’

===Chinese===

““后来，”他接着讲，“这时戈博走上前来，禀报说我们已收拾停当，可以开拔。‘你们收拾好了，我很高兴，’我说，‘因为你们要是不走，而且快快地走，就再也走不成了，就是这么句话。万贝放出一支战队来杀我们，转眼就到。’”

““戈博的脸当场吓成了绿色，两个膝盖捉对儿相磕。‘啊，我说什么来着？’他嚷道。‘命运女神正在万贝的地界里四处游荡。’”

““好得很；眼下你们只有一件事：走得比它快些。不行，不行，这几支象牙你们休想撂下——告诉你们，我可不同它们分手。’”

===Original===

“Gobo said no more, but hastily directed the men to take up their loads, and then asked which way we were to run.

“ ‘Ah,’ I said to Maiwa, ‘which way?’

“ ‘There,’ she answered, pointing towards the great mountain spur which towered up into the sky some forty miles away, separating the territories of Nala and Wambe﻿—‘there, below that small peak, is one place where men may pass, and one only. Also it can easily be blocked from above. If men pass not there, then they must go round the great peak of the mountain, two days’ journey and half a day.’

“ ‘And how far is the peak from us?’

“ ‘All tonight shall you walk and all tomorrow, and if you walk fast, at sunset you shall stand on the peak.’

===Chinese===

“戈博不再言语，慌忙指挥众人上驮，然后问我们该朝哪边跑。”

““啊，”我对迈瓦说，“往哪边走？””

““‘那边，’她答道，指着约四十英里外插天而立的那道大山梁——它隔开了纳拉与万贝的地界——‘那边，那座小孤峰底下，有一处人过得去的地方，仅此一处。再者，从上面也很容易把它堵死。人若打那里过不得，便须绕大山的孤峰，两天又半天的路。’”

“‘那孤峰离我们多远？’”

“‘今夜你须走一整夜，明天一整日；走得快，日落时你便站上孤峰。’”

===Original===

“I whistled, for that meant a five-and-forty miles trudge without sleep. Then I called to the men to take each of them as much cooked elephant’s meat as he could carry conveniently. I did the same myself, and forced the woman Maiwa to eat some as we went. This I did with difficulty, for at that time she seemed neither to sleep nor eat nor rest, so fiercely was she set on vengeance.

“Then we started, Maiwa guiding us. After going for a half-hour over gradually rising ground, we found ourselves on the further edge of a great bush-clad depression something like the bottom of a lake. This depression, through which we had been travelling, was covered with bush to a very great extent, indeed almost altogether so, except where it was pitted with glades such as that wherein I had shot the elephants.

===Chinese===

我吹了声呼哨，因为这意味着四十五英里的苦赶，觉也不得睡。随即我吩咐众人，每人拣熟象肉尽量带，背得动多少带多少。我自己也照办，一路还强劝那妇人迈瓦吃一些。这差事办得吃力，因为那时的她，仿佛不睡、不吃、也不歇，复仇的心火烧得太凶了。

于是我们出发了，由迈瓦引路。在渐渐抬升的地面上走了半个钟头，我们发现自己到了一片洼地的外缘；那洼地广大，遍覆灌木，很像一片湖底。我们一路穿行的这片洼地，望出去几乎尽是灌木，只除了几片零零星星的林间空地，比如我射杀大象的那一片。

===Original===

“At the top of this slope Maiwa halted, and putting her hand over her eyes looked back. Presently she touched me on the arm and pointed across the sea of forest towards a comparatively vacant space of country some six or seven miles away. I looked, and suddenly I saw something flash in the red rays of the setting sun. A pause, and then another quick flash.

“ ‘What is it?’ I asked.

“ ‘It is the spears of Wambe’s impi, and they travel fast,’ she answered coolly.

“I suppose that my face showed how little I liked the news, for she went on﻿—

“ ‘Fear not; they will stay to feast upon the elephants, and while they feast we shall journey. We may yet escape.’

===Chinese===

走到坡顶，迈瓦站住，抬手搭在眼上回望。忽然她碰了碰我的手臂，隔着那片树的海洋，朝约六七英里外一处相对空旷的地面指去。我望去，猛然看见有什么东西在落日的红光里一闪。停了一停，又是急促的一闪。

“‘那是什么？’我问。”

“‘是万贝战队的矛，它们来得快，’她冷冷地答道。”

想必我的脸色显出了这消息多么不受用，因为她接着说——

“‘别怕；他们要停下来吃大象。趁他们大嚼，我们赶路。我们兴许还逃得脱。’”

===Original===

“After that we turned and pushed on again, till at length it grew so dark that we had to wait for the rising of the moon, which lost us time, though it gave us rest. Fortunately none of the men had seen that ominous flashing of the spears; if they had, I doubt if even I could have kept control of them. As it was, they travelled faster than I had ever known loaded natives to go before, so thorough-paced was their desire to see the last of Wambe’s country. I, however, took the precaution to march last of all, fearing lest they should throw away their loads to lighten themselves, or, worse still, the tusks; for these kind of fellows would be capable of throwing anything away if their own skins were at stake. If the pious Aeneas, whose story you were reading to me the other night, had been a mongrel Delagoa Bay native, Anchises would have had a poor chance of getting out of Troy, that is, if he was known to have made a satisfactory will.

“At moonrise we set out again, and with short occasional halts travelled till dawn, when we were forced to rest and eat. Starting once more, about half-past five, we crossed the river at noon. Then began the long toilsome ascent through thick bush, the same in which I shot the bull buffalo, only some twenty miles to the west of that spot, and not more than twenty-five miles on the hither side of Wambe’s kraal. There were six or seven miles of this dense bush, and hard work it was to get through it. Next came a belt of scattered forest which was easier to pass, though, in revenge, the ground was steeper. This was about two miles wide, and we passed it by about four in the afternoon. Above this scattered bush lay a long steep slope of boulder-strewn ground, which ran up to the foot of the little peak some three miles away. As we emerged, footsore and weary, on to this inhospitable plain, some of the men looking round caught sight of the spears of Wambe’s impi advancing rapidly not more than a mile behind us.

===Chinese===

此后我们转身再赶，直走到天黑透，不得不候月亮上来；这误了工夫，倒叫人得些歇息。万幸，谁也不曾看见那阵不祥的矛光；若是看见了，我怕连我也弹压不住他们。饶是如此，他们行路之快，胜过我素来所知的任何负重土人——想赶紧甩脱万贝地界的那份心，实在太切。至于我，特意押在最后，生怕他们抛掉驮子轻身，更糟的是抛掉象牙；因为这一路货色，一旦自己那张皮要紧起来，是什么都抛得出去的。倘若那位虔诚的埃涅阿斯——前几夜你们还念他的故事给我听——生来是个德拉瓜湾的杂混土人，那么安喀塞斯想逃出特洛伊，机会可就渺茫了；当然，还得人家知道，老头子立下过一份叫人满意的遗嘱。

月上之后我们重新上路，走走歇歇，直走到天明，才不得不停下来打尖进食。五点半左右再度动身，正午涉过了河。接着便开始穿密丛的漫长苦爬——就是我射死那头公水牛的那片密丛，只是在我们以西约二十英里，在万贝克拉尔这一面不过二十五英里。这样的密丛有六七英里，穿过去好不费力。接下来一带疏林，行来容易，可是地更陡了，算是扯个平。疏林约两英里宽，下午四点来钟，我们穿了过去。疏林之上，是一道乱石纵横的长陡坡，一路朝上，直抵三英里外那座小孤峰的脚下。我们拖着两条酸痛的腿，爬上这片不留情面的荒坡时，有几个人回头一望，顿时望见万贝战队的矛正急速逼近，离我们已不足一英里。

===Original===

“At first there was a panic, and the bearers tried to throw off their loads and run, but I harangued them, calling out to them that certainly I would shoot the first man who did so and that if they would but trust in me I would bring them through the mess. Now, ever since I had killed those three elephants single-handed, I had gained great influence over these men, and they listened to me. So off we went as hard as ever we could go﻿—the members of the Alpine Club would not have been in it with us. We made the boulders burn, as a Frenchman would say.

“When we had done about a mile the spears began to emerge from the belt of scattered bush, and the whoop of their bearers as they viewed us broke upon our ears. Quick as our pace had been before, it grew much quicker now, for terror lent wings to my gallant crew. But they were sorely tired, and the loads were heavy, so that run, or rather climb, as we would, Wambe’s soldiers, a scrubby-looking lot of men armed with big spears and small shields, but without plumes, climbed considerably faster. The last mile of that pleasing chase was like a fox hunt, we being the fox, and always in view. What astonished me was the extraordinary endurance and activity shown by Maiwa. She never even flagged. I think that girl’s muscles must have been made of iron, or perhaps it was the strength of her will that supported her. At any rate she reached the foot of the peak second, poor Gobo, who was an excellent hand at running away, being first.

===Chinese===

起先一阵大乱，挑夫们要甩下驮子逃命；我把他们训了一顿，大声说：头一个这么干的，我必定开枪；又说，他们但肯信我，我定带他们闯出这场乱子。自从我独力射杀那三头象以来，我在这些人中间威信大增，他们都听我的。于是我们撒开两腿，能跑多快跑多快——阿尔卑斯俱乐部的会员们也休想同我们争一日之长。照法国人的说法，我们把脚下的石头都跑得冒烟了。

跑出约一英里，矛尖开始从疏林带里冒出来；追兵望见我们，呐喊之声直撞进我们耳朵。先前再快，此刻更快了一倍，恐怖给我这群好汉插上了翅膀。无奈他们实在累狠了，驮子又重；任凭我们怎样跑——不如说怎样爬——万贝的兵还是爬得快出许多：那是一伙獐头鼠目的人，扛大矛，持小盾，头上没有翎毛。那场痛快的追猎，最后一英里活脱是一场猎狐，我们做狐狸，始终摆在猎犬眼前。最叫我惊奇的，是迈瓦那份非常的耐力与脚力：她连一步都不曾懈怠过。我想，那姑娘的筋肉必是铁打的；再不然，便是意志在撑着她。总之，她第二个到达孤峰脚下；头一个到的，是可怜的戈博——逃跑这一道，他本是把好手。

===Original===

“Presently I came up panting, and glanced at the ascent. Before us was a wall of rock about one hundred and fifty feet in height, upon which the strata were laid so as to form a series of projections sufficiently resembling steps to make the ascent easy, comparatively speaking, except at one spot, where it was necessary to climb over a projecting angle of cliff and bear a little to the left. It was not a really difficult place, but what made it awkward was, that immediately beneath this projection gaped a deep fissure or *donga*, on the brink of which we now stood, originally dug out, no doubt, by the rush of water from the peak and cliff. This gulf beneath would be trying to the nerves of a weak-headed climber at the critical point, and so it proved in the result. The projecting angle once passed, the remainder of the ascent was very simple. At the summit, however, the brow of the cliff hung over and was pierced by a single narrow path cut through it by water, in such fashion that a single boulder rolled into it at the top would make the cliff quite impassable to men without ropes.

“At this moment Wambe’s soldiers were about a thousand yards from us, so it was evident that we had no time to lose. I at once ordered the men to commence the ascent, the girl Maiwa, who was familiar with the pass, going first to show them the way. Accordingly they began to mount with alacrity, pushing and lifting their loads in front of them. When the first of them, led by Maiwa, reached the projecting angle, they put down their loads upon a ledge of rock and clambered over. Once there, by lying on their stomachs upon a boulder, they could reach the loads which were held to them by the men beneath, and in this way drag them over the awkward place, whence they were carried easily to the top.

===Chinese===

不多时我也喘着赶到，抬头打量那面崖壁。面前是一堵约一百五十英尺高的石墙，岩层叠出一串台阶似的凸棱，登起来——比较着说——还不算难，只除一处：那里须翻过一道前凸的崖角，再稍稍偏左。这地方本算不得真难，麻烦在于，凸角正下方张着一道深深的裂罅，或者说沟壑（donga）——我们此刻正站在它的口上——无疑原是孤峰与崖壁倾泻的水流冲出来的。悬在这道深渊上方，到了那个节骨眼，脑瓜不坚的人是要发晕的，后来的结果也证明了。翻过凸角，剩下的路便十分简单。只是到了顶上，崖口向前倒悬，被水切出一条窄径穿它而过；窄到什么地步呢——从上面滚进一块巨石，没有绳索的人便休想再上来。

这时万贝的兵离我们约一千码，显而易见，一刻也耽搁不得。我当即吩咐众人开始攀登，由熟悉这道隘口的迈瓦姑娘领头引路。于是他们抖擞精神往上爬，把驮子顶在身前，又推又举。头一批人由迈瓦带着到了凸角跟前，便把驮子搁在一方岩台上，翻了过去。到了那边，他们伏在一块圆石上，便能接住下面的人递上来的驮子，就这样把东西拖过那处险地，再往上抬，便轻巧了。

===Original===

“But all of this took time, and meanwhile the soldiers were coming up fast, screaming and brandishing their big spears. They were now within about four hundred yards, and several loads, together with all the tusks, had yet to be got over the rock. I was still standing at the bottom of the cliff, shouting directions to the men above, but it occurred to me that it would soon be time to move. Before doing so, however, I thought that it might be well to try and produce a moral effect upon the advancing enemy. In my hand I held a Winchester repeating carbine, but the distance was too great for me to use it with effect, so I turned to Gobo, who was shivering with terror at my side, and handing him the carbine, took my express from him.

“The enemy was now about three hundred and fifty yards away, and the express was only sighted to three hundred. Still I knew that it could be trusted for the extra fifty yards. Running in front of Wambe’s soldiers were two men﻿—captains, I suppose﻿—one of them very tall. I put up the three hundred yard flap, and sitting down with my back against the rock, I drew a long breath to steady myself, and covered the tall man, giving him a full sight. Feeling that I was on him, I pulled, and before the sound of the striking bullet could reach my ears, I saw the man throw up his arms and pitch forward on to his head. His companion stopped dead, giving me a fair chance. I rapidly covered him, and fired the left barrel. He turned round once, and then sank down in a heap. This caused the enemy to hesitate﻿—they had never seen men killed at such a distance before, and thought that there was something uncanny about the performance. Taking advantage of the lull, I gave the express back to Gobo, and slinging the Winchester repeater over my back I began to climb the cliff.

===Chinese===

可这些都要工夫，而士兵们喊叫着，挥舞大矛，正飞快逼近。他们已到了四百码之内；还有好几件驮子，连同全部象牙，等着从岩石上转运上去。我还立在崖脚，朝上面的人喊话指点，却也明白，挪窝的时候快到了。不过在动身之前，我想，不妨先给逼近的敌人来一点精神上的效果。我手里端着一支温彻斯特连发卡宾枪，无奈距离太远，用它打不出效力；我便转向在我身边吓得筛糠的戈博，把卡宾枪递给他，换回我的特快步枪。

敌人这时离我们约三百五十码，而特快步枪的标尺只定到三百码。不过我知道，多出的五十码，它也担当得起。跑在万贝士兵前头的有两个人——我猜是队长——其中一个身材极高。我竖起三百码照门，背靠岩石坐下，深深吸一口气稳住自己，罩定那高个子，准星给他吃得足足的。觉得稳稳咬住了，我一扣扳机；子弹中的的声响还未传到耳中，我已看见那人双臂一扬，头朝下载了下去。他的同伴猛地站住，倒给了我一个好目标。我迅速罩住他，放了左管。他原地转了半圈，便瘫作一堆。这一来敌人踌躇了——他们从未见过人在这样远被打死，只道这手绝活里带着邪性。趁这阵迟疑，我把特快步枪还给戈博，把温彻斯特往背上一挎，开始攀崖。

===Original===

“When we reached the projecting angle all the loads were over, but the tusks still had to be passed up, and owing to their weight and the smoothness of their surface, this was a very difficult task. Of course I ought to have abandoned the tusks; often and often have I since reproached myself for not doing so. Indeed, I think that my obstinacy about them was downright sinful, but I was always obstinate about such things, and I could not bear the idea of leaving those splendid tusks which had cost me so much pains and danger to come by. Well, it nearly cost me my life also, and did cost poor Gobo his, as will be seen shortly, to say nothing of the loss inflicted by my rifle on the enemy. When I reached the projection I found that the men, with their usual stupidity, were trying to hand up the tusks point first. Now the result of this was that those above had nothing to grip except the round polished surface of the ivory, and in the position in which they were, this did not give them sufficient hold to enable them to lift the weight. I told them to reverse the tusks and push them up, so that the rough and hollow ends came to the hands of the men above. This they did, and the first two were dragged up in safety.

“At this point, looking behind me, I saw the Matukus streaming up the slope in a rough extended order, and not more than a hundred yards away. Cocking the Winchester I turned and opened fire on them. I don’t quite know how many I missed, but I do know that I never shot better in my life. I had to keep shifting myself from one enemy to the other, firing almost without getting a sight, that is, by the eye alone, after the fashion of the experts who break glass balls. But quick as the work was, men fell thick, and by the time that I had emptied the carbine of its twelve cartridges, for the moment the advance was checked. I rapidly pushed in some more cartridges, and hardly had I done so when the enemy, seeing that we were about to escape them altogether, came on once more with a tremendous yell. By this time the two halves of the single tusk of the great bull alone remained to be passed up. I fired and fired as effectively as before, but notwithstanding all that I could do, some men escaped my hail of bullets and began to ascend the cliff. Presently my rifle was again empty. I slung it over my back, and, drawing my revolver, turned to run for it, the attackers being now quite close. As I did so, a spear struck the cliff close to my head.

===Chinese===

我们到达凸角时，驮子都已运完，只剩象牙还须递上去；它们又重又滑，这是顶难的一桩。照说，我早该把象牙扔下；事后我不止一次为此自责。说真的，我在这上头的固执，简直就是罪过；可我素来在这些事上固执，一想到要丢下这几支好象牙——得来费了我多少心血、冒了多少险——我便委实割舍不下。唉，它们险些也要了我的命，还当真要了可怜的戈博的命，这个下文很快便见分晓；更不必提我的步枪给敌人造成的损失了。我到凸角时，发现那些人正照他们一贯的蠢法，象牙尖朝前地往上递。这样递，上头的人除了象牙那圆溜溜、滑腻腻的表面，没有一点抓处；以他们的姿势，这点抓握，提不起那样的分量。我叫他们倒过头来往上顶，好让粗糙中空的牙根那头，送到上面人的手里。他们照办了，头两支便稳稳当当拖了上去。

正在这时，我回头一望，只见马图库人摆成散乱的横队，涌上坡来，已不到一百码。我扳开温彻斯特的机头，转身朝他们开火。放空了多少，我不很清楚；我只知道，这辈子从没打得这样好过。我不得不从一个敌人飞快转向另一个敌人，几乎来不及瞄准，全凭眼睛估打，就像那些专打碎玻璃球的行家。活儿尽管快，人还是一片一片地倒；等我把卡宾枪里十二发子弹打光，敌人的攻势一时总算顿住了。我急急再压进一些子弹，几乎还没压完，敌人见我们就要整个儿从他们手里溜走，便呐喊一声，再度猛冲上来。这时，只剩那支大独牙锯开的两半，还等着往上运。我一枪一枪，打得同先前一样见效；可是任凭我怎样拦，总有几个人穿过我的弹雨，开始攀崖。不多时，枪又空了。我把它往背上一挎，拔出左轮，转身便跑——攻击者已经逼得很近。就在这时，一支矛擦着崖壁，扎在我脑袋近旁。

===Original===

“The last half of the tusk was now vanishing over the rock, and I sung out to Gobo and the other man who had been pushing it up to vanish after it. Gobo, poor fellow, required no second invitation; indeed, his haste was his undoing. He went at the projecting rock with a bound. The end of the tusk was still hanging over, and instead of grasping the rock he caught at it. It twisted in his hand﻿—he slipped﻿—he fell; with one wild shriek he vanished into the abyss beneath, his falling body brushing me as it passed. For a moment we stood aghast, and presently the dull *thud* of his fall smote heavily upon our ears. Poor fellow, he had met the Fate which, as he declared, walked about loose in Wambe’s country. Then with an oath the remaining man sprung at the rock and clambered over it in safety. Aghast at the awfulness of what had happened, I stood still, till I saw the great blade of a Matuku spear pass up between my feet. That brought me to my senses, and I began to clamber up the rock like a cat. I was halfway round it. Already I had clasped the hand of that brave girl Maiwa, who came down to help me, the men having scrambled forward with the ivory, when I felt someone seize my ankle.

“ ‘Pull, Maiwa, pull,’ I gasped, and she certainly did pull. Maiwa was a very muscular woman, and never before did I appreciate the advantages of the physical development of females so keenly. She tugged at my left arm, the savage below tugged at my right leg, till I began to realize that something must give way ere long. Luckily I retained my presence of mind, like the man who threw his mother-in-law out of the window, and carried the mattress downstairs, when a fire broke out in his house. My right hand was still free, and in it I held my revolver, which was secured to my wrist by a leather thong. The pistol was cocked, and I simply pointed it downwards and fired. The result was instantaneous﻿—and so far as I am concerned, most satisfactory. The bullet hit the man beneath me somewhere, I am sure I don’t know where; at any rate, he let go of my leg and plunged headlong into the gulf beneath to join Gobo. In another moment I was on the top of the rock, and going up the remaining steps like a lamplighter. A single other soldier appeared in pursuit, but one of my boys at the top fired my elephant gun at him. I don’t know if he hit him or only frightened him; at any rate, he vanished whence he came. I do know, however, that he very nearly hit *me*, for I felt the wind of the bullet.

===Chinese===

最后半支象牙正从岩石上消失，我扬声招呼戈博和另一个往上顶象牙的人，跟着它一块儿消失。戈博这可怜虫，何须请第二回；说实在的，坏就坏在这份急劲儿上。他一个箭步扑向那块凸岩。象牙那头还悬在外头；他没去抓岩石，一把抓了象牙。象牙在他手里一拧——他脚下一滑——摔了下去；一声凄厉的喊叫，他坠进了下面的深渊，跌下去的身子擦着我身边过去。我们一时都吓呆了；不多时，他落地的沉闷声响，重重撞进我们耳朵。可怜的人！他到底撞上了照他自己断言、正在万贝地界里四处游荡的那位命运女神。随后剩下那人骂了一声，扑向岩石，安全地翻了过去。出了这样骇人的事，我惊得呆立当场，直到看见一柄马图库长矛的阔刃，从我两脚之间戳上来，才回过神来，像猫一般往岩石上爬。我爬到半途，早已握住那位勇敢姑娘迈瓦的手——她下来帮我，男人们已抬着象牙先上了——忽觉有人攥住了我的脚踝。

“‘拽，迈瓦，快拽，’我喘着气喊；她也当真使劲拽。迈瓦是个膂力过人的女人；女性体力发达的好处，我从来没有哪一刻体会得这般深切。她拽我的左臂，底下那野人拽我的右腿，拽得我渐渐明白：再这样下去，非有什么东西先散架不可。幸而我神智未乱，就像那位失火时先把丈母娘扔出窗外、再把褥垫搬下楼的老兄。我右手还闲着，握着左轮枪，枪用一根皮条拴在手腕上。枪已扳起机头；我只消朝下一指，扣了扳机。效果立竿见影——就我而言，也可算心满意足。那颗子弹打中了我身下那人的什么地方，我确实说不上来；总之，他松开我的腿，一头栽进深渊，寻戈博作伴去了。转瞬之间，我已站上岩石顶，顺着余下的台阶往上跨，轻快得像个点灯人。又有一个士兵追了上来，但上头我的一个小伙子，朝他放了我的猎象枪。是打中了，还是只吓着了他，我不知道；总之，他从哪里来，回哪里去了。我只知道，他差一点打着了我——子弹的风，我都觉察出来了。”

===Original===

“Another thirty seconds, and I and the woman Maiwa were at the top of the cliff panting, but safe.

“My men, being directed thereto by Maiwa, had most fortunately rolled up some big boulders which lay about, and with these we soon managed to block the passage through the overhanging ridge of rock in such fashion that the soldiers below could not possibly climb over it. Indeed, so far as I could see, they did not even try to do so﻿—their heart was turned to fat, as the Zulus say.

“Then having rested a few moments we took up the loads, including the tusks of ivory that had cost us so dear, and in silence marched on for a couple of miles or more, till we reached a patch of dense bush. And here, being utterly exhausted, we camped for the night, taking the precaution, however, of setting a guard to watch against any attempt at surprise.”

===Chinese===

又过了半分钟，我和迈瓦妇人已立在崖顶，喘着气，却安全了。

“经迈瓦指点，我的人极其凑巧，早把散在近旁的几块大圆石滚拢了来；靠了这些，我们很快便把穿过那道倒悬岩脊的小径堵了个严实，底下的士兵断然爬不上来了。的确，据我所见，他们连试也不曾试——照祖鲁人的说法，他们的心变成油了。”

“歇了几分钟，我们便背起驮子——连那付出了如此代价才保住的象牙——默默行了两英里多路，来到一片密林跟前。到了这里，人人筋疲力尽，便扎营过夜；只是我们不敢大意，设了岗哨，防人夜袭。”

## VI The Plan of Campaign / 六 作战方略

===Original===

“Notwithstanding all that we had gone through, perhaps indeed on account of it, for I was thoroughly worn out, I slept that night as soundly as poor Gobo, round whose crushed body the hyenas would now be prowling. Rising refreshed at dawn we went on our way towards Nala’s kraal, which we reached at nightfall. It is built on open ground after the Zulu fashion, in a ring fence and with beehive huts. The cattle kraal is behind and a little to the left. Indeed, both from their habits and their talk it was easy to see that these Butiana belong to that section of the Bantu people which, since T’Chaka’s time, has been known as the Zulu race. We did not see the chief Nala that night. His daughter Maiwa went on to his private huts as soon as we arrived, and very shortly afterwards one of his head men came to us bringing a sheep and some mealies and milk with him. ‘The chief sent us greeting,’ he said, ‘and would see us on the morrow.’ Meanwhile he was ordered to bring us to a place of resting, where we and our goods should be safe and undisturbed. Accordingly he led the way to some very good huts just outside Nala’s private enclosure, and here we slept comfortably.

“On the morrow about eight o’clock the head man came again, and said that Nala requested that I would visit him. I followed him into the private enclosure and was introduced to the chief, a fine-looking man of about fifty, with very delicately-shaped hands and feet, and a rather nervous mouth. The chief was seated on a tanned ox-hide outside his hut. By his side stood his daughter Maiwa, and squatted on their haunches round him were some twenty head men or *Indunas*, whose number was continually added to by fresh arrivals. These men saluted me as I entered, and the chief rose and took my hand, ordering a stool to be brought for me to sit on. When this was done, with much eloquence and native courtesy he thanked me for protecting his daughter in the painful and dangerous circumstances in which she found herself placed, and also complimented me very highly upon what he was pleased to call the bravery with which I had defended the pass in the rocks. I answered in appropriate terms, saying that it was to Maiwa herself that thanks were due, for had it not been for her warning and knowledge of the country we should not have been here today; while as to the defence of the pass, I was fighting for my life, and that put heart into me.

“These courtesies concluded, Nala called upon his daughter Maiwa to tell her tale to the head men, and this she did most simply and effectively. She reminded them that she had gone as an unwilling bride to Wambe﻿—that no cattle had been paid for her, because Wambe had threatened war if she was not sent as a free gift. Since she had entered the kraal of Wambe her days had been days of heaviness and her nights nights of weeping. She had been beaten, she had been neglected and made to do the work of a lowborn wife﻿—she, a chief’s daughter. She had borne a child, and this was the story of the child. Then amidst a dead silence she told them the awful tale which she had already narrated to me. When she had finished, her hearers gave a loud ejaculation. ‘*Ou!*’ they said, ‘*ou!* Maiwa, daughter of Nala!’

===Chinese===

“尽管经历了那一切——或许也正因为经历了那一切，因为我实在已是筋疲力尽——那天夜里我睡得同可怜的戈博一样死；而此刻，鬣狗们想必正围着他那具摔烂的尸身逡巡。天一亮，我们便歇足了精神，起身赶路，奔纳拉的克拉尔去，日落时分到了那里。克拉尔照祖鲁人的样式建在开阔地上，围着一圈环形的栅栏，里面是一座座蜂窝似的小屋。牛栏在后面，偏左一点儿。的确，无论从习俗还是从言谈里，都很容易看出，这些布蒂亚纳人属于班图各族中自恰卡（T'Chaka）时代以来便以祖鲁族闻名的那一支。那晚我们没有见到纳拉首领。他的女儿迈瓦一到，便径直去了他自住的屋群；不多时，他手下一名头人便来见我们，带来一只羊、一些玉米，还有奶。‘首领向诸位问好，’他说，‘愿明日相见。’同时他奉命把我们领到一处歇宿的地方，让我们人和财物都安全无扰。于是他把路引到紧挨着纳拉私宅围篱的几间很好的屋子里，我们就在那儿舒舒服服睡了一宵。

“次日八点钟左右，那头人又来了，说纳拉请我去见他。我随他走进那圈私宅，被引见给首领本人。他是个仪表堂堂的男人，五十岁上下，手和脚的形状都出奇地秀气，一张嘴却带着神经质的模样。首领坐在屋外一张硝过的牛皮上，女儿迈瓦立在他身旁，四下里蹲着二十来名头人，也就是因杜纳（Induna），而且还不时有新到的加入这一行列。我进去时，这些人纷纷向我致意；首领起身同我握手，吩咐搬来一张凳子给我坐。这一切安排停当，他便满口辞令、极尽土人的礼数，感谢我在他女儿身陷险境之时出手卫护，又把我据守石隘的功劳——照他乐意的说法，是我那份‘英勇’——着实夸奖了一番。我也以得体的话作答，说真正该道谢的是迈瓦自己，因为若不是她预先示警、又熟识这一带的地理，我们今天断不能站在这里；至于守卫隘口，我那是为保性命而战，胆气自然就来了。

“客套既毕，纳拉便叫女儿迈瓦把她的遭遇对众头人讲一遍，她讲得极为简洁，也极有力。她提醒众人，当初她是作为一个不情愿的新娘被送到万贝那里去的——聘礼一头牛也没有，因为万贝放出话来，若不把她白白送过去，便要刀兵相见。自进了万贝的克拉尔，她的白日是愁苦的白日，黑夜是啼哭的黑夜。她挨过打，受过冷落，被支使去干下贱老婆才干的活计——她，一个首领的女儿。她生过一个孩子，这便是那孩子的来历。于是，在一片死寂之中，她把那段已经对我讲过的骇人往事，向他们讲了出来。她讲完之后，听的人齐声发出一声惊叹。‘噢（Ou）！’他们说，‘噢！迈瓦，纳拉的女儿！’

===Original===

“ ‘Ay,’ she went on with flashing eyes, ‘ay, it is true; my mouth is as full of truth as a flower of honey, and for tears my eyes are like the dew upon the grass at dawn. It is true I saw the child die﻿—here is the proof of it, councillors,’ and she drew forth the little dead hand and held it before them.

“ ‘*Ou!*’ they said again, ‘*ou!* it is the dead hand!’

“ ‘Yes,’ she continued, ‘it is the dead hand of my dead child, and I bear it with me that I may never forget, never for one short hour, that I live that I may see Wambe die, and be avenged. Will you bear it, my father, that your daughter and your daughter’s child should be so treated by a Matuku? Will ye bear it, men of my own people?’

===Chinese===

“‘是啊，’她双目灼灼，接着说道，‘是啊，这是真的；我的口中盛满真话，如同一朵花盛满蜜；我的眼中盛满泪水，如同黎明的草上盛满露水。我确实亲眼看着那孩子死去——喏，凭证在此，各位参议，’说着，她掏出那只小小的死人的手，举到众人眼前。

“‘噢！’他们又叫起来，‘噢！是那只死人的手！’

“‘正是，’她继续道，‘这是我死去的孩子的死手。我把它带在身边，为的是永不忘记，连短短一个时辰也不忘记：我活着，为的就是亲眼看着万贝死，血债血偿。我的父亲，你能容忍你的女儿、你女儿的孩子，被一个马图库人这般作践吗？我的族人，你们能容忍吗？’

===Original===

“ ‘No,’ said an old *Induna*, rising, ‘it is not to be borne. Enough have we suffered at the hands of these Matuku dogs and their loud-tongued chief; let us put it to the issue.’

“ ‘It is not to be borne indeed,’ said Nala; ‘but how can we make head against so great a people?’

“ ‘Ask of him﻿—ask of Macumazahn, the wise white man,’ said Maiwa, pointing at me.

===Chinese===

“‘不能，’一位年老的因杜纳起身说道，‘断断容不得。这些马图库恶狗和它们那大嗓门的首领，我们受够了；干脆同他们决一胜负吧。’

“‘确实断断容不得，’纳拉说，‘可是，对方族大势众，我们如何抵挡得住？’

“‘问他——问马库马赞，那位聪明的白人，’迈瓦说着，把手朝我一指。

===Original===

“ ‘How can we overcome Wambe, Macumazahn the hunter?’

“ ‘How does the jackal overreach the lion, Nala?’

“ ‘By cleverness, Macumazahn.’

“ ‘So shall you overcome Wambe, Nala.’

“At this moment an interruption occurred. A man entered and said that messengers had arrived from Wambe.

===Chinese===

“‘我们怎样才能战胜万贝，猎人马库马赞？’

“‘豺狼是怎样算计狮子的，纳拉？’

“‘靠的智谋，马库马赞。’

“‘你也这样去战胜万贝，纳拉。’

“正说到这里，来了个岔子。有个人进来禀报：万贝那边派使者来了。

===Original===

“ ‘What is their message?’ asked Nala.

“ ‘They come to ask that thy daughter Maiwa be sent back, and with her the white hunter.’

“ ‘How shall I make answer to this, Macumazahn?’ said Nala, when the man had withdrawn.

“ ‘Thus shalt thou answer,’ I said after reflection; ‘say that the woman shall be sent and I with her, and then bid the messengers be gone. Stay, I will hide myself here in the hut that the men may not see me,’ and I did.

“Shortly afterwards, through a crack in the hut, I saw the messengers arrive, and they were great truculent-looking fellows. There were four of them, and evidently they had travelled night and day. They entered with a swagger and squatted down before Nala.

===Chinese===

“‘他们带来什么话？’纳拉问。

“‘他们来讨还您的女儿迈瓦，还要连那白人猎手一并交出去。’

“‘这话我该怎样回复，马库马赞？’来人退下之后，纳拉问我。

“‘你就这样回复，’我略一思忖，说道，‘就说那女人一定送到，我也随她同去，然后打发使者们上路。且慢，我先躲进这屋里，别叫他们瞧见我。’于是我照办了。

“不多时，我从屋壁的一道缝里望见使者们到了。那是几个面目凶横的大汉，一共四个，显见得是昼夜兼程赶来的。他们大摇大摆走进来，在纳拉面前蹲成一排。

===Original===

“ ‘Your business?’ said Nala, frowning.

“ ‘We come from Wambe, bearing the orders of Wambe to Nala his servant,’ answered the spokesman of the party.

“ ‘Speak,’ said Nala, with a curious twitch of his nervous-looking mouth.

“ ‘These are the words of Wambe: “Send back the woman, my wife, who has run away from my kraal, and send with her the white man who has dared to hunt in my country without my leave, and to slay my soldiers.” These are the words of Wambe.’

“ ‘And if I say I will not send them?’ asked Nala.

===Chinese===

“‘有何贵干？’纳拉皱着眉问。

“‘我们自万贝处来，向他的仆人纳拉传达万贝的命令。’为首的发言人答道。

“‘讲。’纳拉说，那张神经质的嘴奇怪地抽动了一下。

“‘这是万贝的话：“把从我克拉尔里逃走的那个女人、我的妻子送回来；再叫那个胆敢不经我许可、到我地界里打猎、杀害我士兵的白人一并发来。”这是万贝的话。’

“‘要是我说个不字呢？’纳拉问。

===Original===

“ ‘Then on behalf of Wambe we declare war upon you. Wambe will eat you up. He will wipe you out; your kraals shall be stamped flat﻿—so,’ and with an expressive gesture he drew his hand across his mouth to show how complete would be the annihilation of that chief who dared to defy Wambe.

“ ‘These are heavy words,’ said Nala. ‘Let me take counsel before I answer.’

“Then followed a little piece of acting that was really very creditable to the untutored savage mind. The heralds withdrew, but not out of sight, and Nala went through the show of earnestly consulting his *Indunas*. The girl Maiwa too flung herself at his feet, and appeared to weep and implore his protection, while he wrung his hands as though in doubt and tribulation of mind. At length he summoned the messengers to draw near, and addressed them, while Maiwa sobbed very realistically at his side.

“ ‘Wambe is a great chief,’ said Nala, ‘and this woman is his wife, whom he has a right to claim. She must return to him, but her feet are sore with walking, she cannot come now. In eight days from this day she shall be delivered at the kraal of Wambe; I will send her with a party of my men. As for the white hunter and his men, I have nought to do with them, and cannot answer for their misdeeds. They have wandered hither unbidden by me, and I will deliver them back whence they came, that Wambe may judge them according to his law; they shall be sent with the girl. For you, go your ways. Food shall be given you without the kraal, and a present for Wambe in atonement of the ill-doing of my daughter. I have spoken.’

“At first the heralds seemed inclined to insist upon Maiwa’s accompanying them then and there, but on being shown the swollen condition of her feet, ultimately they gave up the point and departed.

===Chinese===

“‘那我们便代表万贝向你宣战。万贝要把你们生吞活吃。他要把你们斩尽杀绝；你们的克拉尔将被踏成平地——这样，’说着他做了个生动手势，用手在嘴上一抹，示意那个胆敢违抗万贝的首领，将被打得何等干净彻底。

“‘这话可够重的，’纳拉说，‘容我计议一下，再作答复。’

“接下来便上演了一小出戏，就一颗未经教化的野蛮头脑而言，这出戏实在演得可圈可点。使者们退了下去，却只退到望得见的地方；纳拉则一本正经地同他的因杜纳们商议起来。迈瓦姑娘也扑倒在他脚下，作哭泣哀求庇护之状；他便直搓双手，仿佛疑惧交加、心乱如麻。末了他召使者们上前，当面作答，迈瓦便在他身旁极其逼真地抽抽噎噎。

“‘万贝是位大首领，’纳拉说，‘这女人是他的妻子，他有权索还。她必须回他那里去，只是她的双脚走得溃烂了，眼下动身不得。自今日起第八天，她必被送到万贝的克拉尔；我将派一队自己的人押送。至于那白人猎手和他的人，我与他们并无干系，他们的罪过也担保不得。他们不请自来，闯到我这里，我自会从哪里来的送回哪里去，凭万贝按他自己的法度发落；他们将随那女子一同送去。至于你们几位，请自便吧。寨外将为你们备下吃食，另有一份献给万贝的礼物，以抵偿我女儿的过失。我的话说完了。’

“起初，使者们似乎执意要迈瓦当时当地随他们动身，但等见到她那双脚肿得不成样子，终于让了步，起身回去了。

===Original===

“When they were well out of the way I emerged from the hut, and we went on to discuss the situation and make our plans. First of all, as I was careful to explain to Nala, I was not going to give him my experience and services for nothing. I heard that Wambe had a stockade round his kraal made of elephant tusks. These tusks, in the event of our succeeding in the enterprise, I should claim as my perquisite, with the proviso that Nala should furnish me with men to carry them down to the coast.

“To this modest request Nala and the head men gave an unqualified and hearty assent, the more hearty perhaps because they never expected to get the ivory.

“The next thing I stipulated was, that if we conquered, the white man John Every should be handed over to me, together with any goods which he might claim. His cruel captivity was, I need hardly say, the only reason that induced me to join in so hair-brained an expedition, but I was careful from motives of policy to keep this fact in the background. Nala accepted this condition. My third stipulation was that no women or children should be killed. This being also agreed to, we went on to consider ways and means. Wambe, it appeared, was a very powerful petty chief, that is, he could put at least six thousand fighting men into the field, and always had from three to four thousand collected about his kraal, which was supposed to be impregnable. Nala, on the contrary, at such short notice could not collect more than from twelve to thirteen hundred men, though, being of the Zulu stock, they were of much better stuff for fighting purposes than Wambe’s Matukus.

“These odds, though large, under the circumstances were not overwhelming. The real obstacle to our chance of success was the difficulty of delivering a crushing assault against Wambe’s strong place. This was, it appeared, fortified all round with *schanses* or stone walls, and contained numerous caves and koppies in the hillside and at the foot of the mountain which no force had ever been able to capture. It is said that in the time of the Zulu monarch Dingaan, a great impi of that king’s having penetrated to this district, had delivered an assault upon the kraal then owned by a forefather of Wambe’s, and been beaten back with the loss of more than a thousand men.

“Having thought the question over, I interrogated Maiwa closely as to the fortifications and the topographical peculiarities of the spot, and not without results. I discovered that the kraal was indeed impregnable to a front attack, but that it was very slightly defended to the rear, which ran up a slope of the mountain, indeed only by two lines of stone walls. The reason of this was that the mountain is quite impassable except by one secret path supposed to be known only to the chief and his councillors, and this being so, it had not been considered necessary to fortify it.

===Chinese===

“等他们走得不见踪影，我才从屋里出来，大家一起商量局势、订立方略。头一件，我仔细向纳拉言明：要我白白贡献经验和劳力，那可不成。我听说万贝的克拉尔外围有一道栅栏，是拿象牙垒的。此番大事若能成功，这些象牙便要归我，算作我的外快，条件是纳拉得拨人手，把它们扛到海边去。

“对这一谦逊的要求，纳拉和众头人满口应承，毫无异议；之所以应承得如此爽快，多半因为压根没指望象牙真能到手。

“我提的第二条是：倘若我们得胜，须把白人约翰·埃弗里交到我手里，连他名下的一切财物一并交割。不必说，他惨遭囚禁，才是我肯卷入这桩异想天开的险事的唯一缘由，但出于策略，我小心把这一条藏在幕后。纳拉应允了。我的第三条是：妇孺不得杀害。这条也谈妥之后，我们便着手研究方式与手段。原来万贝是个势力颇强的小酋长，也就是说，他至少能拉出六千战士上阵，而且常年有三四千人聚在自己克拉尔左近，那克拉尔向来号称固若金汤。纳拉这边呢，仓促之间至多纠集一千二三百人，不过他们是祖鲁血统，真要论打仗的成色，比万贝的马图库人强得多。

“这等悬殊虽然可观，在眼下的局面里倒还算不得灭顶之灾。我们成功机会的真正障碍，是难以对万贝那处坚固的巢穴发动一场毁灭性的强攻。原来那地方四围都筑着散斯（schans），也就是石砌的胸墙；山坡上和山脚下还有数不清的岩穴和石丘（koppie），从来不曾有一支兵力把它们攻克。据说在祖鲁王丁干（Dingaan）的时代，那位国王的一支劲旅开进这一带，向当时属于万贝先人的一座克拉尔发动强攻，结果被击退，折损了一千多人。

“我把这问题反复掂量之后，便细细盘问迈瓦那地方的工事和地形细故，问下来收获不小。我发现那克拉尔若从正面攻打，确实固若金汤，背后却几乎不设防——那一面顺着山坡向上延展，实际只凭两道石墙。缘故在于：那座山除一条秘密小径外，根本无路可通，而这小径，据信只有首领和他的参议们知晓；既然如此，大家便觉得不必设防了。

===Original===

“ ‘Well,’ I said, when she had done, ‘and now as to this secret path of thine﻿—knowest thou aught of it?’

“ ‘Ay,’ she answered, ‘I am no fool, Macumazahn. Knowledge learned is power earned. I won the secret of that path.’

“ ‘And canst thou guide an impi thereon so that it shall fall upon the town from behind?’

“ ‘Yes, I can do this, if only Wambe’s people know not that the impi comes, for if they know, then they can block the way.’

“ ‘So then here is my plan. Listen, Nala, and say if it be good, or if thou hast a better, show it forth. Let messengers go out and summon all thy impi, that it be gathered here on the third day from now. This being done, let the impi, led by Maiwa, march on the morrow of the fourth day, and crossing the mountains let it travel along on the other side of the mountains till it come to the place on the further side of which is the kraal of Wambe; that shall be some three days’ journey in all.4 Then on the night of the third day’s journey, let Maiwa lead the impi in silence up the secret path, so that it comes to the crest of the mountain that is above the strong place, and here let it hide among the rocks.

===Chinese===

“‘好，’她讲完之后我开了口，‘那么说说你那条秘密小径——你对它知道多少？’

“‘知道，’她答道，‘我又不是傻子，马库马赞。求得学问，便得权柄。那条小径的秘密，是我自己赢来的。’

“‘那么你能引一支战队走上去，叫它从背后直捣那座城镇么？’

“‘能，这事我办得到，只要万贝的人不知道战队要来；他们若知道了，便能堵死那条路。’

“‘那么，我的计策是这样。听着，纳拉，看看它好不好；你若有更好的，只管拿出来。派出使者，召集你全部的战队，叫他们于自今日起的第三天，集结于此。此举既毕，第四天一早，叫战队由迈瓦引路开拔，翻过群山，沿着山的另一面前行，直抵万贝克拉尔所在的那一面；全部路程，约计三天。4 而后在第三天旅程的当夜，叫迈瓦引着战队，蹑足循秘密小径上山，上到坚垒背后那道山顶的脊线，就地在岩石间潜伏下来。

===Original===

“ ‘Meanwhile on the sixth day from now let one of thy *Indunas*, Nala, bring with him two hundred men that have guns, and lead me and my men as prisoners, and take also a girl from among the Butiana people, who by form and face is like unto Maiwa, and bind her hands, and pass by the road on which we came and through the cutting in the cliff on to the kraal of Wambe. But the men shall take no shields or plumes with them, only their guns and one short spear, and when they meet the people of Wambe they shall say that they come to give up the woman and the white man and his party to Wambe, and to make atonement to Wambe. So shall they pass in peace. And travelling thus, on the evening of the seventh day we shall come to the gates of the place of Wambe, and nigh the gates there is, so says Maiwa, a koppie very strong and full of rocks and caves, but having no soldiers on it except in time of war, or at the worst but a few such as can easily be overpowered.

“ ‘This being done, at the dawn of day the impi on the mountain behind the town must light a fire and put wet grass on it, so that the smoke goes up. Then at the sight of the smoke we in the koppie will begin to shoot into the town of Wambe, and all the soldiers will run to kill us. But we will hold our own, and while we fight the impi shall charge down the mountain side and climb the *schanses*, and put those who defend them to the assegai, and then falling upon the town shall surprise it, and drive the soldiers of Wambe as a wind blows the dead husks of corn. This is my plan. I have spoken.’

“ ‘*Ou!*’ said Nala, ‘it is good, it is very good. The white man is cleverer than a jackal. Yes, so shall it be; and may the snake of the Butiana people stand up upon its tail and prosper the war, for so shall we be rid of Wambe and the tyrannies of Wambe.’

“After that the girl Maiwa stood up, and once more producing the dreadful little dried hand, made her father and several of his head councillors swear by it and upon it that they would carry out the war of vengeance to the bitter end. It was a very curious sight to see. And by the way, the fight that ensued was thereafter known among the tribes of that district as the War of the Little Hand.

===Chinese===

“‘与此同时，自今日起第六天，纳拉，叫你的一名因杜纳带上二百名持枪的人，把我同我的人当作囚犯押解着，再从布蒂亚纳人中挑一个姑娘，要身形面目都与迈瓦相仿，把她的双手捆住，沿我们来时的那条路，穿过崖上的夹缝隘口，直奔万贝的克拉尔。但这二百人不得携带盾牌翎羽，只带枪和一支短矛；他们遇见万贝的人时，便说自己是来向万贝交还那女人和那白人及其一行的，特来向万贝请罪。这样他们便可安然通行。如此一路行走，第七天傍晚，我们将抵达万贝那座寨子的门前；据迈瓦说，离寨门不远有一座石丘，岩石累累，岩穴处处，形势险固，只是平素不驻一兵，除非战时；纵或有，也只是寥寥几个，轻易便能收拾。

“‘这步既成，天一亮，山上的战队便在镇子背后放起一堆火，火上压湿草，让烟升起来。我们望见烟号，便从石丘上朝万贝的镇里开火，众兵必然都跑来杀我们。但我们自有办法撑住；趁我们厮杀，战队便从山腰冲下，攀上散斯，把守垒的人一个个戳死在矛下，随即扑进镇里，杀他个措手不及，把万贝的兵像风卷谷壳一般驱散。这便是我的计策。我的话说完了。’

“‘噢！’纳拉说，‘好计，好得很。这白人比豺狼还要精明。就这么办；愿布蒂亚纳人的神蛇翘起尾巴，保佑此战得胜，如此我们便摆脱了万贝，摆脱了万贝的暴政。’

“随后迈瓦姑娘站起身来，又取出那只可怕的小小的枯手，叫她的父亲和几位主要参议对它起誓、凭它起誓：这场复仇之战，必打到最后一刻才罢休。那场面委实奇特。顺带一说，后来打起来的那一仗，在当地各族口中，便得了个‘小手之战’的名字。

===Original===

“The next two days were busy ones for us. Messengers were sent out, and every available man of the Butiana tribe was ordered up to ‘a great dance.’ The country was small, and by the evening of the second day, some twelve hundred and fifty men were assembled with their assegais and shields, and a fine hardy troop they were. At dawn of the following day, the fourth from the departure of the heralds, the main impi, having been doctored in the usual fashion, started under the command of Nala himself, who, knowing that his life and chieftainship hung upon the issue of the struggle, wisely determined to be present to direct it. With them went Maiwa, who was to guide them up the secret path. Of course we were obliged to give them two days’ start, as they had more than a hundred miles of rough country to pass, including the crossing of the great mountain range which ran north and south, for it was necessary that the impi should make a wide detour in order to escape detection.

“At length, however, at dawn on the sixth day, I took the road, accompanied by my most unwilling bearers, who did not at all like the idea of thus putting their heads into the lion’s mouth. Indeed, it was only the fear of Nala’s spears, together with a vague confidence in myself, that induced them to accept the adventure. With me also were about two hundred Butianas, all armed with guns of various kinds, for many of these people had guns, though they were not very proficient in the use of them. But they carried no shields and wore no headdress or armlets; indeed, every warlike appearance was carefully avoided. With our party went also a sister of Maiwa’s, though by a different mother, who strongly resembled her in face and form, and whose mission it was to impersonate the runaway wife.

“That evening we camped upon the top of the cliff up which we had so barely escaped, and next morning at the first breaking of the light we rolled away the stones with which we had blocked the passage some days before, and descended to the hillside beneath. Here the bodies, or rather the skeletons of the men who had fallen before my rifle, still lay about. The Matuku soldiers had left their comrades to be buried by the vultures. I descended the gully into which poor Gobo had fallen, and searched for his body, but in vain, although I found the spot where he and the other man had struck, together with the bones of the latter, which I recognized by the waistcloth. Either some beast of prey had carried Gobo off, or the Matuku people had disposed of his remains, and also of my express rifle which he carried. At any rate, I never saw or heard any more of him.

“Once in Wambe’s country, we adopted a very circumspect method of proceeding. About fifty men marched ahead in loose order to guard against surprise, while as many more followed behind. The remaining hundred were gathered in a bunch between, and in the centre of these men I marched, together with the girl who was personating Maiwa, and all my bearers. We were disarmed, and some of my men were tied together to show that we were prisoners, while the girl had a blanket thrown over her head, and moved along with an air of great dejection. We headed straight for Wambe’s place, which was at a distance of about twenty-five miles from the mountain-pass.

===Chinese===

“此后两天，我们忙得不可开交。使者四出，布蒂亚纳部族凡拿得动家伙的男子，都被召来赴‘一场大舞会’。地方不大，到第二天晚上，约莫一千二百五十名战士已带着矛和盾集结完毕，确是一支精悍耐苦的队伍。第三天拂晓——也就是使者们动身后的第四天——主力战队照例施过战前的法事，便在纳拉亲自统率下开拔；他深知自己的性命和酋位都押在这一战上，便明智地决意亲临督战。迈瓦也随军同行，由她引路走那条秘密小径。当然，我们只得让他们先走两天，因为他们要穿行一百多英里的崎岖地面，包括翻越那条南北走向的大山脉——战队必须绕个大圈，才躲得开耳目。

“然而到第六天拂晓，我也动身了，随行的是我那一百个不情愿的挑夫，他们全然不喜欢这桩把脑袋往狮子口里送的主意。说实在的，若不是惧怕纳拉的矛，再加上对我将信将疑的一点信心，他们断不肯应承这趟冒险。同行的还有约二百名布蒂亚纳人，人人持枪，枪却五花八门——这些人里有枪的不少，只是枪法实在不敢恭维——但他们不带盾，头上臂上也不见羽饰臂环；总而言之，一切像要开仗的行头，都小心避开了。随队而行的，还有迈瓦的一个妹妹——同父异母的妹妹——眉目身段都酷似迈瓦，她的使命，便是假扮那个出逃的妻子。

“当晚，我们就在先前死里逃生爬上来的那道悬崖顶上宿了营；次日清晨天刚破晓，我们搬开几天前堵住隘口的石块，下到山坡上。那些死在我枪下的男人们的尸身——或者不如说骸骨——还四处躺着。马图库士兵丢下自家同伴，任凭兀鹰去收殓。我下到可怜的戈博坠落的那条沟壑里去寻他的尸首，却徒劳无功，虽然我找到了他同另一个人摔落的地方，还有那人的骸骨——凭那条缠腰布我认出了它。戈博不是叫什么猛兽拖了去，便是叫马图库人收拾了，连同他扛着的那支特快步枪一起。总而言之，此后我再没见过他，也没再听人提起。

“一入万贝的地界，我们便采取十分谨慎的行进之法。约五十人散开走在前头，以防突袭；另有五十来人远远缀在后面。其余一百人夹在当中，抱成一团，我和假扮迈瓦的姑娘，还有我全部的挑夫，就走在这团人当中。我们被解除了武装，我的人有几个还被互相拴在一起，以示囚犯身份；姑娘头上蒙一条毯子，一路作出垂头丧气的模样。我们直奔万贝的住地而去，那地方离山口约二十五英里。

===Original===

“When we had gone some five miles we met a party of about fifty of Wambe’s soldiers, who were evidently on the lookout for us. They stopped us, and their captain asked where we were going. The head man of our party answered that he was conveying Maiwa, Wambe’s runaway wife, together with the white hunter and his men, to be given up to Wambe in accordance with his command. The captain then wanted to know why we were so many, to which our spokesman replied that I and my men were very desperate fellows, and that it was feared that if we were sent with a smaller escort we should escape, and bring disgrace and the wrath of Wambe upon their tribe. Thereon this gentleman, the Matuku captain, began to amuse himself at my expense, and mock me, saying that Wambe would make me pay for the soldiers whom I had killed. He would put me into the ‘Thing that bites,’ in other words, the lion trap, and leave me there to die like a jackal caught by the leg. I made no answer to this, though my wrath was great, but pretended to look frightened. Indeed there was not much pretence about it, I was frightened. I could not conceal from myself that ours was a most hazardous enterprise, and that it was very possible that I might make acquaintance with that lion trap before I was many days older. However, it seemed quite impossible to desert poor Every in his misfortune, so I had to go on, and trust to Providence, as I have so often been obliged to do before and since.

“And now a fresh difficulty arose. Wambe’s soldiers insisted upon accompanying us, and what is more, did all they could to urge us forward, as they were naturally anxious to get to the chief’s place before evening. But we, on the other hand, had excellent reasons for not arriving till night was closing in, since we relied upon the gloom to cover our advance upon the koppie which commanded the town. Finally, they became so importunate that we were obliged to refuse flatly to move faster, alleging as a reason that the girl was tired. They did not accept this excuse in good part, and at one time I thought that we should have come to blows, for there is no love lost between Butianas and Matukus. At last, however, either from motives of policy, or because they were so evidently outnumbered, they gave in and suffered us to go our own pace. I earnestly wished that they would have added to the obligation by going theirs, but this they declined absolutely to do. On the contrary, they accompanied us every foot of the way, keeping up a running fire of allusions to the ‘Thing that bites’ that jarred upon my nerves and discomposed my temper.

“About half-past four in the afternoon we came to a neck or ridge of stony ground, whence we could see Wambe’s town plainly lying some six or seven miles away, and three thousand feet beneath us. The town is built in a valley, with the exception of Wambe’s own kraal, that is situated at the mouth of some caves upon the slope of the opposing mountains, over which I hoped to see our impi’s spears flashing in the morrow’s light. Even from where we stood, it was easy to see how strongly the place was fortified with *schanses* and stone walls, and how difficult of approach. Indeed, unless taken by surprise, it seemed to me quite impregnable to a force operating without cannon, and even cannon would not make much impression on rocks and stony koppies filled with caves.

===Chinese===

“走出约五英里，我们同一支五十来人的万贝士兵撞了个正着，他们显然正专程守望我们。他们拦下队伍，带队的队长问我们往哪里去。我们这队的头人答道，他正押着万贝出逃的妻子迈瓦，连同白人猎手及其随从，遵万贝之命送去交割。那队长又问，我们何以来了这么多人；我们的发言人答说，我同我的人都是亡命之徒，押送的人若少了，只怕我们脱逃，给他们的部族招来羞辱，还惹万贝震怒。于是这位马图库队长老爷便拿我寻起开心来，嘲弄我说，万贝定要我为杀掉的士兵偿命，要把我塞进‘咬人的东西’里——换句话说，那捕狮的机关——把我丢在那里，像条被夹住腿的豺狼一样死去。我强按怒火，一言不答，只装出惊惶的样子。其实也用不着怎么装，我是真惊惶。我心里明白，这是一桩凶险到极处的营生，很可能不出几天，我就要同那捕狮机关结缘了。然而，眼看可怜的埃弗里身陷囹圄而掉头不顾，终究办不到，我只好往前走，把一切托付给天意——从前此后，我不止一次不得不这么办。

“这时又生出一桩新的难处。万贝的士兵执意要一路作伴，非但如此，还千方百计催我们快走，因为他们自然巴望赶在天黑前抵达首领的住地。可我们这边，却有十足的理由，非要挨到夜色四合才到不可，因为我们全指望借着暮色掩护，去抢占那座俯瞰全镇的石丘。末了他们催逼得太紧，我们索性一口回绝，说再快也快不了，理由是姑娘乏了。他们听了解释老大不痛快，有一阵我真以为要动起手来，因为布蒂亚纳人同马图库人之间，本就没有半分情谊。不过到最后，或是出于权宜，或是因为明摆着人数悬殊，他们让了步，听凭我们按自己的脚程走。我诚心诚意巴望他们好事做到底，各走各的路，可这一层他们断然回绝。非但如此，他们一步不落地陪我们走完全程，嘴里还不住拿‘咬人的东西’打趣，一句连一句，搅得我神经发麻，心浮气躁。

“下午四点半前后，我们走上一道乱石累累的鞍部，或者说山脊，从那里可以清楚望见万贝的镇子，就在六七英里外、比我们低三千英尺的地方。镇子坐落在一道山谷里，唯独万贝自家的克拉尔例外——它建在对面山坡的一排岩穴口上，我正盼着明朝看见我们战队的矛尖在那山坡上放光呢。即便从我们站立之处，也看得出这地方叫散斯和石墙护卫得何等严实，何等难以近前。的确，除非出其不意，在我看来，一支没有炮的兵力休想攻克它；即便有炮，对着这满是岩穴的顽石和石丘，怕也讨不到多少便宜。

===Original===

“Then came the descent of the pass, and an arduous business it was, for the path﻿—if it may be called a path﻿—is almost entirely composed of huge water-worn boulders, from the one to the other of which we must jump like so many grasshoppers. It took us two hours to climb down, and, travelling through that burning sun, when at last we did reach the bottom, I for one was nearly played out. Shortly afterwards, just as it was growing dark, we came to the first line of fortifications, which consisted of a triple stone wall pierced by a gateway, so narrow that a man could hardly squeeze through it. We passed this without question, being accompanied by Wambe’s soldiers. Then came a belt of land three hundred paces or more in width, very rocky and broken, and having no huts upon it. Here in hollows in this belt the cattle were kraaled in case of danger. On the further side were more fortifications and another small gateway shaped like a V, and just beyond and through it I saw the koppie we had planned to seize looming up against the line of mountains behind.

“As we went I whispered my suggestions to our captain, with the result that at the second gateway he halted the cavalcade, and addressing the captain of Wambe’s soldiers, said that we would wait here till we received Wambe’s word to enter the town. The other man said that this was well, only he must hand over the prisoners to be taken up to the chief’s kraal, for Wambe, was ‘hungry to begin upon them,’ and his ‘heart desired to see the white man at rest before he closed his eyes in sleep,’ and as for his wife, ‘surely he would welcome her.’ Our leader replied that he could not do this thing, because his orders were to deliver the prisoners to Wambe at Wambe’s own kraal, and they might not be broken. How could he be responsible for the safety of the prisoners if he let them out of his hand? No, they would wait there till Wambe’s word was brought.

“To this, after some demur, the other man consented, and went away, remarking that he would soon be back. As he passed me he called out with a sneer, pointing as he did so to the fading red in the western sky﻿—‘Look your last upon the light, White Man, for the “Thing that bites” lives in the dark.’

“Next day it so happened that I shot this man, and, do you know, I think that he is about the only human being who has come to harm at my hands for whom I do not feel sincere sorrow and, in a degree, remorse.”

===Chinese===

“接下来便是下山的隘口，那可是件苦差事，因为那条路——倘若也算得路——几乎全由一块块水磨的巨砾铺成，我们只好像一群蚱蜢，从这块蹦到那块。我们足足花了两点钟才下到底；顶着那般的烈日，到得山脚时，我先已去了一大半条命。不多时，天将擦黑，我们来到第一道工事：那是一道三重的石墙，墙上开一个寨门，窄得一个人勉强挤得过。有万贝的士兵同行，我们毫不受盘问便过了。接着是一片三百步开外的地带，乱石嵯峨，坎坷不平，上面不盖一间屋子。遇有危险，牲口便圈进这片地带的坑洼里。再往前，又是几道工事，还有一道小小的、形如 V 字的寨门；透过门望出去，正是我们计划夺取的那座石丘，黑沉沉地立在背后一线大山的前面。

“一路走，我一路把主意低低授给我们那位队长；于是到了第二道寨门，他便叫队伍停下，对万贝士兵的队长说，我们要在此地候着，等万贝准许进镇的口令。对方说这样也好，只是必须把囚犯交出来，押上山去首领的克拉尔，因为万贝‘急着开饭’呢，他的‘心巴不得在阖眼睡觉之前，亲眼看看那白人安歇的模样’，至于他的妻子，‘他自然是要欢迎的’。我们的领队答说，这事他做不得主，因为他奉的将令，是把囚犯当面交到万贝自家的克拉尔，将令不可违；他若把囚犯放出自己手掌心，囚犯的安危叫谁担待？不行，他们须候在此地，直到万贝的口令传来。

“那人迟疑片刻，终于应承，走开去了，临走说去去便回。他打我身边经过时，指着西天将熄的红光，冷笑一声道——‘白人，好好看最后一眼天光吧，那咬人的东西，可是住在黑地里呢。’

“说来也巧，第二天我就把此人打死了。而且你们知道么，我想，凡在我手里遭了殃的人里面，唯独这一个，我既不觉得由衷难过，也谈不上有几分悔意。”
